# KB: API reference (REST + WebSocket)

Moved VERBATIM out of CLAUDE.md (tier-2 pass).

## API Reference

### REST Endpoints

#### The app page's CSP reports (lane app-page-csp, 2.369.331)
- `POST /api/csp-report` — where a browser posts what the app page's REPORT-ONLY policy (src/app-csp.js) would block. A door of src/auth.js: no cookie, no same-origin rule (a sandboxed frame posts `Origin: null`). The route judges its own: `Content-Type` application/csp-report, application/reports+json or application/json, else 415; ≤ 32 KB, else 413 (never buffered past it); 60 posts a minute per address that would ADD a line, else 429 (a repeat writes nothing and is never paced). → 204. A report → one line of data/csp-reports.jsonl per (directive, blocked, source, line), with no value; ONE For-you notice per new (directive, blocked) pair from the app page (a frame the page made: a line only). src/server/csp-reports.js.
- `GET /frame-host?kind=design|mail|preview|eml|docx[&n=…][&pictures=1][&base=<dir>]` (signed in) — THE FRAME HOST: one static host document, its kind's policy as a real header (src/server/frame-host.js); `kind=preview&base=<a local folder>` ⇒ 302 to `/frame-host/f/<token>/`. `GET /frame-host/f/<token>/<path>` — the preview's folder, a door of src/auth.js (no cookie; the token is the credential): a web file under the folder, never a dot name or a symlink out; 404 otherwise.
- `GET /api/csp-reports` (signed in) → `{policy: 'report-only', app: {reports, lines}, frames: {reports, lines}, sanitizer: {reports, lines}, noticed, refused, paced}` — reports since this boot, lines on disk; the Diagnostics report's chip. `sanitizer` (lane chrome-156-followups) = the lines of kind `sanitizer-parse`: a handler DOMPurify's inert parse set and dropped (Chrome 156), never a notice, never in `app`.
- **A hub that met the mermaid bug (lane csp-mermaid-sites r2–r3, .363)**: at boot the sink re-judges each line on disk an earlier version judged the app page's that a sanitizer of app-csp `PARSE_SCRIPTS` explains — the line is rewritten `kind: sanitizer-parse` + `rejudged` (no `noticed`), THEN the For-you notice it filed is closed: in `GET /api/user-todos` (and the `user-todos-updated` broadcast) that item leaves `open` — `status: 'done'`, `resolvedBy: 'system'` — and `GET /api/csp-reports` counts the line in `sanitizer.lines`, no longer `app.lines`. Once per boot, ONE log line; the For-you store not up yet ⇒ nothing changes until it answers (tried again each second and at each report).
- `GET /bundle.js?v=<stamp>` · `GET /mermaid.js?v=<stamp>` (app-csp `PARSE_SCRIPTS`, public/ through src/server/static-public.js; lane csp-mermaid-sites r2) — a `?v=` naming another build than the file on disk ⇒ 302 to `<script>?v=<its own stamp>`, `Cache-Control: no-store` (the stamp = the index route's: the file's mtime in whole ms). Its own stamp, no `?v=`, a method other than GET / HEAD, or any other file ⇒ served as before. A report from a tab opened before an upgrade then names the bytes that ran (Chrome 156 posts `source-file` with no query at all — the sink judges the file on disk).

#### Published pages (2.364.0)
- `GET /p/:id` — serve a published page. Middleware-exempt; module gates: private ⇒ cookie, public ⇒ open. Response always carries `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts …` (opaque origin — the XSS guard).
- `GET /api/pages` — list; `GET /api/pages/by-path?path=` — record for a source file (the explorer dialog's state probe).
- `POST /api/pages/publish` `{path, name?, public?}` — copy-in publish, upserts by source path (stable /p/<id>).
- `POST /api/pages/:id` `{public?, name?}` — flags; `DELETE /api/pages/:id` — unpublish.

- `GET /api/home` — home directory (+ authEnabled flag)
- `POST /api/login` / `POST /api/logout` — password auth (rate-limited; sets/revokes HttpOnly cookie token); `GET /login` login page — the cookie's token is stored as its sha256 only; 30-day sliding, 180-day cap; a vault lock signs every login out (lane vault-e2e-password-fix-r2, «Vault» below)
- **THE SAME-ORIGIN RULE (2.369.274, src/write-origin.js + src/auth.js):** every POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE and every WebSocket handshake that rides the owner's cookie (or an instance without sign-in) must come from VibeSpace's own page — else `403 {error: 'cross-site', origin: <the offered Origin or null>, own: <this request's own origin>}` (an upgrade: `401`), one `[auth] refused a cross-site …` log line. `Sec-Fetch-Site` decides (same-origin / none pass; same-site / cross-site refused), else `Origin` must equal ours; neither header (curl, the CLIs) passes; `Authorization: Bearer …` is never judged. The doors (/api/agent/, /hook, /dav, /svc, /p/, /otel/, telemetry ingest, the editor bridge) judge their own credential. Reads are never gated. The UI says 「这次操作来自别的站点，已拒绝」 for it (`fetchJson` / `api()`).
- `POST /api/auth/set-password` `{current?, newPassword? | remove?}` — behind the cookie gate (2.369.274); a configured password must be presented as `current`; an SSO-only instance needs a signed-in request (anonymous 401); with no auth configured it is open (first-run onboarding sets the first password).
- `GET /api/backend-status` — CLI readiness for onboarding: {claude,codex} × {installed, version, loggedIn} (file-existence detection, never spawns CLIs); + one row per harness whose descriptor answers `creds.machineStatus` (antigravity, lane antigravity-harness: {installed, cmdPath, version null, loggedIn — a STAT of ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/antigravity-oauth-token: true, or null = unknown (with a session bus agy keeps the machine login in the OS keyring), email null, loginMethod, namedLoggedIn}) — never a spawn, never a read of the login
- `GET /api/cli-registration` (lane cli-2-1-308-records) — the claude builds this server saw that the release has no record table for: `{newest, hub: {version, registered} | null, unregistered: [{version, sources: ['hub' | 'conversation'], firstSeen, draft: {file, takenAt, against, changes} | null}]}`. `POST /api/cli-registration/draft` — HUMAN-TRIGGERED, one at a time: the static dump of the hub's installed claude (never run) into data/claude-cli-dumps/<v>.json ⇒ `{ok, version, wrote, file, changes, against}`; 409 `{code: no-cli | registered}`, 500 `{code: failed, error}`.
- `GET /api/files?path=` — list directory
- `GET /api/file/info?path=` — file metadata (size, isBinary) without reading content
- `GET /api/file/content?path=` — read text file (max 10MB)
- `GET /api/file/raw?path=[&host=]` — serve raw file (every preview element: images, PDF iframe, video, audio, chat thumbs, jobs-panel pictures). **NAMED (lane raw-filename, 2026-09-28):** a successful answer (200 / 206 Range / HEAD) carries `Content-Disposition: inline; filename="<ASCII fallback>"; filename*=UTF-8''<percent-encoded name>` (src/file-disposition.js — fallback = printable ASCII without `"`/`\`, other characters `_`; control characters dropped from both forms; an empty basename ⇒ no header) so the browser's own save controls name the file, never `raw`. Always `inline` (an `attachment` would turn the PDF iframe preview into a download). A 404 / 403 / 503 carries no name (local: sendFile's `headers` option = the successful transfer only). Remote (`host=`): RemoteFs.downloadTo sets the same header just before the first byte (or at a successful empty end); a missing file answers 404 with no name on both transports (r2 — ssh used to answer an empty 200); a failed spawn 502. A dotfile (`.hidden.png`) is served and named (r2: `dotfiles: 'allow'` — send's legacy rule made it a 404; `/api/download` likewise).
- `GET /api/file/binary?path=&offset=&length=` — read binary chunk (max 1MB)
- `GET /api/file/excel?path=` — parse Excel → JSON
- `GET /api/file/docx?path=` — parse Word → HTML
- `GET /api/file/csv?path=&offset=&limit=&sep=` — streaming CSV/TSV row range (virtual scroll)
- `POST /api/format` — server-side code formatting (Python/Go/Rust/Shell via ruff/black/shfmt/gofmt/rustfmt)
- `GET /api/download?path=[&host=]` — download file: `attachment` (local: `res.download`, both forms; remote: RemoteFs.downloadTo, both forms via contentDisposition — r2: the raw name used to make setHeader throw on a CJK name and the request was never answered). Any synchronous throw from a remote stream helper answers 502 (files.js `remoteStream`). `GET /api/download-zip?host=` likewise (`<dir>.zip`, both forms)
- `POST /api/upload` — multipart upload (fileNames JSON field for Unicode filenames, preservePaths=1 for folder uploads with recursive mkdir)
- `GET /api/file/serve/*` — path-based raw file serving (was the HTML preview's `<base href>`; no client caller since lane frame-host-preview — the preview's folder is `/frame-host/f/<token>/`)
- `POST /api/file/write` — write file
- `POST /api/mkdir` — create directory
- `POST /api/rename` — rename
- `DELETE /api/file?path=` — delete
- `POST /api/archive` — create archive from same-parent paths[] (`.zip`/`.tar[.gz|.bz2|.xz]`, execFile zip/tar, 409 on existing dest unless overwrite)
- `GET /api/archive/list?path=` — archive contents (unzip -l / tar -tvf parse; 20k entry cap)
- `POST /api/archive/extract` — extract archive to dest (never overwrites unless overwrite:true — unzip -n / tar -k)
- `POST /api/archive/extract-entry` — extract ONE entry to a temp file (spawn streaming, 200MB cap) → client opens via normal viewer pipeline
- `POST /api/file/copy` / `POST /api/file/move` — recursive copy/move to FULL dest path (fs.cpSync; move falls back to copy+rm across devices; 409 on conflict unless overwrite). `progress:1` (2.215.0) → returns `{opId}` immediately and runs as a polled op: `GET /api/file/transfer-status?id=` {done,total(bytes),status,error,dest}, `DELETE` cancels (best-effort; local worker copies can't abort mid-flight). Cross-host relays count bytes INLINE on the tar/cat stream (du -sb pre-computes total, BSD -sk×1024 fallback); local/same-host branches poll du on dest. In op mode the 409-exists check surfaces as op error 'exists' — the client maps it back onto the confirm-overwrite flow. Paste always requests progress; the client row renders only if the op outlives 400ms (small pastes look unchanged)
- `GET /api/download-zip?path=` — stream folder as zip download (spawn `zip -r - `, no temp file)
- `GET /api/file/stat?path=&du=1` — extended properties (mode/uid/entryCount; optional bounded du -sb)
- `GET /api/dir-complete?path=` — directory autocomplete (500ms timeout, supports `~`) — a caller sending only `path` gets the old `{suggestions}` (≤ 20 absolute folders under the typed parent)
- `GET /api/dir-complete?path=&bases=&probe=&q=&kind=[&host=]` — THE PATH FIELD's answer (lane path-field): `bases` = a JSON array of `~/…` / absolute paths in order (each LISTED, and the typed parent against each), `probe` = a JSON array (≤ 40) of paths stated only (exists, git, fileCount, mtime), `q` = the fuzzy words (the folder index searched: segment prefix > subsequence > token, CJK plain, ≤ 60 hits), `kind` = `folder` | `file` (files listed with name / size / mtime). ⇒ `{ suggestions, listing: { home, machine, dirs: { '~/…': { kids, files?, git, fileCount, mtime, more, stub?, mount? } }, links: { '~/合同': '~/work/contracts' }, localized, mounts, nondirs }, self: { home, roots }, took, partial }` within 500 ms (`partial` when the machine was slower — the caches still fill); `self.roots` = the instance's own checkout, its data dir, a home folder whose package.json names vibespace (never offered unasked). `?host=` answers the same from that machine (the host rung)
- `GET /api/hosts/:id/dir-complete?path=[&bases=&probe=&q=&kind=]` — the host rung: `path` alone = the old `{suggestions}`; with any of the path field's params = the same slice computed ON that machine (one sh round trip over the device link, else ssh; its display names via mdls on a Mac)
- `GET /api/hosts/:id/recent-cwds` — `{ cwds: [≤ 8 paths], recent: [{ path, count, last }] }` — lane path-field: `recent` = how many conversations started in each folder there and the newest (ms), newest first, ≤ 20
- `GET /api/home-folders` — (lane path-field: the instance's own checkout, its data dir and dot-folders are never in it) this machine's home folders, each with a BOUNDED count of the files it holds (`{home, folders:[{name, path, files, more, mount}]}`; a mount point named, never walked; `?host=` ⇒ `{folders: [], unsupported: 'remote'}`; cached 10 s) — the New Session dialog's and the tour's folder chips (lane door-day-one-landing). A ws `create` may carry `purpose: 'sign-in'` (a sign-in terminal: `active-sessions` rows carry it, the sidebar lists no row); `GET /api/vault/state` / `vault-state` carry `secretWaiting` (not set up + a secret stored ⇒ the setup ask)
- `GET /api/fonts` — system monospace fonts via `fc-list` (cached, fallback for client-side detection)
- `POST /api/custom-grids` — add custom grid preset `{rows, cols}`
- `DELETE /api/custom-grids` — remove custom grid preset `{rows, cols}`
- `GET /api/sessions` — all sessions (lock-first discovery + tmux detection)
- `GET /api/active` — WebUI-managed (dtach) sessions only
- `GET /api/usage` — rate limit data (PASSIVE since 2.60.0 — read from data/usage-cache/ written by the vibespace-usage statusLine hook; NO background /api/oauth/usage poll unless the opt-in setting `accounts.activeUsagePolling` is on, default off + danger-confirm. A cheap LOCAL read, so the taskbar polls it every 8s). Also gates the /v1/models OAuth fetch behind the same toggle
- `GET /api/usage-stats?from=&to=&backend=&account=&host=&pivot=` — the Usage-window ledger aggregation (UsageHistory.scan()+aggregate; **since 2.369.167 perf ⑥ the handler AWAITS `scanSettled()` before aggregating — the walk yields every MiB, so the answer is always the SETTLED ledger, never a half-walked one; same for rid-info's `eventForRid`/`eventForMid`, now async. Response shapes unchanged**): totals + daily series + groups by day/model/account/billing/project/mode/hour/weekday/session/**origin**, deduped by requestId, account-attributed, Claude AND Codex (2.63.0). `pivot` = comma list of `dimA:dimB` 2-D crosses (≤8 since 2026-09-10 — a cap that silently dropped a panel's pivot left it rendering a hole; since B-f95d ② (2.369.274) the window asks for a session / project cross only once a panel that draws it is on screen, bounded crosses ride the load). **`only=pivots` (2.369.274)** → the answer is `{pivots, asOf?, partial?}` ALONE — never the totals / groups / series / pricing the window already holds (All time here: 5.1 MB not re-sent) → response `pivots['a:b']` = [{key, cells:{bKey: finalized-bucket}}] for split-series dashboard panels (2.99.3). **`origin` (2026-09-10) = which KIND of transcript the request came from** — `'main'` (the conversation itself) | `'subagent'` | `'workflow'` | `'unknown'` (a row whose transcript is gone, or a remote scanner too old to stamp it); the `session` dim's rows also carry `project` (the conversation's cwd) so the window's session table needs no second lookup. `account` = comma list of bucket keys (account ids / `__global__`) — the window's Account chips pass a linked pair `<subId>,__global__` when the CLI login IS that account; from/to also drive the Custom… date-range picker. `GET|POST /api/usage-stats/pricing` — read / PATCH-merge the per-model + per-account price table (data/usage-history/pricing.json v2; tier match = longest-key substring, editor lists all tiers). `GET /api/usage-stats/rid-info?rid=&mid=` — the msg-meta popup's per-message billing lookup (2.266.1): `usageHistory.eventForRid(rid)` (exact, or `h:<host>:` suffix) then `eventForMid(mid)` (2.284.1; live claude records carry only message.id) → `{found:false}` | `{found:true, acct, aname, atype, pool, poolName, host, hostName, be, model, effort}` — `be`/`model`/`effort` added 2026-09-06 for codex parity: codex passes rid = the ledger's synthetic `cx:<thread>:<cumulative total>` key and mid = the 0.153 `resp_…` response id (walker v3 bakes both); `be` lets the client name the global bucket's login per harness ('ChatGPT login' vs 'CLI login'); `model`/`effort` are CONSUMED by the popup as fallback rows when the record's own meta lacks them (chat-view `_showMsgMeta`, appended after the Billing row — never duplicated over the sync rows).
  - **`ingestPaused` (lane ledger-dedup-law F4, .363):** present only when a month of a machine's usage is HELD (r4: ONE row per machine × month — its other months are recorded) — `[{host, hostName, shard, month: 'YYYY-MM', size, ratio, why: 'size' | 'ratio' | 'ids', since, lastAt, held (the events held beside the file; null while a big hold is read), dropped (the oldest held events dropped past the bound — counted; r6: the size of a SET of those events, each once however often its device re-sends it; r7: never an event the drain already recorded — r8: `null` while its hold is not read yet (never 0: the notice keeps the count it said), and filtered against the month's index when the hold ends), `unchecked` (r8: some of the dropped rows were held without the month's index in memory — they may be in the ledger: «may already be recorded»), `gen` (r8: the hold's generation), holdMax (bytes, 64 MiB), holdError? (THIS month's hold could not be written — r5: never another month's), drain: null | {state: 'pending' | 'draining' | 'failed', code?, tries?, at?} (r5: the month is back in shape and stays HELD until its held events are recorded — a resume follows the drain; `failed` = ENOSPC …, tried again at the machine's every ingest and at a restart; `at` = the last failed try, ms; a month whose drain is under way is listed here while a batch's rows of it ride the answer's `held` with no `pausedMonths` entry — its file is in shape), exact, remedy: {state: 'running' | 'judging' | 'failed' | 'not-rewritten' | 'insufficient' | 'proven-over' | 'ids' | 'none', code?, error?, removed?, nextAt?, stopped?}}]` (its monthly shard over 2 GiB or ~2× its distinct rows; `remedy` = the dedup of the repeated rows — under way, or what happened and when it is tried again; round 2; round 3: `judging` = an index-judged pause after a restart, judged again by the index; `proven-over` / `ids` = removing rows cannot help, `stopped: true`, `nextAt: null`; `exact` = the index's count judged it). The Usage window says it under the totals (src/lib/usage-ledger-note.js `PAUSED_WORDS`, a row per state; r4: + `pausedHeld` when events are held, `pausedDropped` when some were dropped; r5: `pausedDraining` / `pausedDrainFailed` for a month waiting on its drain). Not on an `only=pivots` answer.
- `POST /api/usage-stats/harvest-hosts` `{host?, force?}` → `{hosts: {<id>: answer}}` | `{busy: true}` — each machine's harvest AND ingest (hosts.harvestUsage with `ledger`; lane ledger-dedup-law): the answer = `{added, appended, duplicates, held?, paused?, pausedMonths?, holdFailed?, ack, since: 'hub' | 'device' | 'unsupported'}` — the batch is split BY MONTH (r4): `appended` (= `added`) the events written to their months' files; `duplicates` the events the ledger or a month's hold already had (written zero bytes — r6: and re-sent events the hold had DROPPED that stay dropped, already counted). **THE DEDUP LAWS (r7, written down):** (c) **the first payload that LANDS wins** — a rid the month's file or its hold already has is a duplicate whatever its payload (a held row's correction is a duplicate; a dropped row's re-send lands only where the hold has room for it). The daemon CAN produce one: its walker emits a request's first transcript line and skips only a CONTIGUOUS repeat (usage-walker `lastRid`), so a request whose lines a transcript repeats with another request between them is emitted again with that line's usage — the ledger keeps the first, as its write-time dedup and the streaming dedup always have (the choice: the first copy, never a later «correction»). (d) **a rid is unique within (month, host)** — the hold's set and a shard's rid index are per month: the same raw request id with a November timestamp is a November row while October's set still counts it (no dedup across months), and two daemons of ONE host id (an old one and its successor, or one id on two machines) share that machine × month's hold and its set; `held` this batch's events of a month out of shape (or of a month whose earlier held events are still being recorded), HELD beside its file (`data/usage-history/_held/`, ≤ 64 MiB per machine × month; r6: the dropped events' ids beside it, `<month>~<host>.<gen>.dropped` — a re-sent dropped event is held again only where it fits without dropping another) — never refused; `pausedMonths` = one record per paused month of the batch (the `ingestPaused` row below: `month`, `held` = all held for it, `dropped` = the oldest held events dropped past the bound — counted, `remedy`, `drain`, `holdError?`), `paused` = the first of them; `holdFailed` = `[{month, shard, error}]` — a month whose hold could not be written (ENOSPC …; that month only, r5 — r7: the append cut back, the hold as it was on disk and in memory), or LISTED (`EIO` — a listing that fails is no empty disk: the month's rows wait, unacked, until a listing succeeds), or would rotate past its generation's segment numbers (`hold-seq-bound`, unreachable — refused loudly) — r8: the listing's failure is a STATE (retried on its own timer, 5 s → 60 s; ONE For-you notice `itemKey: hold-list`): only a month that NEEDS its hold (out of shape, paused) is refused by it — a month in shape and not paused is appended — and the answer carries **`retryAfter`** (seconds to the next listing); `hold-slow` (its hold's jobs ran past 30 s — they go on; the re-send is a duplicate) and `hold-busy` (8 jobs waiting) are unacked too. **r8: the answer is a PROMISE** when a month of the batch goes to its hold or waits for one being read back (the hold's I/O is async — server.js's push road and hosts.js's harvest await it); a batch with no held month answers at once, as before; **`ack`** (r5) = usage-history `ackOf(answer)`, THE verdict both device roads read (this harvest's hub-kept cursor and the live push's ack, server.js `hosts.onUsageEvents`): true when every event is appended, a duplicate or held — a paused month alone never withholds it — false when deferred or a month's hold could not be written (the hub's cursor stays, the device sends the batch again; what it already gave costs zero bytes); `since` = whose cursor the device walked from (the hub's copy, its own on the first hub-kept run, or its own because its daemon is older) | `{added: 0, appended: 0, duplicates: 0, deferred: 'ledger-index-loading' | 'ledger-hold-loading', ack: false}` (a big shard's rid index or a big hold still loading in steps — nothing appended, the device keeps the events) | `{added: 0, appended: 0, duplicates: 0}` (an empty batch — nothing to commit, no `ack`; `since` rides it when known) | `{added: 0, throttled: true}` | `{error}`. Write order: hold → ledger → cursor — crash-safe (a crash in between re-sends events that cost zero bytes), not fsync-durable (the project's appends never fsync). r9: the months that need no hold are appended before any wait; a month whose hold is being read back waits ≤ HOLD_ANSWER_MS (then `holdFailed: [{…, error: 'hold-slow'}]`; 8 batches already waiting ⇒ `hold-busy` at once); a month whose hold could not be READ (EACCES, EIO — or the `_held/` listing failed) answers `holdFailed: [{month, shard, error: <code>}]` and the answer carries `retryAfter` (seconds until that hold, or the listing, is read again — by its own timer; a re-send before then meets the same answer). r10: EVERY deferred month carries `retryAfter` — `hold-slow` the bound (30 s), `hold-busy` what is left of the first waiting batch's bound, `ledger-index-loading` 1 s (the whole-batch `deferred` answer too). r11: `hold-gen-max` — the month's generations are spent (a notice item names HOLD_GEN_MAX, 1e9): its next hold is refused by name, no `retryAfter` (the item's end ends it). `retryAfter` is a HINT no reader paces by yet: the device push re-sends at its next walk (a transcript change + 1.5 s), the hub's harvest at its own ≥ 60 s gate.
- `POST /api/usage/refresh` `{account: '__global__'|subId}` — **USER-INITIATED on-demand quota fetch (2.70.0)**: the ONLY source of model-scoped weekly buckets (Fable) since the statusline payload carries just 5h/7d. Human-gated equivalent of the CLI's own /usage command: NEVER on a timer, ≥60s/account server-side + honors the 429 backoff (endpoint 429s hard with retry-after up to 3600s). **Gated by `accounts.onDemandQuotaRefresh` (2.72.0, user-requested after the ban question): 'manual' (DEFAULT — ⟳ button only) / 'auto' (also once on popup open when scoped data >30min stale) / 'off' (route 403s, ⟳ hidden, hint says so); first ⟳ click shows a one-time explainer dialog (localStorage vibespace.quotaRefreshAck). Result persisted to data/usage-cache/<key>.json (`source:'on-demand'`, `scopedFetchedAt`) so it survives restarts and the statusline hook's preserve-merge. §ban-safety: do NOT wire this to any scheduler. Popup: ⟳ in the Claude section title, scoped bars show their own 'as of' age, empty-scoped shows a hint note. **B-855a c1 (2026-09-17): the LOCAL answer names its rung — `{success:true, via:'session'|'cli-panel', rung:'control'|'panel', identityVerified, why, sessionId?, skipped:[{sessionId, why}]}` (`skipped` = live sessions the control rung would not ask: OTel-divergent or inside a re-point's lag shadow); an identity REFUSAL of the panel answers `{error:'not recorded — <both identities>', rung:'panel', identityVerified:false, why}` and makes NO further vendor call.** **2.369.114: a live-session reading the window guard ARCHIVED is not a refresh — it is listed in `skipped` and the ladder continues to the isolated panel rung.** **pool-silent-members acceptance r3: HUMAN-GATED** — an agent's `vsst_`/`jbt_` Bearer ⇒ `403 {code:'human_only'}` before anything is read; a door's refusal BY NAME (`not refreshed — …` for a folder token or a locked vault, else `needs re-login … , loginNeeded:true`) answers before the lapsed sentence, which is said only when a panel ran; THE USAGE BEARER LAW — the ladder sends only the CLI's own login token, never a long-lived one (ours or planted): a folder holding only one ⇒ `{error:'not refreshed — the account\'s folder holds a long-lived token …', rung:'token'}`, 0 vendor requests.
- `POST /api/usage/repair-identity` — **B-855a c2 (2026-09-17), HUMAN-TRIGGERED: the standing identity repair by hand** (the same `repairSidecarsByApiPhase` every boot runs after the one-shot migrations): derives each roster account's API phase from its slot-verified rate-limit readings, re-stamps a contradicting established-window sidecar, archives + rebuilds a contradicting usage-cache snapshot, re-admits the last 24 h of archived own readings; cookie-authenticated, an agent's `vsst_`/`jbt_` Bearer ⇒ `403 {code:'agent-forbidden'}`; answers `{success, counts:{evidence, noEvidence, stamped, restamped, confirmed, kept, replaced, emptied, stripped, readmitted}, ms, identities:[{key, name, apiPhase, n, of, sidecar, sidecarWas, cache, readmitted}]}` and reloads the panel memory (idempotent; archive-never-destroy in data/archive/readings-foreign-usage-cache.ndjson). Since the final verifier pass the same repair also runs HOURLY on a timer (quiet unless it changed something), and `POST /api/usage/refresh`'s `why` on a phase refusal may add that the weekly window "may have MOVED" with the count of the account's own candidates that already share the panel's window; an answer on a SHARED phase is `identityVerified:false` with `why` naming who shares it.
- `GET /api/usage/probe-log?key=<accountId>&rung=panel|control&limit=200` — the raw /usage probe ring (2.369.109): the last N probes with the verbatim reply, the parse, the write verdict and the machine-wide oauthAccount around the spawn (`{records:[…], file}`; src/server/usage-probe-log.js; read-only, the file is data/usage-probe-log.ndjson + .1; since B-855a c1 a panel record also carries `configDir`, `configOrgBefore/After`, `identity`, `identityVerified`)
- `POST /api/telemetry` — client event ingest ({events:[...]}, ≤20/batch; dropped silently when telemetry.enabled=false); `GET /api/telemetry/summary?days=` — grouped errors + byName/byDay/byVersion for the ⚙ Diagnostics report (src/telemetry.js). FLEET (2.101.0): `POST /api/telemetry/ingest` = the central-collector receive endpoint — enabled ONLY when env `VIBESPACE_TELEMETRY_INGEST_TOKEN` is set (shared Bearer token = on-switch + gate, timing-safe compare; cookie-auth EXEMPT in auth.js since senders have no cookie); batches land in central-YYYY-MM.ndjson stamped with the sender's instance id (original ts/version preserved, clamped). `GET /api/telemetry/central-summary?days=` (cookie-authed) = per-instance totals + cross-instance grouped errors → the Diagnostics report's Fleet section. Sender side: telemetry.forwardUrl/forwardToken settings fall back to VIBESPACE_TELEMETRY_FORWARD_URL/_TOKEN env (helm-configurable fleet-wide; user setting wins); _forward sends Bearer. Helm values telemetry.{forwardUrl,forwardToken,ingestToken}
- `GET /api/ci-heavy` — the heavy release-gate markers for the ⚙ Diagnostics report (src/server/ops-routes.js; read-only, tiny local JSON, no git): `{runs:[{sha,result,startedAt,endedAt,ms,suites,absent,failed,flaky,reason,unlocked,scope,selected,lanes}], running:[…]}`. **2026-09-15**: `scope` is `'full'` or `'affected'` (the push-time tier runs only the suites the pushed range touches; `selected` = how many; a marker written before the field is a full run) and `lanes` = the parallel lane count the run used — a FULL green is still owed once per 24 h and `npm run ci:status` says which tier the next push launches.
- `GET /api/runtime` — the node this server runs against THE floor table (lane runtime-floor; src/server/ops-routes.js → src/server/runtime-boot.js; read-only, no network): `{node, execPath, base (the node this start began on when it switched, else null), own: {version, node} | null (data/runtime/current), running, verdict: {state: ok|below-floor|unknown-major|unknown, version, major, floor, wanted, advisory}, advisory: {id, date, url, cves} | null, check: null | {state: running|done, wanted, ok, code, version, error} (the boot pull's runtime check), table: {major: floor}}` — the ⚙ Diagnostics report's «Node runtime» section.
- `GET /api/boot-trace[?cpu=1]` — this boot's own loop clock, live until its window closes (src/server/boot-trace.js, B-1319; read-only): `{pid, startedAt, startAt, mainMs, listenAt, windowMs, closed, traced, maxHoldMs, eldMaxMs, holds: [{at, ms, name, sites?}], steps, spans, notes}` — each hold ≥ 100 ms with its step / span; `VIBESPACE_BOOT_TRACE=1` adds each hold's sync-call `sites` + `ops` / `opsDropped`. Lane ledger-dedup-law r12: `VIBESPACE_BOOT_TRACE=1` or `VIBESPACE_BOOT_TRACE_CPU=1` arms a CPU sampler (`cpu: {samples, dropped, intervalUs, running, error?}`); `?cpu=1` FOLDS its samples (once — the profiler stops, never restarted: a start costs ~150 ms of the loop), after which each hold's `sites` also names the repo frames its CPU stood in (`cpu <fn> (<file>:<line>)`); the window's close folds them too (data/boot-trace.json). r14: a hold is NAMED only by what fills it — `sites` lists a source (the calls, the CPU samples) only when its sites cover half the hold or 100 ms; a source below that rides the hold as `faint` (`holds: [{at, ms, name, sites?, faint?}]`). r15: a source's fill is its RAW time (unrounded, rounded once — never the sum of its sites' rounded ms); ⚙ Diagnostics lists `faint` sites greyed under the named ones.
- `GET /api/version[?fresh=1]` / `GET /api/changelog-diff?lang=en|zh|ja[&fresh=1]` — the ⚙ → Update VibeSpace… dialog (src/server/ops-routes.js): the canonical repo's master `package.json` (`{version, commit, latest}`) and its user changelog, fetched lazily on request (15-min cache, `fresh` = a 60-s floor), each file cached separately. **`lang` (2026-09-30)** picks `CHANGELOG.md` / `CHANGELOG.zh.md` / `CHANGELOG.ja.md` (anything else ⇒ `en`); the English file lists the versions and each entry comes from the language file when it has that `## <version>`, else in English: `{current, latest, lang, entries:[{version, head, body, lang}], atLatest}` — `entries` = every version newer than the running one (none ⇒ the running version's own entry, else the newest), each entry's `lang` = the language it is in. A language file the canonical lacks (404) ⇒ every entry in English. `VIBESPACE_CHANGELOG_FIXTURE_DIR` (TEST ONLY) reads the same file names from a local directory — a missing file answers as a 404 — so a gate never reaches the network.
- `GET /api/sysinfo` — container-aware system snapshot (cgroup v2→v1→host memory, workspace df, loadavg, top-8 RSS procs) for the System rail panel. **MEMORY IS THE WORKING SET, never raw `memory.current` (2.312.0, a fleet user's permanent false 100%): a cgroup's total COUNTS RECLAIMABLE PAGE CACHE, so any file-heavy pod (transcript reads, a dataset pass) pins it at the limit forever — 29GB of cache, 2.7GB of actual processes, `memory.events` showing 770 reclaims and ZERO oom_kills. A gauge that can never fall is worse than no gauge: the real leak it exists to catch cannot stand out. `used = memory.current − inactive_file` (the kubectl-top/eviction definition, verified byte-for-byte against `kubectl top` on the affected pod), with `raw`+`cache`+`anon` carried alongside so the panel NAMES the difference instead of contradicting other tools; bare hosts use MemAvailable, not freemem. The REMOTE probe had always been right (`MemTotal − MemAvailable`) — this was the local twin drifting, the exact class §CS-SEPARATION exists to prevent, and the reason any new "how much memory" reader must go through memInfo().** `?host=<id>` = the same shape from the machine — **ONE implementation since 2.314.0 (a missed CS twin the user caught)**: the daemon bundles src/sysinfo.js and serves a capability-gated `sysinfo` op (containerized machines report their cgroup limit; macOS = vm_stat working set via the same module); the ssh script in server.js is the FALLBACK RUNG for daemon-less hosts only (raw-host semantics: MemTotal=limit). Any new "how much memory" reader goes through the module, never a re-derivation. Test: scripts/test-sysinfo-op.mjs (real daemon, incl. the 2.300.0 reply-op routing rule — an op-less reply times out silently). History stays local-only (the sampler runs in this server). **2.226.3 lesson: the 2.223.1 commit shipped a PARTIAL state (draw fn without DOM builder + dispose calling a nonexistent helper) — charts invisible AND leaving the System panel threw, bricking rail switching; all three _panelDispose call sites now catch (rail-dispose-failed telemetry) and scripts/test-sys-panel.mjs guards the cycle; src/sysinfo.js also runs a 45s memory-pressure watch → WS `sysinfo-alert` broadcast at ≥80%/≥92% of the container limit (30min cooldown per level, instant on escalation; toast + rail badge client-side — 2.216.0, userL's 32Gi pod OOM kill took every dtach session with zero warning)
- `POST /api/port-forward/:id/path` / `DELETE …/path` — main-domain PATH MOUNT (2.358.0, owner request: services under the app's own domain instead of one random frp subdomain each): body {name} → the forward serves at `/svc/<name>/` BEHIND VibeSpace auth (registered after auth.middleware; the ws upgrade branch in server.js's single dispatcher checks requestAuthed like /ws). src/server/path-mounts.js proxies to the forward's server-loopback port resolved PER REQUEST (mounts survive restarts with zero re-establishment; works for remote machines through the tunnel): prefix strip + query preserved, Host rewritten, X-Forwarded-Prefix/-Proto/-Host set, absolute-path Location redirects rewritten under the mount, no-slash form 302s to the canonical `/svc/<name>/`, unknown mount = 404, mounted-but-down = honest 502, ws upgrades spliced raw. INHERENT LIMIT (UI hint says so): apps emitting absolute asset paths must support a base path (vite `base`, jupyter `base_url`, code-server natively) — bodies are deliberately NOT rewritten (that's the embedded-browser proxy's job). Names `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,40}`, unique across forwards (clash names the holder). **PER-MOUNT PUBLIC/PRIVATE (2.359.0, owner's sharing case): body {public:true} makes the mount reachable WITHOUT a VibeSpace login (shareable link); DEFAULT stays private — exposure is an explicit confirmed act. SECURITY MODEL: the /svc prefix is EXEMPT from auth.middleware (a blanket cookie gate would make public mounts impossible), so the per-mount `requestAuthed` checks inside path-mounts (HTTP handler AND ws upgrade) are the ONLY gate for private mounts — both sides pinned (private-no-cookie → 401 incl. ws, public-no-cookie → 200). UI: lock toggle on the mount's address row, going public requires a danger-confirm naming the full URL; 'public' chip when open.** Test: scripts/test-path-mounts.mjs (17, real http+ws round trips; in the ci gate).
- `POST /api/port-forward/:id/publish` also accepts body `{sub}` (2.358.0) — a user-CHOSEN FIXED subdomain for the frp relay (frps routes any name under subDomainHost; validated `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}`, persisted as publicSub so restarts keep it — same slot the broker's random name used; a taken name fails loudly at the relay). Only HTTP backends ride subdomains (https/tcp fall through to IP:port mode as before); the UI publish flow prompts for it via showInputDialog.
- `GET /api/sysinfo/procs` — FULL process table for the System panel's process manager (2.354.0, btop-like): `{procs:[{pid,ppid,user,pcpu,pmem,rss,state,etime,cmd}], total, sampled, serverPid?}` — capped by capProcs (top-by-RSS ∪ top-by-CPU, ≤350 transported; `total` is the uncapped count and the UI names the truncation). `pcpuNow` rides each row when live sampling is available (Linux /proc utime+stime delta between successive calls, keyed pid:starttime against pid recycling — the counter-capture class); `sampled:false` (ssh fallback rung, macOS) means CPU% is ps's lifetime average and the UI says so. `?host=<id>` = same shape via the ladder: capability-gated `proc-list` device op (the daemon runs the SAME src/sysinfo.js listProcs — successive panel polls give the daemon its delta baseline, so remote CPU% is live too) → ssh rung `ps axo <PS_COLUMNS>` parsed orchestrator-side by the shared parsePsProcs. The columns and their interpretation live ONLY in src/sysinfo.js. Test: scripts/test-sysinfo-op.mjs (real daemon, row-shape parity local vs op) + the restore-smoke route battery.
- `POST /api/sysinfo/signal` — signal a process from the panel: `{host?, pid, sig}` (sig enum-whitelisted TERM/KILL/INT/HUP/STOP/CONT, pid integer-validated >1 BEFORE any shell string is built). Local = process.kill with a self-guard (the server's own pid → error naming the Update/systemctl flow) + a 450ms settle then `gone` honesty flag; remote = ONE verdict shell script (`kill -SIG` → sleep → **`ps -p` existence probe** → OK-GONE/OK-ALIVE/EPERM/ESRCH) over dm.runCmd, ssh _hostShell as the fallback rung. **TWO REVIEW-PINNED INVARIANTS (2.354.0, adversarially confirmed + E2E'd on a real host): ① existence is probed with `ps -p`, NEVER `kill -0` — kill(2) with sig 0 performs the SAME permission check as a real signal, so with `kill -0` every remote permission-denied kill misreported as "no such process" while the process kept running (the local rung splits errno correctly — this was local/remote divergent honesty on the ONE kill path); ② rung choice happens at CONNECT time only — once a device link exists, a runCmd failure throws ("may or may not have landed — check and retry") instead of falling to ssh, because the signal may already be delivered and a blind re-send double-signals.** Errors are 400s with the verdict spelled out (permission denied / already gone) — the panel toasts them, incl. a null-response guard (fetchJson returns null on network failure; unguarded, STOP/CONT showed a false success toast). Pins: test-sysinfo-op (ps -p/kill -0 greps).
- `GET /api/vnc/status` / `POST /api/vnc/start` — in-container desktop (src/vnc.js; served by src/routes/desktop-apps.js since 2026-09-13, answers unchanged): status {available, running, port}; start = spawn-or-adopt. WS `/api/vnc` = the RFB↔TCP bridge (cookie-authed at upgrade). **WS UPGRADE INVARIANT (2.104.0, real bug)**: the main wss is `noServer` and ONE `server.on('upgrade')` dispatcher routes /ws, /proxy/, /api/vnc — NEVER construct `WebSocketServer({server, path})`: ws's own listener abortHandshake(400)s every non-matching upgrade (it silently killed /proxy/ site WebSockets for months and the VNC bridge on arrival) **SINCE 2026-09-13 the WS `/api/vnc` upgrade is served by THE ONE desktop bridge src/server/desktop-stream.js as stream id `desktop-singleton` (`upgradeId('/api/vnc')`), the same relay as `/api/desktop/<id>/stream` — server.js's `bridgeVncSocket` is gone.**
- **APPS THAT SURVIVE A REBUILT MACHINE (Layer 0, 2.369.200 — docs/design-app-persistence.zh.md §3.1; src/routes/apps.js over src/server/apps-engine.js; every failure `{error, code}`).** THE USER'S DOOR (cookie-only; an agent's `Bearer vsst_` / `jbt_` ⇒ 403 `agent_forbidden` on EVERY route here): `GET /api/apps?host=` → `{host, appsDir, manifest:{v, generation, sources, entries:[{id, kind:'apt'|'deb'|'uv-tool'|'npm'|'appimage', packages, source, by:{kind:'user'|'agent', conversation?, name?}, approvedAt, rows, services, why?, deb?}], resolved:{codename, arch, baseSha, debs, at}}, manifestError, rows:[catalog rows app.<entry>], entries:[{id, packages, rows, indexed}], replay:{markerAt, decision:{run, rung, why}, baseShaNow, baseShaStored, last}, replaying, drift:{drift, why, added?, removed?, changed?}, updates:{count, list, at}, refreshedAt, slot, facts, proposals:[open], helpers}` · `GET /api/apps/search?host=&q=` → `{results:[{package, summary}]}` · `POST /api/apps/plan {host, request} | {proposalId}` → `{host, request, plan, facts, install, digest[, proposal]}` — `request` = `{kind:'apt', packages}` | `{kind:'deb', debPath}` | `{kind:'source', source:{id, uris, suites, components, key}}` | `{kind:'source-remove', sourceId}` | `{kind:'remove', entryId}` | `{kind:'refresh'}` | `{kind:'adopt', packages}` | `{kind:'replay', rung?}`; `plan` = `{ok, code, error, canRun, kind, mode, entryId, packages, closure, closureKey, newCount, downloadBytes, installedBytes, origins, replaySeconds, commands, deb?, sourceSpec?, updates?, removes?, nonce}` (refusal codes: no_facts · no_apt · bad_name · not_found · conflict · removes · needs_snap · bad_source · disk · shared · no_sudo · nothing) · `POST /api/apps/install {host, request | proposalId, planDigest}` → 200 `application/x-ndjson`: `{reattached, pid, since}` · `{log}` lines · ONE `{done, host, kind, entryId, label, rows, reattached, run, state}` | ONE `{error, code, plan?, digest?}` (`plan_changed` · `busy` 409 before the stream · `not_run` (another install held the slot) · `refused` · `not_recorded` · `install_*`) · `POST /api/apps/proposals/:id/reject` → `{ok, proposal}` (409 `proposal_state`) · `GET /api/apps/icon?host=&row=` → image/png | image/svg+xml (nosniff, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`), 404 · `GET /api/apps/helper-prompt?host=&q=` → `{prompt}` · `POST /api/apps/helpers {host, sessionId, request}` / `DELETE /api/apps/helpers/:sessionId`. THE AGENT'S FACE (`Bearer vsst_` only — a job token 401; nothing executes): `GET /api/agent/apps?host=` → `{host, entries, proposals (its own), replay, drift, updates, refreshedAt}` · `GET /api/agent/apps/search?q=` · `POST /api/agent/apps/plan {request}` (no argv handed out) · `POST /api/agent/apps/proposals {host?, request (apt | remove | source only — else 403 agent_forbidden), why}` → `{ok, proposal, text}` (a refused plan is never filed: its code) · `GET /api/agent/apps/proposals/:id?wait=<s ≤ 100>&since=<state>` → `{…proposal, text}` (403 `not-yours`; a terminal answer takes back the stashed copy of the outcome) · `GET /api/agent/apps/status` · `GET /api/agent/apps/kinds` → `{kinds}` (the declared app-kind rows of src/app-kinds.js, one per kind — what `vibespace-app list / add / install` read; lane dc-apps-rows) · `POST /api/agent/apps/user-kind {kind:'uv-tool'|'npm'|'appimage', name, why}` (records a user-level tool the agent installed itself). Docs topic `apps` (`GET /api/agent/docs/apps`). WS broadcast `apps-updated {host}`. The `desktop-serve` op table gains `app-status` · `app-plan` · `app-install` · `app-remove` · `app-refresh` · `app-adopt-drift` (asked of a paired machine only when its hello-ack names `app-install`); `GET /api/desktop/apps` registry rows of installed apps carry `app` (the entry), `icon`, `desktop`, `package` and — unavailable — `reasonCode` `app-missing` | `restoring` | `restore-failed`. For-you items of origin `apps`: action `{type:'app-install', id, host, kind}`.
- **THE APPS OPERATOR DOOR (lane app-persist-layer1) — NOT HTTP:** a unix socket of the server's account, `~/.vibespace/apps/operator/ops.sock` (0600 in a 0700 dir, opened after listen; src/server/apps-ops-door.js). One request per connection: one NDJSON line ≤ 4 KiB within 5 s, `{"v":1,"op":"refresh","apply":false|true}`. Answer = NDJSON: `{log}` lines while a run streams, then ONE `{ok:true, plan:{layer:'sys'|'host', canRun, updates:[{package,from,to}], count, skippedHost:[id]}}` (apply false) | `{ok, done:true, layer, known, upgraded, partial, refreshedAt, skippedHost}` (apply true = the user's Refresh run through the ONE slot, the plan's digest, by operator) | `{ok:false, code, error}` — codes bad-request · bad-version · bad-op (the op set is closed: refresh only) · too-long · timeout · busy (+ since) · no_sudo · the plan's own refusal · refresh_failed · plan_changed. Client: `vibespace-app refresh [--all] [--json]` with no session (exit 0 / 1 / 2 in a conversation / 3 no door / 75 busy).
- **OPEN WITH LIBREOFFICE (docs/design-desktop-apps.zh.md §7.9, 2026-09-27; PURE src/office-open.js is the ONE verdict).** `POST /api/desktop/apps` takes `file` (the absolute path ON the file's machine) + `fileHost` (default `host`): the route runs the machine rule before any machine is asked — 409 `machine-mismatch` (the app runs where the file is), 400 `relative-path` / `not-office-file` / `not-office-app`; no `appId` ⇒ the file's module row (`libreoffice-writer` | `-calc` | `-impress`); the machine answers 409 `app-absent` (+ `remedy {what, label, packages}` on a local launch), 404 `file-missing`, 409 `host_needs_daemon` (an agent predating the office rows). The record gains `office`, `file`, `fileMtimeAtLaunch`, `profileDir` (the session's own LibreOffice profile), and at its end `fileMtimeAtEnd`, `fileChanged`, `fileEndError?`, `fileLockRemovedAt` | `fileLockKept`, `fileSignalledAt`. **`GET /api/desktop/open-with?host=&path=&app=[&peek=1]`** → the verdict WITHOUT launching, 200 either way (`peek=1` — the explorer's menu: a paired machine not connected right now is not connected for a menu, the answer carries `unchecked: true`): `{ok:true, catalogId, module, file, label, hostId, host}` | `{ok:false, code, error, remedy?, host}` (codes in judging order: relative-path · not-office-file · machine-mismatch · host-unreachable · not-office-app · host_needs_daemon · app-absent). `GET /api/desktop/install-plan?host=&what=` — `what` = `xpra` (default) | `libreoffice-writer` | `libreoffice-calc` | `libreoffice-impress` | `libreoffice` (400 `bad-request` otherwise); a LibreOffice plan = `{ok, source:'apt', what, label, packages, script, commands, canRun, code, error, root}`. **`POST /api/desktop/install {host, what}`** = the install-xpra stream for any `what` (NDJSON `{reattached,pid,since}` · `{log}` · ONE `{done, what, available:true, label, source, hostId, reattached}` | ONE `{error, code, plan?}` — a LibreOffice install is done only when the machine now serves the row, else `still-absent`); `/install-xpra` unchanged. **WS broadcast `file-changed` `{host: null|<hostId>, path, mtime, by:'desktop-app', appId, label}`** — ONCE per ended session whose document's mtime moved (the page relays it as the `vibespace:file-changed` window event; the code editor re-checks the file at once).
- **A FIXED PICTURE SIZE AT LAUNCH (lane launch-picture-size, .272; the table = src/desktop-pin.js SIZE_CHOICES).** `POST /api/desktop/apps` (both shapes, any `host`) takes `pin: 'WxH' | {w,h} | null` — judged by parsePin at the ROUTE (400 `bad-size` / `size_out_of_bounds`, its own words) and again on the machine; once the rung resolves, a rung that cannot hold it answers 400 `pin_unsupported` ("a fixed picture size needs the xpra or Xvfb display backend — this app would run on <backend> (<via>), where the picture follows the pane; …") before any record exists; else the answer's record is BORN with `pin` (a relaunch carries its own). `POST /api/agent/window/open` takes `size: 'WxH' | 'auto'` (pinVerdict, the caller as the opener; 400 `bad-size` / `size_out_of_bounds` / `pin_unsupported`, nothing launched). The CDP rung: `POST /api/agent/browser/size {size: 'WxH'|'auto', profile?, check?}` (agent Bearer — its OWN conversation; a `sessionId` naming another ⇒ 403 `not_your_window`; `check: true` judges only) and `POST /api/browser/size {sessionId, size, profile?}` (the user's, any live session; an agent's session or job token ⇒ 403 `agent_forbidden` — its door is the one above; int271 seam 2) → `{ok, fixed: {w,h}|null, applied: 'live'|'now'|'later'|'none'|'check', code?, why?}`; a bad size 400 in parsePin's words; no such session 404. The live view's stream answers a viewer's `fit {claim|force}` while fixed with `{type:'refused', code:'size_pinned', width, height, error}` and its fit records carry `state: 'fixed'`. CLIs: `vibespace-window open <app> [--size WxH]`, `vibespace-browser size <WxH|auto>`, `vibespace-browser open <url> --size WxH` (judged before the open, applied after); a refused size exits 2.
- **DESKTOP APPS (docs/design-desktop-apps.zh.md, P8-1 2026-09-13; every failure is `{error, code}`). LANE D (b) (2026-09-25): `POST /api/desktop/apps` (both shapes, any `host`) takes `scaleChoice` = the app's default scale chosen in the launch dialog (`'auto'` | 1 | 1.5 | 2 | 2.5 | 3; 'auto' / absent = none; anything else 400 `bad-request` `scaleChoice must be one of auto, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3`, refused by the ROUTE before a paired machine is asked); the record then runs at it with `scaleOrigin: 'app'` (precedence: a relaunch's choice > the app default > an explicit `desktop.appScale` > auto); a paired machine receives it inside the `launch` op's `body` (op shape unchanged; an older agent drops it). `POST /api/desktop/apps/:id/relaunch` `scale` accepts 2.5 and 3 too (the menu's rows = auto + 1 / 1.5 / 2 / 2.5 / 3). User state `desktopAppScale` = `{<app id | exec:basename>: 1|1.5|2|2.5|3}` (merge-only PATCH, broadcast `user-state-updated`; auto REMOVES the key). LANE C2 (2026-09-25): `GET /api/desktop/apps?host=<hostId>` and `POST /api/desktop/apps {host, …}` run on THAT machine (a paired device's agent through the `desktop-serve` op; '' / `local` = this machine exactly as before; a malformed host 400 `bad-request`, an unknown one 400 `unsupported-host`, an agent without the capability 409 `host_needs_daemon`, no answer 503 `host_unavailable`) — the list answers THAT machine's `registry` / `availability` / `cap` + `host:{hostId, label}` and the running `apps` of EVERY machine; every other route names a record by id on whichever machine holds it. A paired machine's record carries `hostId` (the hub's host id), `hostLabel` (a display string) and, while its machine does not answer, `state:'unknown-host-offline'` + `lastKnownState` + `hostError` (a view state only). `GET /api/desktop/machines` → `{machines:[{hostId, label, transport:'local'|'dial'|'ssh', link, connected, capabilities, platform, selectable, code:'ready'|'connect'|'offline'|'host_needs_daemon'|'no_x11'}]}` (built without a connect ladder). `GET /api/desktop/install-plan?host=` → `{hostId, facts:<installFacts>, plan:{ok, code?, error?, source:'apt'|'xpra.org', packages, script, commands, canRun, already, aptXpra, installed, root}, digest}` (verify-r6 I1: `digest` = the plan SHOWN — commands + source + packages — null for a refused plan). `POST /api/desktop/install-xpra {host, planDigest?}` / `POST /api/desktop/install {host, what, planDigest?}` (verify-r6 I1: a `planDigest` that is not the plan the machine would run now ⇒ the stream's ONE line is `{error, code:'plan_changed', plan, digest}` — the new plan, nothing run) → 200 `application/x-ndjson`: `{log}` lines, then ONE `{done:true, installed, source, hostId, reattached}` or `{error, code:'no_x11'|'no_apt'|'no_repo'|'no_sudo'|'no_facts'|'install_failed'|'install_timeout'|'install_link_lost'|'install_unrecorded'|…, plan?}` (`install_timeout` = past 15 min, the install STILL RUNNING there — never killed; `install_link_lost` = the device's link dropped mid-run; `install_unrecorded` = it ended without recording its exit — killed / a reboot). VERIFY r2 (2026-09-25): the install runs DETACHED on the machine (its own session; log `xpra-install.log`, pidfile `xpra-install.pid` = "<pid> <starttime>", exit `xpra-install.exit` = "<code> <unix s>" in the machine keeper's data dir — a device's `~/.vibespace`, the hub's `data/`); an install already running there is RE-ATTACHED — the stream's FIRST line is `{reattached:true, pid, since}`, then its log from the start — never a second apt; 409 `busy` while THIS hub follows one on that machine — including one it stopped following (timed out / link lost) until the machine's facts say it is gone (at most 60 min). A per-click op on a paired machine whose link dies mid-request answers 503 `host_unavailable` at once (the agent client fails in-flight requests on link loss). The hosted xpra client (`/api/desktop/:id/xpra-ui/*`) of a remote record is the hub's own xpra tree, else the device's read through its agent.** (Pre-C2 text: every route takes `host` and REFUSES a non-local one — 400 `{error, code:'unsupported-host'}` — v1 was local-only.) `GET /api/desktop/apps` → `{apps:[record + live{cpuPct, memBytes, memMetric ('pss'|'anon'|'anon-sum'|'rss'), over, since, sampledAt, pids, rssBytes (DEPRECATED — ΣVmRSS, a fact never judged; one release)} + idle{idleMs,limit,remainingMs,expired}], registry:[row + available/path/reason/reasonCode] (`parkedUntil` retired 2026-09-25 — there is no park), availability:{backend, via, fallbackWhy, ladder:[{backend, ok, via, why}], xpra, bins}, cap:{used, cap}, idleTimeoutMin}` · `POST /api/desktop/apps` body `{appId}` | `{exec, args?, cwd?, label?}` → the record (state `launching`; 400 bad-request/exec-not-found/cwd-missing/needs-wayland, 409 cap/no-backend/backend-not-wired (`runaway-parked` retired 2026-09-25 — a launch is never refused by a resource sample; the record's `live` = `{cpuPct, memBytes, memMetric, rssBytes (deprecated), pids, over, since, sampledAt}`)) · `GET /api/desktop/apps/:id` → the record (404 not-found) · `POST /api/desktop/apps/:id/stop` → the record (`exited`, `stoppedBy:'user'`) · `POST /api/desktop/apps/:id/keep-alive` → the record with `idleTimeoutMs:0` · **P8-2 chunk x1 (2026-09-21):** `GET /api/desktop/apps/:id/windows` → `{ok, why, windows:[{id, title, cls, instance, x, y, w, h, mapped}]}` (the app's windows on its display — on an xpra display xpra's own wrappers filtered; an on-demand snapshot, the live title rides the picture) · `GET /api/desktop/:id/xpra-ui/` → 302 to `…/xpra-ui/index.html?path=/api/desktop/<id>/stream&…` and `GET /api/desktop/:id/xpra-ui/*` = the installed xpra's UPSTREAM html5 client served behind the cookie auth (nosniff, no-store, no listing, no dotfiles; 409 `not-xpra` for a record on another rung, 503 `xpra-ui-unavailable` without a www tree; `?netem=rtt:200,kbps:1000` on the bare dir is remembered for the id ONLY under `VIBESPACE_DESKTOP_NETEM=1`, echoed in `X-VibeSpace-Netem`) — the D21 (c) (a) validation slice. **P8-2 chunk x2 (2026-09-22): the window no longer shows that page — it loads `…/xpra-ui/js/Protocol.js` (with its `lib/*.js` + `Utilities.js`) through this route as its protocol WORKER (same-origin, cookie-authed) and draws the session itself; the html5 page stays reachable as the validation slice.** `availability` gained `stream` (the winner's kind) + `prefs` (the instance order); the record gained `stream` ('rfb'|'xpra', from the table) + `probe` ('rfb'|'http', the READY probe's kind). Setting `desktop.backendPrefs` (string, default '') = a comma list of rung ids that REORDERS the ladder for NEW apps (unknown ids ignored; a running app keeps its backend). Record shape (§4, facts only): `{id, label, exec, args, cwd, env?, source:'registry'|'adhoc', appId?, backend, via, recipe (r2: the bring-up recipe name the table gave it), fallbackWhy, display, port, pids:{x,app,server,wm}, starts:{…}, startedAt, readyAt?, adoptedAt?, endedAt?, state:'launching'|'ready'|'exited'|'failed', exitCode, lastError, stoppedBy?, idleTimeoutMs, lastInputAt}`; `live` (in GET/broadcast views) = `{cpuPct, rssBytes, pids, sampledAt}` over the session SET (r2), `availability.recipe` names the winner's recipe. r2: `port` is a MEASURED fact — the record turns `ready` only when the 127.0.0.1 listener is held by the recorded picture server's session, else `failed` naming the port and pid. **P8-2 x3 (2026-09-22):** every route above is driven on a real server with cookie auth by test-desktop-xpra; `PATCH /api/settings` takes `desktop.idleTimeoutMin` and `desktop.backendPrefs` live, both read at LAUNCH and stamped on the record (a running app keeps what it was born with); `availability.xpra` = `{version, raw, www}` of the installed xpra (the html5 tree the window loads its protocol worker from); an xpra session's `port` is a 127.0.0.1-only listener (the binding check). **P8-2 chunk x4 (2026-09-22): the record view carries `fitMode` ('follows' | 'fixed' | 'client' | 'shared' | null — from the rung's `fit` column by `via`), `fitBy` ('keeper' | 'client' | null), `fb {w, h, at}` (the framebuffer as xdpyinfo states it, measured by the keeper) and `fit {ok, wid, w, h, moved, at, why, ms, via}` | `{ok:false, why, at}` (the last app-fit act, or a refusal by name; `via` 'wm' = the CLIENT maximised through the window manager, 'xdotool' = moved/resized directly — 2026-09-22; under a WM `wid` is the CLIENT inside the frame, never the frame); `GET /api/desktop/apps/:id/windows` rows gained `depth` (1 = a top-level; under a reparenting WM the app's client is depth 2 inside a nameless depth-1 frame). x4 close-out: the record carries `appTitle` (string | null — the fitted app window's own title on a keeper-fitted rung, APP-CONTROLLED text, textContent only), and a window whose name xwininfo cannot print (COMPOUND_TEXT, a failed conversion) is still a row with `title: null` (it used to be dropped).** **2.369.158 (HiDPI, design-desktop-apps §7.6):** `POST /api/desktop/apps` takes `dpr` (the launching client's devicePixelRatio, a number 1..3; absent ⇒ 1; anything else ⇒ 400 bad-request `dpr must be a number from 1 to 3`); the record carries `scale` (1 | 1.5 | 2 — fixed at launch from the setting `desktop.appScale` (enum auto | 1 | 1.5 | 2, default auto = 2 when the dpr is 1.5 or more, else 1 — never the text-only 1.5 (r2); xpra stream only, 1 elsewhere)) and `dpi` (the display's font dpi, 96 × scale / GDK_SCALE — `xpra start --dpi`; the window's client sends the same value in its hello / display-configure). **Round 3 A3 (2.369.166, docs/design-desktop-apps-seamless §3.4):** the launch also takes `uiScale` (the launching client's UI scale = its body zoom as a fraction, 0.6..2; absent ⇒ 1; else 400 `uiScale must be a number from 0.6 to 2`) and `auto` DERIVES from `dpr × uiScale` (clamped 1..3): GDK_SCALE by the ratio-nearest integer (midpoints √2 / 2√2), the fraction only UPWARD into the dpi — 1.25 ⇒ 1×/120, 1.5 ⇒ 2×/96, 2.5 ⇒ 2×/120, 3 ⇒ 3×/96; `scale` is now any value 1..3 and the record adds `scaleOrigin` (`auto` | `setting` | `chosen`, null off the xpra stream) + `scaleFrom` (`{dpr, uiScale}` for auto). `POST /api/desktop/apps/:id/relaunch` body `{scale: 'auto'|1|1.5|2, dpr?, uiScale?}` → `{app, replaced}`: the SAME app (a catalog row by `appId`, a typed command as typed) launched at that scale (`chosen`; `auto` re-derives from the RELAUNCHING client — the one being replaced does not count against the cap), then the old session stopped with `stoppedBy: 'relaunch'` + `replacedBy: <new id>` (committed BEFORE its stop broadcasts — every window showing the old id follows the new one in place); 409 `not-ready` (not running / already stopping) / `not-xpra` (a whole-display rung has no scale) / `relaunch-browser` (a browser's profile belongs to its session), 400 `bad-request` (a scale outside the four), 404 `not-found`. On the xpra stream the client's desktop size and every geometry packet are DEVICE px (pane CSS px × devicePixelRatio) and its hello lists `size-constraints` in `metadata.supported`. **B-bfe6 (2.369.166):** `POST /api/desktop/apps {appId, url?, keepProfile?}` — `url` (http/https, ≤ 2048) and `keepProfile` (boolean) are for a BROWSER row (`registry[].browser` = 'chromium'|'firefox'; the row also answers `execs`, the served `exec`/`path`/label and `confinement`); elsewhere 400 `not-a-browser`; a bad URL 400 `bad-url`; 400 `automation-flag` / `profile-not-owned` / `profile-is-users`; 409 `browser-absent` / `snap-profile-unreachable`. A browser record carries `browser`, `profileDir`, `keepProfile`, `url`, `confinement` and, once ended, `profileRemovedAt` | `profileKept` | `profileError`. **Round 3 A2 (2.369.166):** a record that ended by an APP EXIT carries `windowsAtExit` (integer | null — the windows a person could still see on the app's own display at the exit, counted by the keeper before the teardown; null = not counted: the shared singleton, a failed census) in the GET and in the `desktop-apps-updated` broadcast; a stop carries none (`stoppedBy` names it). Every client's window closes itself on `exited` with `stoppedBy` set or no window left (`windowsAtExit` 0 or null) unless an agent lease holds the app; `failed` never.
- **A WINDOWS / macOS MACHINE'S WHOLE DESKTOP (design 014 D1, lane desktop-vnc-native, 2.369.204) — people only: any agent token (Bearer, body `token`, `?token=`) is 403 `human_only`.** `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/connect {host}` → `{ok, id: 'machine-desktop.<host>', streamPath, platform, auth: ard|password|user|none, type}` | `no_vnc` (409, with `vnc` = the probe) / `not_desktop_machine` / `host_unavailable`. `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/run {host, cmd}` → `{ok, shown}` (the line as it ran, detached) | `multi_line` / `hidden_chars` / `too_long` / `empty` (400), `run_failed` / `not_desktop_machine` (409). `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/last-close?host=` → the bridge's last close (`machine-offline` when its link dropped). `GET /api/desktop/install-plan?host=&what=tightvnc` + `POST /api/desktop/install {host, what: 'tightvnc', planDigest}` (NDJSON; `no_admin` / `not_windows` / `plan_changed` / `busy` / `still-absent` by name). WS `/api/desktop/machine-desktop.<host>/stream` (403 `human_only` on a token). `GET /api/desktop/machines` rows of a connected Windows / macOS machine carry `desktop: 'vnc-native'`, `setup`, `vnc` and code `desktop_ready` / `no_vnc` / `offline`.
- **A WINDOWS / macOS MACHINE'S INSTALLED APPS (lane machine-desktop-apps, 2.369.252) — people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token).** `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/apps?host=<id>[&fresh=1]` → `{hostId, platform, rows: [{name, launch, source: start-menu | uninstall | applications | install, seenAt, pending?}], total, truncated, listedAt, code?, error?}` — the list the hub keeps (data/desktop-apps-hosts.json `installed`), listed again when due (`fresh=1` = Check again; else only when never listed or older than 10 min). Answered 200 with the list as it was when the machine cannot list now: `code` `host_needs_daemon` (its agent predates the op — never asked), `offline`, `list_failed`; a Linux machine / this machine 409/400-class `not_desktop_machine`, an unknown host `unsupported-host`. **Device op `desktop-apps-list`** (capability `desktop-apps-list` in the hello-ack; client `DeviceManager.desktopAppsList({budgetMs})` gated; reply `desktop-apps-list-result` `{result: {ok, platform, rows, total, truncated, listedAt, ms, timedOut?} | {ok:false, code, error}}`) — the daemon runs src/installed-apps.js listInstalled where the apps are; the hub re-judges every row (`rowsFromDevice`: a launch line `desktopRunPlan` accepts, a known source, a name ≤ 200).
- **THE DESKTOP PICKER'S MACHINE DISPLAYS + LINK (lane desktop-v2-entry, design q-030) — people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token).** `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/displays?host=<id>` → the device op `display-facts` (capability-gated; agentd: a Mac's JXA `NSScreen.screens`, Windows' PowerShell `Screen.AllScreens`, bounded 10 s) `{ ok, platform, displays: [{id, name, x, y, w, h, scale, primary}] }` in a TOP-LEFT origin (a Mac's frames flipped against its primary; points ×1, the page's PURE `screenMap` finds the factor) — 200 `{ok:false, code}` for `host_needs_daemon` / `host_unavailable` / `displays_failed` / `bad_output`. `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/link?host=<id>` → `{ device: ms|null, took }` (one mux PING; the page subtracts `took` from its own timing for browser ⇄ hub). `GET /api/vnc/status` gained `seat: 'wayland'|null` (the hub's own WAYLAND_DISPLAY / XDG_SESSION_TYPE — the picker's local row words). Client agent method `DeviceManager.displayFacts()`.
- **ONE APP OF A WINDOWS / macOS MACHINE AS ITS OWN WINDOW (lane machine-app-crop) — people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token).** `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/run` takes `window: true` ⇒ the answer adds `launched: {pid, at} | null` (the machine's own pid + clock). `POST /api/desktop/machine-app/track {host, pid?, since?, target?}` → `{key, answer, pin}` (`key` null + `answer.state` none / error when no window appeared within 20 s; `host_needs_daemon` 409 for an agent without the op — never asked); `GET /api/desktop/machine-app/watch?key=&seq=` (long poll ≤ 25 s) → `{key, seq, answer, pin, ended?}`; `POST /api/desktop/machine-app/on-top {key, on}` → the device's pin answer + `pin` (one per machine); `POST /api/desktop/machine-app/untrack {key}`. `answer` = `{state: found | gone | none | error, hwnd | windowId, pid, title, rect: {x,y,w,h}, screen: {w,h,scale}, minimized}`. Device ops (agentd, capability-gated): `window-track {pid?, since?, target?, findMs?, watch?, wid?}` → `window-track-result {result}`, pushes `window-rect {wid, result}`; `window-untrack {wid}`; `window-pin {wid, on}` → `window-pin-result {result: {ok, pinned, state?, note?}}`.
- **THE REMOTEAPP SETTINGS CARD OF A WINDOWS MACHINE (lane remoteapp-r1b, 2.369.291, design q-024 r1 §1.3–§1.4) — people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token).** `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/remoteapp/setup?host=<id>` → `{setup}` = the sealed row's view `{id:'remoteapp', hostId, fields, values:{user, domain, certFp}, set:{user, domain, password, certFp}, missing, hasValues, updatedAt, storeError, refused: {code:'rdp_cert_changed', id, at} | null}` — never a password byte, never a mask tail. `POST …/remoteapp/setup {host, user?, domain?, password?}` (omitted = untouched, '' = cleared; a certFp in the body is ignored) → `{setup}`; 400 `invalid-values` naming the field. `POST …/remoteapp/trust {host}` = the first connection (`/auth-only /from-stdin:force /cert:tofu` through the loopback forward) → `{setup}` with the pin (+ `changed`, `client`); 409 `remoteapp_needs_setup` / `rdp_auth_failed` / `rdp_unreachable` / `rdp_session_busy` / `rdp_trust_busy` / `rdp_trust_timeout` / `rdp_trust_no_cert` / `rdp_client_refused` — nothing pinned. `GET …/remoteapp/facts?host=<id>` → `{ok, platform, facts, rows:[{key, met, code, optional, command?}], ready}` | `{ok:false, code:'agent_too_old', have, need}` (an agent without the capability is never asked) | `{ok:false, code:'facts_failed'|'bad_output'}`; a Mac ⇒ 409 `remoteapp_not_windows`. The store broadcasts `integration-machine-updated {id, hostId, why, view}` on a write. **Device op `remoteapp-facts`** (agentd, capability `remoteapp-facts`): `{op:'remoteapp-facts', id}` → `{op:'remoteapp-facts-result', id, result: parseRemoteappFacts' answer + platform}`.
- **A MACHINE'S REMEMBERED DESKTOP SIGN-IN (lane desktop-vnc-remember, 2.369.315) — people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token); no answer ever carries a password.** `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/signins?host=<id>` → `{signin: {kept, auth: ard|vnc|null, at, locked, refused, can, auto}}`; without `host` → `{signins: {<hostId>: state} (kept rows only), locked}`. `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/signin {host, username?, password}` (after the machine took that sign-in) → `{signin}` — seals the per-machine row `desktop-signin/<host>` (kind = the hub's probe), 409 `vault_locked` / `signin_unsupported`, 400 `bad-request` (no password; an ARD row without a name), 404 `unsupported-host`. `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/signin/forget {host}` → `{signin}` (allowed while locked). `POST …/connect` answers `signin` too. The hub reads the row only in the picture bridge (src/server/rfb-signin.js) — never a route.
- **THE WEBRTC STREAM OF A MACHINE'S DESKTOP (lane stream-p1a-transport, design q-041 r2 §2 — P1 part A + P1b) — people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token).** `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/stream/start {host, maxKbps?, source?}` (`source` = `{kind:'display'|'window', id}` — lane stream-p3m-build: ONE window of a Mac, or a display by id; none = the main display) → `{take: false, code, why}` (the rung not taken — `agent_too_old` · `not_desktop_machine` · `no_screen_source` · `no_window_source` · `no_helper` · `screen_permission` · `helper_changed_regrant` · `too_many_streams` · `bad_source` · `offer_failed` · `stream_unsupported` · `host_unavailable`: the window shows the VNC picture) | `{take: true, key, sid, offer:{type:'offer', sdp}, source:{kind, origin:{x,y}, fps}, codec:{mimeType, fmtp}, seq, platform}` — the SDP is the agent's (ICE-lite, BUNDLE, H.264 42e01f packetization-mode 1, one `input` data channel) plus the hub relay's `typ relay` candidates. Then by `key`: `POST …/stream/answer {key, sdp}` → `{ok}` (409 `answer_failed`) · `POST …/stream/candidate {key, candidate:{candidate, sdpMid, sdpMLineIndex}}` → `{ok}` (an empty candidate = `{skipped:'end'}`) · `POST …/stream/restart {key}` → `{offer, seq}` (an ICE-restart offer of the SAME session; 409 `restart_failed`) · `GET …/stream/watch?key&n&seq[&path=direct|relay][&waitMs]` THE LONG POLL → `{key, seq, n, cands:[candidate|null…] (from index n), state, stats:{fps, kbps, frames, dropped, encodeMs, bweKbps, congested, keyRequests, input:{n, refused:{code, at}|null, dropped}, ackMs, source, relay}, ended: null | 'stopped' | 'link_lost' | 'ice_failed' | 'idle' | 'closed' | 'not_found', relay:{port, up, down, dropped}|null}` (answers at once when seq is behind, else ≤ 25 s) · `POST …/stream/stop {key, why?}` → `{stopped}` · `GET …/stream/facts?host=` → `{sessions:[{key, sid, hostId, codec, source, startedAt, state, stats, path, ended}]}` (THE RECORD; `&machine=1[&force=1]` adds `machine` = the agent's `stream-facts` answer or `{ok:false, code, error}` — lane stream-p1b-screen). 404 `not_found` = that stream ended. **The data channel `input`** (one JSON object a message — src/stream-model.js INPUT_KINDS, anything else refused by name): `{t:'mv', x, y}` · `{t:'md'|'mu', x, y, b:0|1|2}` · `{t:'wh', x, y, dx, dy}` (120 a notch, dy > 0 = up) · `{t:'kd'|'ku', c:<DOM code>}` · `{t:'clip', text}` (≤ 65 536 chars) · `{t:'ack', f:<RTP timestamp>}`; x, y = the source's pixel + its origin. **THE VIEWER'S MICROPHONE (lane stream-mic-road, 2.369.339):** `…/stream/start {…, mic: true}` asks for the microphone's audio m-line (an agent that says `stream-mic` adds it recvonly, Opus, in the one BUNDLE; the answer's `mic: true` says it did — an older agent's never does); the long poll's `stats.mic` = `{state: 'idle'|'live'|'no_target'|'closed', to: 'virtual'|'speakers', device:{name}, mic:{name}|null, packets, bytes, dropped, runs, startedAt, ended:{why, at}|null, helper:{ev, underruns, frames}|null, code, error}` and `stats.ice` (werift's ICE view: `{state, nominated:{local, remote}|null, remotes:[…], pairs:[{local, remote, state, nominated, remoteNominated, rx, tx}]}`). `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/mic?host=[&force=1]` → `{hostId, facts, state:{state:'installed'|'reboot_owed'|'inactive'|'absent'|'unsupported'|'unknown', platform, which, render, mic, speaker, defaultMic, micIsDefault, target, code, error}, doors:{make, restore}, words:{road, key, params, note}}` (agent_too_old 409; this machine `not_desktop_machine`) · `POST …/mic/default {host, to:'virtual'|'restore'}` → the same + `{to, device}` (`already_default` · `not_installed` · `nothing_to_restore` · `set_failed`). The setup rows: `GET /api/desktop/install-plan?host&what=virtual-mic|virtual-mic-remove[&lang=zh|ja|en]` → `{hostId, mic, plan:{ok, what, which:'vbcable'|'vibespace-mic'|'blackhole', label, vendor, license, site, source, packages, url, sha256, signer, prompts, notes, removes?, fallbackWhy?, commands, argv, env?, uac|adminDialog, after:'mic'}, digest}` (`already_installed` · `not_installed` · `unsupported` · `no_facts`); `POST /api/desktop/install {host, what, planDigest, lang}` streams as every install and ends `{done, what, label, mic, defaultsKept}` or `{error, code: no_admin | no_password | install_failed | still-absent | still-present | plan_changed}`. People only (`human_only`). Lane speaker-passthrough-v1 (2.369.340): `…/stream/start` takes `audio: true` (the page wants the machine's sound on the stream's audio m-line — asked of a `stream-audio` agent only; the answer's `audio` says whether it was offered) · `POST …/stream/sound {key, on}` → the agent's `stream-audio` answer (`{ok, on, endpoint?, audio}` | `{ok:false, code, error}`; 409 `host_needs_daemon` for an agent without it; 404 `not_found`) — the strip's 「声音」.
- **THE HAND-OVER OF A MACHINE'S DESKTOP (lane desktop-handover, 2.369.312) — the owner's half people only (`human_only` 403 for any agent token):** `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/handover {host, key, principal}` (`key` = the window's stream session, `principal` = the picker's `agent:<conversation id>`) → `{ok, lease:{handle:'md-<host>', holder:{key, name}, input, since, display}}` | 409 `stream_not_live` / `not_a_display` / `host_needs_update` (the agent lacks desktop-grab / desktop-input — checked here) / `no_conversation` / `not_an_agent`; `POST …/handover/pause {host, cause?}` · `…/resume {host}` · `…/revoke {host}` (404 `no_lease`). `stream/start` takes `after` (the previous key: a display switch carries the lease); the `stream/watch` answer carries `handover: {state: agent|user|rebinding|none, holder, dot, own, ended?}` (verify r1: every stream of the machine hears its lease; `own` = this stream carries it — the pointer draws only there). THE AGENT'S half = the window-target routes on `md-<host>` handles: `GET /api/agent/window/targets` adds `machines:[{handle, label, origin:'machine', state, mode:'pixels', shot}]`; screenshot → image/png + `X-Window-Shot {w, h, originX, originY, coords:'display', machine, display}`; act click(at, button) / key(chord) / type(text, keys?) / scroll(direction, by, at); attach (held) / detach (hands it back) / snapshot (409 mode_pixels) / watch. Codes: 403 desktop_not_handed · chord_refused, 409 window_paused · desktop_switching · screenshot_first · host_needs_update · mac_still_pending, 400 outside_display · type_too_long · not_ascii, 404 not_found (fix r2: the machine's stream session is gone — the lease ended `daemon_gone`); a cut act's 409 window_paused carries `did {partial, sent, of, charsTyped?, cancelled?, released?}`; a paste's `did.clipboard: 'restoring'` (Windows: the machine's own clipboard comes back). Device ops `desktop-grab {sid}` → `{ok, png64, w, h, display, origin, road}` and `desktop-input {sid, events ≤ 200}` → `{ok, n, refused:[{i, code}], display, clipboard?}` (the stream session's own sink, the agent's lane — fix r2: an owner record drops the rest ⇒ `+ {partial, cancelled:'owner_input', sent, of}`; over 200 ⇒ `too_many_events`).
- **ONE WINDOW OF A MACHINE AS THE STREAM (lane stream-p2w-build, design q-041 r3 §3 P2w) — people only.** `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/stream/start {host, source: {kind:'window', id}, fp16?}` (int304: the Mac's body — one door for a Windows and a Mac window) = the same session as the desktop's with the machine's window as the source (`take:false` codes add `bad_source` / `agent_too_old` / `no_window_source`; a Windows agent refuses a non-whole-number id `bad_window`); `GET /api/desktop/machine-desktop/stream/windows?host=` → the agent's `stream-windows` answer (the picker — a Mac's rows come from its screen helper's facts, the same shape) | `{ok:false, code:'agent_too_old', have, need}` (gated on the op's own capability `stream-windows`); `POST /api/desktop/machine-desktop/stream/restore {host, window}` → that Windows window back from minimized, never the focus (a Mac answers `restore_unsupported`).
- **WS `GET /api/desktop/<id>/stream[?viewer=<id>]`** — THE desktop bridge (src/server/desktop-stream.js): cookie auth at the upgrade INSIDE the bridge (401), 404 when nothing serves the id (lane C2: incl. a paired machine's app whose machine does not answer), 502 `Machine unreachable` when a paired machine's picture forward cannot open (the upstream of a paired machine's app = the hub forward's loopback port, `streamEndpointFor`; everything below is the same relay), an unknown kind 501 by name, `rfb` ⇒ RFB↔TCP to 127.0.0.1:<port> with the /api/vnc backpressure numbers, **`xpra` (P8-2 chunk x1, 2026-09-21) ⇒ ws↔ws to the xpra server's own WebSocket on 127.0.0.1:<port> (subprotocol `binary`, binary frames both ways, one message per packet, the same backpressure / 20 s ping / named close — an upstream `disconnect` packet's reason lands in the close line; MEASURED xpra rule (x2, 2026-09-22): the server sends clipboard tokens to ONE client — the first with clipboard enabled, then whoever last sent input (its `ui driver`) — a viewer that never touched the app receives no copies; input for the idle clock = the packet types a human produces (`XPRA_INPUT_TYPES`), an unreadable packet counts; the policy verdict is applied PER PACKET on this kind too (2026-09-22 — a refused Watch viewer's hello / ping_echo / damage-sequence / map- and configure-window / keyboard-config reach xpra, only its input packets are cut out; the r1 all-or-nothing-per-chunk rule dropped the hello and the pane never got a picture); `?netem=rtt:N,kbps:N` shapes the relay ONLY under `VIBESPACE_DESKTOP_NETEM=1`)**; **P9b: `?viewer=<1–64 [A-Za-z0-9._-]>` names the pane, and when an agent holds a window lease the bridge asks the window-targets engine per client message — Watch ⇒ the viewer's input messages are CUT OUT of the relay (the update request beside them still goes through), taken over ⇒ the holder viewer only, everybody else `held`; the singleton `/api/vnc` is never gated;** INPUT is reported to the keeper (idle clock, throttled 2 s) only for client RFB messages that ARE input — KeyEvent / PointerEvent / ClientCutText / the QEMU extended key event, walked statefully across frames (r2: noVNC sends a FramebufferUpdateRequest on every repaint, which used to count;
