ONE frame shape, and on 2.1.257 exactly ONE producer: the CLI's `system/status` records (`{status:'compacting'}` → compact_start, a `hook_started` inside that stage → hooks_start, `{status:null, compact_result|compact_error}` → compact_end) — which is also the only lane that sees an AUTO compaction; PLUS a terminal `compact_end` with `result:null` whenever the TURN ends (`result`/`compact_boundary`, or the harness's own idle `session_state_changed`) while a compaction is still in flight. `_streamingKind === 'compacting'` is a claim about RIGHT NOW that the client mirrors as a held stage gating the whole guidance card, so EVERY place the server retires that claim says so (one named `retireCompaction`): a PreCompact hook that BLOCKS the compaction emits no outcome record at all, and the ws-handler `/compact` send site sets the kind before the CLI has said anything — before this frame the client kept "Compacting: running <hook> hooks…" forever and every later "Prompt is too long" card lost its rewind-and-retry sentence (2026-09-07). `result:null` on purpose: "ended" is not "succeeded". The declared `compact_progress` record feeds the same frame but has never been observed on our stdout), `page-published` (2.366.0, 2.366.1 also fires on flag changes/removal: `{sessionId, page}` — publish/republish, a visibility or name change, or removal (`page.removed:true`) of a page of this session, by the agent CLI or the file-browser dialog), `goal-updated` (goal/goalStatus/goalElapsed/statusMsg), `exited`, `attached` (includes `messages`, `totalCount`, `isStreaming`, `streamingLabel`, `streamingKind`, `chatStatus`, `taskState`, `goal`, `goalStatus`, `goalElapsed`, `queue`, `queueSupported`, `queueVerbs`, `queueKnown`, `responseStyleLive`, `turnState`, `inProgressTools`), `active-sessions`, `editor-open`, `editor-close`, `effective-size`, `user-state-updated`, `bookmarks-updated`, `settings-updated`, `custom-themes-updated`, `state-sync`, `state-ack`, `state-snapshot`, `layout-sync`, `desktop-updated`, `tasks-updated`, `session-status-updated`, `accounts-updated`, `harness-caps-updated` (S9: `{backend, caps:{fork}}` — a harness capability VERIFIED at runtime, today opencode fork from the serve OpenAPI probed lazily after the first discovery; the client merges it into BACKEND_META caps and re-renders the sidebar; `/api/home harnesses[].caps` carries the same verdict for later loads), `harness-store-updated` (2.369.50: `{backend, parked, parkedKind:'runaway'|'crash'|null, reason}` — a harness STORE that broke; today the opencode serve keeper parking after crashes or after its /proc RUNAWAY guard stopped it. The client stores `reason` on BACKEND_META[backend].storeReason (user actions quote it) and toasts a NEW reason once; `/api/home harnesses[].storeReason` carries the same string for later loads. Pushed only on a CHANGE of reason, so it is not a heartbeat), `server-notice` (2.226.0 probe→user channel: {key,text,level} → client toast + notification history; USE THIS for any new server-side probe instead of inventing a channel — serverNotice(key,text,{level}) in server.js is key-deduped per boot and keeps undelivered keys for retry), `sysinfo-alert`, `error`. Resume no-convo breaker: a failed `claude --resume` arms a 10-min per-conversation cooldown (2.207.1 anti-bootloop); when the transcript verifiably exists the client pops an unmissable retry-now confirm that bypasses it via `ignoreNoConvo` (2.237.0 — the 2.227.3 retry bar went unnoticed at the window bottom, real report). Session `exited` carries `reason`+`detail` from classifyCliDeath (2.226.0 — known CLI canned errors surfaced in the read-only bar / exited overlay; new death signatures belong in that one classifier), `killed` (2.369.16: reply to the REQUESTER of a `kill` — `{sessionId: <the id the client SENT>, resolvedId: <after the stale-id remap>, ok:true}` after the attached-only `exited` broadcast, or `{ok:false, reason:'not-found'}`; the client's `app.killSession` re-sends an unacknowledged plain kill on every reconnect until one of these lands, 3-minute watchdog disarms), `attach-ack` also carries `{progress:{done,total}|null, queued}` every 10s while the session's first-attach history rebuild is queued/running (2.369.16), `attach-ack` (synchronous proof-of-life before the possibly slow `attached`). **Unknown client→server type (Plugin Ph1, the switch's new `default:`)**: `{ type:'error', code:'unknown-type', message:'Unknown message type: <type>', unknownType, reqId? }` — reqId echoed when the request carried one (so `ws.request` callers fail fast), **never a sessionId** (session-scoped `error` frames flip live windows into the exited/read-only path; an unknown type is not a dead session); server side = rate-limited log (one line per type per 10 min with the running count) + telemetry event `ws-unknown-type`. Before this the type was silently ignored. **`session-vcs` (design-unknown-records, 2026-09-21: `{sessionId, host, kind, branch, cwd, at}` — the claude `vcs_state_changed` fact, pushed by session-brain.noteVcsState from either feed to EVERY connected client (not only the session's attached ones — the explorer that must re-list may be a client's only window); the client re-lists every open File Explorer whose directory is that cwd or under it on the same host; the card's git chip rides `active-sessions` (`vcs`), the timeline row rides session-status history; `attached` also carries `backgroundTasks` = the harness's last published level set or null).**

**StateSync's arrival rule — `state-ack`, `from`, `basedOn` (lane statesync-last-arrival, B-cede, 2.369.332)**: a client's `state-set {store, key, value, basedOn}` (basedOn = the store version it had seen) is answered to the SENDER ONLY with `state-ack {store, key, version}` — the version its set produced (a delete of a key the store does not hold is answered with the store's version, broadcast to nobody); the `state-sync` broadcast to the others (and every replayed op of a resync) carries `from` (a per-socket id the hub mints — never the client's text) and `basedOn`. A hub write (no socket) carries neither and is acked to nobody. The client drops a remote op the server ordered before its own acked set of the key and holds one that arrives while its own set awaits the ack (src/sync-arrival.js). A server-owned store (`clientWrites: false`) refuses the write and sends no ack — no page writes one. **The `prepended` stamp (lane stop-refills-composer, .353)**: a `state-sync` op MAY carry `prepended` (a positive integer) — written only by the SERVER (Stop's put-back, src/server/queue-putback.js, on the `drafts` store, key `chat:<id>`; a client never sends one): the op's `value` is the merged draft and its first `prepended` characters are the put-back prefix. A client always APPLIES such an op (the arrival rule's delta row — never held behind an own set nor dropped under one) and the composer lands only the prefix in front of its live box; the op rides the ring, so a resync replays it with the stamp. And a client `state-set` on `drafts`/`chat:<id>` whose `basedOn` is BELOW a put-back's version is stored as that put-back's prefix + its value (the server belt — the client never saw the put-back).

**`viewport` on layout-sync (§6b guard 10, 2026-10-13)**: every client `layout-sync` carries `viewport: 'phone'|'desktop'` (app.isMobile); the server stores it on the desktop's record (`autoSave.viewport` — a stamp inside the sent state is replaced by the message's; an unstamped sync stores none) and rebroadcasts it on the message. A desktop applies a `phone` record as membership + content only (no z / gridBounds / maximize / minimize / snap / sidebarOpen / grid); an unstamped record is treated as a desktop's. A phone's record carries the held record's arrangement verbatim and no box for a window born on the phone.

**The layout-sync shrink belt (`layout-sync-refused`, userW inc-mun7qjmw-iksh, 2026-09-29)**: a client's `layout-sync {desktopId, state, evidence}` now carries `evidence: [{id, why}]` — the windows it closed / moved / replaced / could not build lately (`why` ∈ closed | moved | replaced | unbuilt). The server refuses (PURE `shrinkVerdict`, src/lib/desktop-record.js) a sync that loses ≥ 2 windows of the stored desktop record that neither the evidence nor a gone session explains: nothing written, nothing broadcast, a journal line + telemetry `layout-sync-refused`, and the SENDER gets `{type:'layout-sync-refused', desktopId, reason:'shrink-without-close', unexplained:[winId], arrived:[winId], state:<the record kept>}` — the client reconciles (its view = that record + what it built; kb-design-lessons §6b item 8). A client that carries no evidence (a pre-fix bundle) is judged the same way. Verify r1: the server remembers per desktop what each write ADDED and which socket wrote it (10 min); an unexplained drop of a window ANOTHER socket added lately is refused at any count and named in `arrived`. A reconnecting client re-reads `GET /api/layouts` 250 ms after its socket opens (§6b item 9; retried 3× on failure, verify r2 ⑥). A per-desktop write for a desktop the meta does not name (deleted meanwhile) is refused before the belt: `layout-sync-refused {desktopId, reason:'no-such-desktop', desktops: <the current meta>}` — nothing written, nothing broadcast; the client treats it as the `desktop-updated` it missed (verify r2 ⑦). **THE LAYER GATE (design q-039 P1, 2.369.273):** after the belt, a sync stamped `viewport:'phone'` that moves anything outside the workspace — a held window's z / box / maximize / minimize / snap, the record's grid, sidebar, bars, theme or fonts (PURE `layerGate`, src/lib/desktop-record.js; legacy spellings, absent flags and guard 4's 0.002 normalised) — is refused: `layout-sync-refused {desktopId, reason:'cross-layer', fields:[<canonical names>], state:<the record kept>, sentAt}`, nothing written or broadcast; the client reconciles exactly as for the belt (a phone's re-send is membership only). A desktop / unstamped sync, a first write and a window the record does not hold are never judged (kb-design-lessons §6b item 10). **THE ACK (verify r5 ⑤)**: every `layout-sync` the client sends carries `sentAt` (the save's compose time); the server answers each write it MADE with `{type:'layout-sync-ack', desktopId, sentAt, seq}` after the write + broadcast (a refusal frame carries `sentAt` back too). The client (`LayoutManager.noteLayoutSent` / `_onLayoutAnswer` / `_settleSave`) releases the acts a save carried — the move / geometry witnesses of that desktop (`_carried` per desktop), its held closes, its divider ratios, its dirty mark — only at the ack, never at the send; a refused desktop stays held; a socket that dies is owed nothing (its acts stay held, re-sent after the re-read). An older server answering no ack: 2 s on an open socket, then the send-stamp rule; once an ack has been seen the fallback is off.

**A window never loses its name; unbuilt is not dropped (lane layout-stopped-window, .365)**: a `layout-sync` evidence entry may carry `by` (`{id, why: 'replaced', by: <the window that replaced it>}`) and `why: 'nameless'` (a window this page holds that names no conversation yet — left out of the write). The server's write (PURE `keepNames`) gives a nameless NEW entry the name of the entry it replaced (`by`, else the one dropped entry of its name) or leaves it out; `layout-sync-refused` (`shrink-without-close`) also refuses a stopped / unknown SESSION window dropped as 'unbuilt' at any count (the journal names the titles). The rollback point's `writer.evidence` is `{why: [titles…]}` (was `{why: count}`), `writer.named` = `{carried, withheld}`. r2: a session window entry may carry a top-level `hostId` (its conversation's machine when no openSpec names one — a resume's birth name, a carried name; an openSpec's own `hostId` wins when present); the server's carry keeps it with the conversation.

**Capability-gated session verbs (§2.13, 2026-09-07)**: `review-start` is accepted only when `capsOf(session.backend).review` (codex today) and the codex thread-name writeback on `rename` only when `capsOf(...).renameWriteback` — both were `session.backend === 'codex'` branches. The client mirrors (`BACKEND_META[id].caps.review` / `.renameWriteback` / `.forkAtMessage`) are deep-compared against the server rows by test-harness-contract, which also pins all SIX call sites — including the CLIENT trigger that decides whether `rename-session` is sent at all (`sidebar-state.js renameSession`, gated on the mirror row since round 3) — against a re-introduced backend-id branch, and pins the rename trigger positively so the gate cannot be deleted instead. **The manual codex quota verbs (2.369.151; the reset verb REMOVED in 2.369.157 r2 — it was a second writer outside the engine's `writeResetCredit`, with no spend ceiling, no per-identity floor and no origin, so its failure walked the switch/wait ladder as an auto attempt; a person now spends a credit only through `POST /api/accounts/:id/reset-credit`, and an old client's `codex-reset-credit` frame gets the `default:` `{type:'error', code:'unknown-type'}` answer — what follows describes 2.369.151)**: `{type:'codex-reset-credit', sessionId}` (consume a stored rate-limit reset credit) and `{type:'codex-read-limits', sessionId}` (an on-demand account/rateLimits/read) are written as the SAME-named wrapper stdin verb only for a live CHAT session whose harness row says so — reset credit needs `capsOf(session.backend).resetCredit === true`, read limits needs `quotaProbe === 'rpc-rate-limits'` (the rung usage-pool-engine's probeQuotaForKey writes the same verb on); both were one `session.backend === 'codex'` branch. Codex is served both; claude / shell / an unknown id get `{type:'error', sessionId, code:'not-codex-chat'}` (a scoped refusal the client keeps in its explicit allow-list). The reply rides the event stream as `rate_limits_updated`. Pinned in test-harness-contract (the SITES census + a positive read + the unchanged verdict per harness). No client surface sends `codex-reset-credit` today (the ⟳ sends `codex-read-limits` — and its CLIENT half picks the live session through the client caps mirror `backendFeatureCaps(s.backend).quotaRefresh === 'session-rpc'`, never `(s.backend || 'claude') === 'codex'` (2.369.151; SITES row 'client codex ⟳ trigger' + a positive pin; the census checker also catches the DEFAULTED `(x || 'claude') === 'codex'` spelling it used to read as clean); the engine's auto-consume writes the stdin verb itself, through the spend authorizer — `codex-reset-credit` in SPEND_REASONS).
**Input queue (`queue-op`, 2026-09-06; VERB TABLE 2026-09-07)**: `{type:'queue-op', sessionId, op, id, afterId?, text?}` — act on a message the user sent DURING a running turn. `op` is one of the closed set `QUEUE_VERBS` = `remove | steer | steer-all | reorder | edit | run-now | run-all`, validated against the harness's `backend-caps` `inputModes.queueVerbs` table (NEVER a backend id; the old `{steer, queueOps}` booleans are a DERIVED view of that table), then formatted by the adapter's `formatQueueOp` and written to the wrapper's stdin. **The frame speaks RELATIVE user intent and the wrapper synthesises the app-server's absolute shapes** (design-harness-features §2.1, owner decision 1): `reorder` carries `afterId` (the id it lands BEHIND; `null` = the front of the queue — a POSITION, so the key is present only when the client sent one) and the wrapper turns it into `thread/queue/reorder {queuedSubmissionIds}` — the FULL order — computed from a freshly paged list, so an item another writer queued in between keeps its place; `edit` carries only the new `text` and the wrapper rebuilds the whole `input`, preserving image/localImage/audio/localAudio/skill/mention BY EXCLUSION and clearing the replaced element's `text_elements` — and, when the text really changed, it also emits a `webui_user_retracted` for that row's `clientUserMessageId` (merge review 2026-09-07: the bubble already on screen claims the PRE-EDIT words, codex will commit the NEW ones and can therefore never retire that claim, and an unretired claim DELETES an unrelated codex-only record of the same words later; the bubble itself stays, per this verb's client law that an edit leaves the bubble's meaning untouched); `run-now` REQUIRES its id (`formatQueueOp` throws without one) because `thread/queue/start` with no id drains the whole queue — `run-all` is its own verb for that. Refusals: a verb the harness does not serve is refused with a sentence naming what happens to the message anyway (plus a machine `reason`); the wrapper answers `busy` (a turn is running), `not-editable` (an agent-to-agent message), `anchor-gone`, `stale-order`, `incomplete`, `empty`, `empty-text`, `gone`. A SECOND gate follows it: the RUNNING wrapper's own `caps.inputQueue` advert (`wrapperCaps()` over the sidecar the wrapper writes) **or** the in-band proof that it has already published a `queue_changed` (a REMOTE wrapper's sidecar lives on ITS machine) — a session spawned before this release passes the caps row and would have its frame dropped silently (2.361.1/2.364.1), so it is refused with "its agent predates the input-queue update … Terminate + Resume". Every refusal answers `{type:'error', code:'queue-op-unsupported', scope:'action', sessionId, op, id, error, message}` naming the harness and what it CAN do — **`op` and `id` are ECHOED** because this reply never becomes a `queue_op_result`, and the strip marked that row pending BEFORE dispatching (the client feeds the echo to `setQueueOpResult`, an id-less batch verb ending every pending row). Two SIZE gates guard the one verb that carries user text: `edit` is refused (`reason:'text-too-long'`) above **20000 characters** — the wrapper's own `QUEUE_EDIT_MAX_CHARS`, above which it publishes no `text` and therefore offers no edit control — and the built frame is refused (`reason:'frame-too-large'`) above **64 KiB**, the same threshold the `input` case treats as the pty-stdin shredding line (JSON escaping can multiply a control-char string sixfold, so the character cap does not imply the byte one). The queue-op frame has NO frame-file bypass; it is bounded instead. **A per-session `error` is a SCOPED refusal (renders in-chat, window untouched) only when it carries `scope:'action'` or one of the client's explicit codes (`input-rejected`, `not-codex-chat`, `queue-op-unsupported`)** — "any code is scoped" is wrong: `ended-during-attach` is coded AND fatal, and must keep taking the view-only rescue + Resume bar. Wrapper → server events (inside the normal record stream): `queued_input {msg_id, turn_id}`, `queue_changed {items:[{id, msgId, preview, text?, ts, kind, from}], turn_id, verbs:[…]}` (the WHOLE queue on every change — the app-server's own `thread/queue/changed` carries no items, so the wrapper re-`list`s, PAGING THROUGH `nextCursor` to the end since the verb table (2026-09-07): a full-order verb computed from a truncated page is refused by the app-server, and a truncated publication lies about what is queued; `text` is the item's FULL text for editable user items ≤20000 chars, so the edit control opens the message and not the 120-char preview; `verbs` is the running wrapper's own verb advert, the only one a REMOTE session's orchestrator can see), `queue_op_result {op, id, msg_id, ok, reason, detail}`. A `queue_op_result` also reaches the client as `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue-result', queueOp, id, ok, reason, text}` so the strip row can end its pending state and wear the refusal. The normalizer turns `queue_changed` into `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue', items}` (session state — never a transcript message) and stamps the originating user bubble with `queueState: 'queued'|'steered'|'removed'|null` through the ordinary `edit` op. `attached` and `created` both carry `queue` (the normalizer's `queueState()`), **`queueSupported`** and **`queueVerbs`** (the verb list the RUNNING wrapper serves — its sidecar `caps.queueVerbs`, or the in-band list from its `queue_changed`; a wrapper that adverts a queue with no list is taken to serve the pre-table `remove/steer/steer-all`, so an older session keeps its controls and is refused only the new verbs) — attach resolves `queueSupported` from the wrapper's sidecar advert OR the normalizer having seen a `queue_changed`; create is always `false`. **`queueVerbs` is `null` — never `[]` — when the server knows NOTHING** (create always; attach when neither the sidecar nor an in-band list is readable): an empty ARRAY is a real answer (a wrapper that named no verbs), and the client INTERSECTS with it, so answering `[]` for "unknown" hid the entire strip from a session the server would have served the legacy three for. On the in-band path the client applies the same mapping the server does — a `queue_changed` carrying no `verbs` means `remove/steer/steer-all` (`LEGACY_QUEUE_VERBS`, one frozen array in the PURE `src/backend-caps.js`, imported by both ends). A reconnecting or newly created window therefore starts from a fact. A `queued_input` from a wrapper that never published a queue falls back to the old system card instead of stamping a chip that could never clear. **`interrupt` (Stop) CLEARS THE QUEUE on every harness (owner decision 2026-09-07)**: both wrappers emit a `queue_op_result {op:'remove', ok:true, msg_id, reason:'stopped'}` per dropped entry BEFORE the emptied `queue_changed` (the normalizer clears the chip of anything that leaves the queue with NO result — that means "it ran"), and a dropped agent-to-agent/job message is handed back to the delivery ladder (`peer_message_result`/`peer_result` `ok:false`). codex deletes every queued item BEFORE it sends `turn/interrupt` — the app-server drains its own queue when the turn ends, interrupt included, so the other order loses the race; a `thread/queue/delete` that fails answers `ok:false` (the item is still queued and WILL run) rather than a silently "cleared" queue, a delete the app-server REFUSES (`{deleted:false}` = it drained the item first, so it RAN) answers `ok:false, reason:'gone'` and its peer entry is NOT re-stashed, and the whole sweep is BUDGETED (~2.5s/RPC, ~6s total) so a wedged app-server never holds Stop — whatever is left is reported `ok:false, reason:'timeout'` and the interrupt goes out. **A SECOND `interrupt` frame (double-click, or a second attached client) is the SAME Stop**: the codex wrapper's sweep is single-flight (the second frame rides it — a second sweep used to report the first one's removals as `gone`) and `turn/interrupt` coalesces per turn while its RPC is unanswered, so the pair produces ONE list, ONE delete per item and ONE interrupt. The client half: the Stop button disables itself and reads "Stopping…" until the turn ends or an 8s fallback re-arms it. **…and what Stop dropped is PUT BACK into the composer (owner 2026-10-24, lane stop-refills-composer, .353)**: after the per-item results and BEFORE the emptied `queue_changed`, the acp / agy / codex wrapper emits ONE `queue_dropped {dropId, by:'stop', turnId, items:[{id, msgId, ts, text, cut, attachments:[{type, mime, name}]}], omitted}` — the person's rows only (never a peer / nudge / retry / system row; codex: only `deleted:true` rows, an inherited `peer-`/`notif-` cid never), oldest first, each text ≤ 20000 (`cut`), ≤ 100000 per record (the rest `omitted`), attachments as descriptors; acp / agy include the UNDISPATCHED active prompt as the oldest row; nothing of the person's ⇒ no record. Envelopes: acp / agy `{ts, type:'acp', kind:'queue_dropped', …}`, codex `{timestamp, type:'event_msg', payload:{type:'queue_dropped', …}}` (record-shape row `codex:rollout:event/queue_dropped`). The server's door at the feedLive gate writes the draft (`state-sync` with `prepended`, above) and the normalizer emits `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue-putback', dropId, n, images, others, cut, omitted}` (live only, once per dropId) — the client's ONE toast. acp's `queue-cleared` notice is retired. Claude emits nothing: measured on 2.1.308, the CLI RUNS a frame it queued mid-turn right after the interrupt.
**Claude's two doors (.363, lane claude-steer-inject — the owner's ruling «A»)**: `chat-input` may carry `steer: true` (only `true` counts) — the person's door was «hand it to the running turn»: the composer stamps every send through plain Enter / Send where the PURE `composerSendModes(caps).steerOnEnter` says so (claude on a wrapper that serves 'steer'), never through Alt+Enter / the hold button. The claude adapter's `formatChatInput(text, msgId, {origin, steer})` puts `webui_steer: true` on the frame's top level (the CLI strips it like `webui_msg_id`; never on a frame VibeSpace types — `origin`). A chat-wrapper whose hello names 'steer' (sidecar `caps.queueVerbs` + every `queue_changed.verbs` = `remove · steer · steer-all · reorder · edit`) writes such a frame to the CLI at once even mid-turn; an older one holds it. Claude's `queue-op` serves `steer` (`{type:'queue-op', op:'steer', id}`) and `steer-all` (`{…, id:null}`) = hand a HELD row over (a slash command is refused `not-steerable`); `run-now` / `run-all` stay refused. The wrapper's record `{type:'input_handover', state:'handed'|'landed'|'own-turn'|'kept', msg_ids:[…], via?:'enter'|'steer'}` sets each bubble's `queueState` ('handed' / 'steered' / none / 'kept'); `queue_dropped` may carry `kept: N` (handed messages that stay with the stopped turn — the `queue-putback` meta op then carries `kept`, present only when > 0). **Acceptance r2:** the hello names `steer` / `steer-all` only once the CLI has said its first `session_state_changed` — until then the sidecar and every `queue_changed.verbs` say `remove · reorder · edit` (the .354 three) and a stamped frame is HELD; a held row the wrapper will never hand over carries `steerable: false` (a slash command, a frame VibeSpace typed — `kind:'system'`), and a refused `steer` says why: `queue_op_result {ok:false, reason:'not-steerable', why: 'command' | 'origin' | 'stopping' | 'unproven'}`. Stop's ONE `queue_dropped` adds each handed frame's fate: `kept` + `keptIds` (the CLI still held them — they stay in the conversation, unanswered) and `landed` + `landedIds` (the model had read them); `items` stays the put-back. A mid-turn frame of the person (never a slash command) is copied into the wrapper's HISTORY BUFFER only (never stdout) as `{type:'user', message, msgId, _fromWebui:true, webui_copy:true, timestamp}` — the restart's record of it. **r4:** the wrapper stamps a person's mid-turn frame with a top-level `uuid` (the CLI keeps it as its user record's `uuid` and as a queued_command attachment's `source_uuid`); the history-buffer copy names it `cliUuid`; the claude CLI is spawned with `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_USER_PROMPT_SUBMIT_HOOK_EVENTS` and its UserPromptSubmit `hook_started` / `hook_response` lines are the wrapper's (never on the session stream); a Stop that found a frame `riding` keeps it (`kept`, `keptIds`) and a later `input_handover {state:'landed'}` says it was read. **r5:** the copy's pictures (and a text block over 8 KB) are REFERENCES — `{type:'image', source:{type:'kept', file:'<id>.img/<sha256>.<ext>', media_type, bytes}}` (`unkept` when the file could not be written), the file beside the buffer; the history read puts the bytes back (a missing file ⇒ the message's image block `{type:'image', gone:true, mediaType, bytes}`, drawn «Picture no longer kept»). A new wrapper record `queue_settle` says what a held frame waits on while a window in which the CLI owes a turn is open: `{state:'settling', until:<epoch ms of the backstop>|null (a prompt hook pauses it), ms, hook?:true}` → `{state:'settled'}`, or `{state:'backstop', ms, msg_ids}` when the 30 s backstop delivered them; the normalizer stamps `settling: {until, hook?}` on each held bubble (an `edit` op carrying `queueState` + `settling`) and draws ONE `noticeKind:'queue-backstop'` note at the backstop. A second interrupt sent while the Stop window is open and no turn runs is held by the wrapper and becomes the one interrupt on the follow-up's init (its `request_id` answered by the CLI there). **r6:** a kept reference is `{type:'kept', sha, ext, media_type, bytes}` — the folder is the session's own `<id>.img`, never named in the record (any other shape: the placeholder); a `chat-input` frame carrying one is refused (`kept_refused`). Each copy line carries `handNo` (0 held, n once written to the CLI). `queue_settle` gains `{state:'running', why}` (the owed turn started — no deadline) and `why: 'stop' | 'handover'` on settling; the normalizer's settle edits carry the hub's `now` beside `until`. A Stop folded into a newer one is answered by the wrapper's own `control_response` (`webui_note`). `node scripts/ci.mjs` refuses an unknown flag / `--help` (usage, exit 2); `VS_NO_REAL_CLI=1` skips the two rows that may bill.
**Live response style (`set-response-style`, 2.369.54)**: `{type:'set-response-style', sessionId, style}` — change a RUNNING chat session's response style (`style:''` clears it and hands the choice back to the agent's own config). Three gates, all required and all capability-based: the harness's `backend-caps` `responseStyle {live, values}` row must say `live` (claude reads `--settings outputStyle` once at spawn, so it is refused here and the chip's menu offers "Restart now to apply" instead), the value must be in that row's `values` (codex's `Personality` enum, read from the app-server's own JSON schema), and the RUNNING wrapper must advert `caps.responseStyle` in the sidecar IT writes (the 2.361.1/2.364.1 skew rule — a session spawned before this release would drop the stdin verb silently). Every refusal answers `{type:'error', code:'style-not-live', scope:'action', sessionId, error, message}` (scoped: renders in-chat, leaves the live window alone). On success the server writes `session._outputStyle`, persists it to session meta and broadcasts `{type:'response-style-updated', sessionId, outputStyle, live:true}` — the chip moves on THAT echo, never optimistically, so a refused switch never leaves a lie on the status bar. The EFFECTIVE value also rides the `active-sessions` payload (`outputStyle`, null = the agent's own config decides) so Session Properties can name the value AND its origin.
**Live response style (`set-response-style`, 2.369.58)**: `{type:'set-response-style', sessionId, style}` — change a RUNNING chat session's response style (`style:''` clears it and hands the choice back to the agent's own config). Three gates, all required and all capability-based: the harness's `backend-caps` `responseStyle {live, values}` row must say `live` (claude reads `--settings outputStyle` once at spawn, so it is refused here and the chip's menu offers "Restart now to apply" instead), the value must be in that row's `values` (codex's `Personality` enum, read from the app-server's own JSON schema), and the RUNNING wrapper must advert `caps.responseStyle` in the sidecar IT writes (the 2.361.1/2.364.1 skew rule — a session spawned before this release would drop the stdin verb silently). Every refusal answers `{type:'error', code:'style-not-live'|'style-wrapper-old', scope:'action', sessionId, error, message}` (scoped: renders in-chat, leaves the live window alone). **The two codes mean different things to the UI** (2.369.58 r2, the 2.363.1 one-code-many-meanings law): `style-wrapper-old` = THIS session's wrapper will never serve the verb, so the client stops offering the live switch and shows "⟳ Restart now to apply"; `style-not-live` = every other reason (dead session, spawn-only harness, out-of-enum value, or a sidecar the wrapper has not written YET), and the client changes no capability belief. On success the server writes `session._outputStyle`, persists it to session meta and broadcasts `{type:'response-style-updated', sessionId, outputStyle, live:true}` — the chip moves on THAT echo, never optimistically, so a refused switch never leaves a lie on the status bar. The EFFECTIVE value also rides the `active-sessions` payload (`outputStyle`, null = the agent's own config decides) so Session Properties can name the value AND its origin. **`attached` also carries `responseStyleLive`** (the running wrapper's `caps.responseStyle` advert, same shape and same reason as `queueSupported`): the client needs BOTH facts to decide whether to offer the live switch or the "Restart now to apply" row, and with only the caps row every pre-release codex session got a refusal it could do nothing about. It is TRI-STATE: `null` when the wrapper has not written its sidecar yet (unknown ≠ "cannot"). `created` deliberately OMITS the key — the sidecar does not exist yet, so the client's third state (undefined = try it) stands until the refusal or the echo answers it.
**Command list + init facts (`msg` op=meta, subtype `slash-commands`, 2026-09-07 / §2.6 of docs/design-harness-features.md)**: `{op:'meta', subtype:'slash-commands', data:{commands:[name…], terminal:[name…]}}` — the harness's CURRENT slash-command list and the terminal-BOUND subset the composer must hide (claude's init frame `slash_commands` + `terminal_slash_commands`; the same op re-fires on the CLI's `system`/`commands_changed` push, on ACP's `available_commands_update` and on the codex wrapper's served list — ONE client path). **REPLACE, never append**: a command absent from the payload is gone. It is a `meta` op and NOT an `edit` on the init card on purpose — ChatView does not re-render a COMPLETE system card, so an `edit` never re-runs its side effects and the composer would keep the boot list all session. The ATTACH/HTTP twin is `chatStatus.initFrame` (session-store `chatStatus()`, `{tools, agents, skills, betas, slashCommands, terminalSlashCommands, mcpServers[{name,status}], mcpServerErrors, plugins, pluginErrors, pluginWarnings, outputStyle, version, memoryPaths{auto,team}}` — claude only, `null` elsewhere; keys the CLI did not send are ABSENT, never zero-filled), which also carries `slashCommands` already reconciled with the newest `commands_changed` — **by POSITION**: the push replaces the frame's list only when it is newer than the newest `init` (a resume/wrapper respawn re-inits AFTER a mid-run push, and the twin must land where the live stream did), and an init that declares no list at all leaves the push standing (live, the op is simply not re-emitted for it). Same facts ride the init card's `initData.frame`.
**Usage + the status-bar facts (`msg` op=meta, subtype `usage`; lane agy-status-bar-facts, .342)**: `{op:'meta', subtype:'usage', data:{input_tokens, cache_read_input_tokens, cache_creation_input_tokens, contextWindow, totals, turn?, turnMs?}}` — the LAST model call's reading (context + cache), `totals` the conversation's running total in codex's shape `{total_tokens, input_tokens, cached_input_tokens, output_tokens, reasoning_output_tokens}`, `turn` `{input, output, thinking, cacheRead, total, calls}` + `turnMs` the turn's own spend + time (antigravity). The attach's `chatStatus` carries the same as `lastUsage` / `totalUsage` / `turnUsage` / `turnMs`. Its antigravity source is agy-wrapper's journal record `{type:'acp', kind:'usage', sessionId, total, turn, last, model, modelFrom 'agy'|'asked', durationSeconds}` (one per turn; src/agy-turn-usage.js) — a `modelFrom 'agy'` model is also a `served-model` op. What a bar shows of it is the harness's `ui.statusBar` row (src/status-bar-facts.js).
**Background tasks (`msg` op=meta, subtype `background-tasks`, design-unknown-records 2026-09-21)**: `{op:'meta', subtype:'background-tasks', data:{tasks:[{id, type, description}]}}` — claude's `system/background_tasks_changed`, a LEVEL signal (the FULL live set, emitted whenever membership changes). The normalizer (the reconcile's ONE owner) SOFT-closes every running BACKGROUNDED task card the set no longer names — by ANY of its keys (a Workflow card's short task id OR its wf_ run id, lane Q verify 2026-09-26) — before emitting it (`taskInfo.status:'finished'`, `closedBy:'level'` — a foreground call is never a member of the set and is never touched; the outcome record that follows overwrites the guess, r3 2026-09-21); the status bar reads its count ("N background tasks") and closes nothing itself; `attached.backgroundTasks` carries the same list for a window that opens mid-run (null = the harness never published one — the card-derived set stands).

**The queue after a RESTART — `queueKnown` + `queue-resync` (2026-09-09)**: a queue publication is a `queue_changed` record on the wrapper's stdout, that stdout is an 800KB head-dropped RING, and that ring is what a restarted server rebuilds the session normalizer from — so after a restart `queueState()` is `[]` **whether the wrapper's queue is empty or holds 25 items, and the two are the same bytes on the wire**. `attached` and `created` therefore carry **`queueKnown`** beside `queue`: `false` = the list is a PLACEHOLDER (this server has never heard this wrapper publish), `true` = a fact. `created` is always `false` (the app-server's queue belongs to the THREAD, so a RESUMED thread hands a brand-new wrapper items it never filled). A payload from before the field simply omits it and the client reads it as `true`. **The client applies the rows either way** — an unknown queue IS the empty list on the wire, and one round trip of no rows beats a row nobody can act on — but suppresses the INFERENCE: only a KNOWN list retires the `Queued` chip of a rendered bubble it does not list. **`queue-resync`** is the answer: a stdin verb (`{type:'queue-resync'}`, `BackendAdapter.formatQueueResync`; the base class REFUSES, so a harness with no queue cannot format it) that makes the wrapper re-publish its queue **including an EMPTY one**, FORCED past `publishQueue`'s own fingerprint dedup — "still empty" is precisely the sentence a deduping publisher cannot say, which is also why the `refreshQueue()` a wrapper runs after a `gone` verdict corrects nobody. It makes **no RPC** (the wrapper re-states what it already believes; an attach may never wait on a wedged app-server), and a resync that lands while the Stop sweep owns the publish is OWED and paid after the sweep's own closing refresh. The attach ASKS only while `queueKnown` is false (self-limiting: the answer flips the flag) and only behind BOTH gates — the harness's `inputModes.queueVerbs` row AND the RUNNING wrapper's `caps.queueResync` sidecar advert. The second gate is load-bearing in an unusual way: the ACP wrapper answers an unknown stdin verb with a VISIBLE error card, so asking an older process would put a red notice in the user's chat on every attach; a wrapper that cannot answer (old build, or REMOTE — its sidecar lives on ITS machine) simply leaves the client on the honest "no rows" state until its own next publication. The answer arrives as an ordinary `queue_changed` through the normal consumer → normalizer → `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue'}` path, so it corrects every attached client at once. **And `queue_op_result {ok:false, reason:'gone'}` REMOVES the row** (client-side) instead of marking it refused: absence is a fact from the process that owns the queue, and a refusal marker there is permanent — the wrapper's own follow-up refresh is fingerprint-deduped away. Every other refusal keeps the row.
**`queueNotifSteer` on the attach payload (B-d963, 2.369.164 batch 7)**: `true` / `false` / `null` — does THIS session's running wrapper fold a `kind:'notification'` peer frame into a running turn (`true`: its advert names a verb table with `steer`), or queue it as a billed turn (`false`: a pre-2.369.63 build — a verb-less sidecar, or an in-band publication with no `verbs`)? `null` = never heard from (no hint). Computed by `notificationSteerOf` from the same sidecar read and in-band proof as `queueSupported`/`queueVerbs`; the client's queue strip shows its restart hint only on `false` and a harness whose notification lane is `steer`. The in-band `meta subtype:'queue'` op answers the same question by carrying `verbs` or not. The delivery ladder's matching refusal (a notification to a BUSY such wrapper) is `{ok:false, lane:'rpc-queue', refused:'wrapper-no-steer', reason}` — the caller stashes it; jobs type it `held.kind:'wrapper-no-steer'`.
**Permission-rule read (`read-permission-rules`, 2026-09-07 — the ONE new stdin verb, READ-ONLY)**: not a ws message — the server writes it to the wrapper's stdin from `GET /api/permission-rules` when the caps row says `permissionRules.liveVerb` AND the RUNNING wrapper adverts `caps.permissionRules` in its sidecar (the 2.361.1/2.364.1 pair; an older wrapper is refused `wrapper-old` with its start time and "Terminate + Resume", never handed a frame it would drop silently). Frame: `{type:'read-permission-rules', requestId}` from `adapter.formatReadPermissionRules` — **both wrappers serve it in the same batch** (design §6 landing discipline). The codex wrapper answers `event_msg {type:'permission_rules', ok:true, requestId, cwd, source:'config/read', config, origins, layers, truncated}` — only the permission-bearing keys travel (`approval_policy, approvals_reviewer, sandbox_mode, sandbox_workspace_write, permissions, default_permissions, include_permissions_instructions`, mirroring `CODEX_PERMISSION_KEYS` in the PURE module and pinned for parity because the wrapper is a shipped single file) plus ONLY this session's own `projects.<cwd>.trust_level`, the layers WITHOUT their config blobs, and a 32 KiB ceiling that says when it capped; the user's model/provider/plugin world and other people's project paths never enter an agent-visible journal. The ACP wrapper answers a TYPED refusal `{kind:'permission_rules', ok:false, requestId, reason:'unsupported-by-protocol', detail, mode, modes}` — ACP v1 has no config-read method, and the one permission fact the protocol DOES carry (the session's live mode) travels with the refusal; it adverts the cap on purpose, because a wrapper that adverts nothing is indistinguishable from one too old to answer and the server would refuse with the wrong reason. Answers are routed by `requestId` (never "the next record wins") and a wrapper that never answers times out into a typed `read-failed` record.
Client → Server: `create` (incl. `fork` flag — S9: a harness whose store forks server-side (opencode: POST /session/:id/fork on the local serve) mints the new id BEFORE the spawn and resumes it; any failure answers `{type:'error', code:'fork-failed', message:'Fork failed: …', reqId}` and no session is spawned; explicit-cwd creates are PREFLIGHTED (2.226.0) — a missing dir refuses with `code:'cwd-missing'`+cwd+hostName unless `recreateCwd:true` (B-7812 danger flow: server mkdirs + arms a one-shot `<vibespace-cwd-notice>` in prompt-context, persisted via session-meta `cwdRecreated`)), `input`, `chat-input`, `permission-response`, `set-permission-mode`, `set-model`, `set-effort`, `set-goal`, `review-start`, `queue-op`, `rename-session`, `size-override`, `interrupt`, `resize`, `attach`, `kill`, `tmux-attach`, `state-set`, `state-resync`, `layout-sync`, `desktop-create`, `desktop-delete`, `desktop-rename`
Server → Client: `created`, `output`, `msg` (normalized: op=create/edit/meta), `subagent-message` (parentToolUseId for tool card status; an ACP sub's frame also carries `subId` and an ACP record as `message` — lane agy-subagent-cards), `streaming-label` (`label`, `kind` — `'compacting'` while a `/compact` turn runs, 2.365.0; null otherwise), `turn-state` (B3 §2.5: `{sessionId, state:'idle'|'running'|'requires_action', authoritative:true}` — the HARNESS's own turn state, pushed on every change; today only claude publishes one, and only when `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS` was on its spawn env. A session that never emits one simply never sends this frame and the client keeps the derived state — `null` is "not reported", never `idle`), `tools-in-progress` (`{sessionId, ids:[tool_use_id]}` — the RESOLVED set of tools the harness says are EXECUTING right now, from claude's `set_in_progress_tool_use_ids` delta records; the client toggles `.chat-tool-inflight`, because a PENDING card is not a RUNNING one. **DORMANT: no harness emits the source record today** — 2.1.257 hands it to a host callback (`onInProgressToolUseIDs`) and it never reaches a stream-json consumer, so `caps.inProgressTools` is false everywhere and this frame is never sent; test-stdout-registry re-measures the live wire every run), `compact-progress` (`{sessionId, event:'hooks_start'|'compact_start'|'compact_end', hookType, hint, result, error}` — card-less like `api_retry`: the spinner label rides `streaming-label`, and this frame is what tells the "Compact now" guidance card to replace its hardcoded 1–2-minute apology with the CLI's real stage, and at the end with the real OUTCOME (`result:'success'` / `error`) rather than reverting to an apology for something that finished. ONE frame shape, and on 2.1.257 exactly ONE producer: the CLI's `system/status` records (`{status:'compacting'}` → compact_start, a `hook_started` inside that stage → hooks_start, `{status:null, compact_result|compact_error}` → compact_end) — which is also the only lane that sees an AUTO compaction; PLUS a terminal `compact_end` with `result:null` whenever the TURN ends (`result`/`compact_boundary`, or the harness's own idle `session_state_changed`) while a compaction is still in flight. `_streamingKind === 'compacting'` is a claim about RIGHT NOW that the client mirrors as a held stage gating the whole guidance card, so EVERY place the server retires that claim says so (one named `retireCompaction`): a PreCompact hook that BLOCKS the compaction emits no outcome record at all, and the ws-handler `/compact` send site sets the kind before the CLI has said anything — before this frame the client kept "Compacting: running <hook> hooks…" forever and every later "Prompt is too long" card lost its rewind-and-retry sentence (2026-09-07). `result:null` on purpose: "ended" is not "succeeded". The declared `compact_progress` record feeds the same frame but has never been observed on our stdout), `page-published` (2.366.0, 2.366.1 also fires on flag changes/removal: `{sessionId, page}` — publish/republish, a visibility or name change, or removal (`page.removed:true`) of a page of this session, by the agent CLI or the file-browser dialog), `goal-updated` (goal/goalStatus/goalElapsed/statusMsg), `exited`, `attached` (includes `messages`, `totalCount`, `isStreaming`, `streamingLabel`, `streamingKind`, `chatStatus`, `taskState`, `goal`, `goalStatus`, `goalElapsed`, `queue`, `queueSupported`, `turnState`, `inProgressTools`), `active-sessions`, `editor-open`, `editor-close`, `effective-size`, `user-state-updated`, `bookmarks-updated`, `settings-updated`, `custom-themes-updated`, `state-sync`, `state-ack`, `state-snapshot`, `layout-sync`, `desktop-updated`, `tasks-updated`, `session-status-updated`, `accounts-updated`, `harness-caps-updated` (S9: `{backend, caps:{fork}}` — a harness capability VERIFIED at runtime, today opencode fork from the serve OpenAPI probed lazily after the first discovery; the client merges it into BACKEND_META caps and re-renders the sidebar; `/api/home harnesses[].caps` carries the same verdict for later loads), `harness-store-updated` (2.369.50: `{backend, parked, parkedKind:'runaway'|'crash'|null, reason}` — a harness STORE that broke; today the opencode serve keeper parking after crashes or after its /proc RUNAWAY guard stopped it. The client stores `reason` on BACKEND_META[backend].storeReason (user actions quote it) and toasts a NEW reason once; `/api/home harnesses[].storeReason` carries the same string for later loads — BOTH from the ONE predicate `storeFailureReason()` in src/server/cli-env.js, which is true only for a PARK: a background service that is simply OFF (the opt-in `opencode-serve` plugin, or the `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0` ops switch) is NOT a broken store and carries no reason, or the red toast would fire on every page load (2026-09-07). Its off-ness rides `/api/home harnesses[].service` instead. Pushed only on a CHANGE of reason, so it is not a heartbeat), `server-notice` (2.226.0 probe→user channel: {key,text,level} → client toast + notification history; USE THIS for any new server-side probe instead of inventing a channel — serverNotice(key,text,{level}) in server.js is key-deduped per boot and keeps undelivered keys for retry), `sysinfo-alert`, `error`. Resume no-convo breaker: a failed `claude --resume` arms a 10-min per-conversation cooldown (2.207.1 anti-bootloop); when the transcript verifiably exists the client pops an unmissable retry-now confirm that bypasses it via `ignoreNoConvo` (2.237.0 — the 2.227.3 retry bar went unnoticed at the window bottom, real report). Session `exited` carries `reason`+`detail` from classifyCliDeath (2.226.0 — known CLI canned errors surfaced in the read-only bar / exited overlay; new death signatures belong in that one classifier), `killed` (2.369.16: reply to the REQUESTER of a `kill` — `{sessionId: <the id the client SENT>, resolvedId: <after the stale-id remap>, ok:true}` after the attached-only `exited` broadcast, or `{ok:false, reason:'not-found'}`; the client's `app.killSession` re-sends an unacknowledged plain kill on every reconnect until one of these lands, 3-minute watchdog disarms), `attach-ack` also carries `{progress:{done,total}|null, queued}` every 10s while the session's first-attach history rebuild is queued/running (2.369.16), `attach-ack` (synchronous proof-of-life before the possibly slow `attached`). **Unknown client→server type (Plugin Ph1, the switch's new `default:`)**: `{ type:'error', code:'unknown-type', message:'Unknown message type: <type>', unknownType, reqId? }` — reqId echoed when the request carried one (so `ws.request` callers fail fast), **never a sessionId** (session-scoped `error` frames flip live windows into the exited/read-only path; an unknown type is not a dead session); server side = rate-limited log (one line per type per 10 min with the running count) + telemetry event `ws-unknown-type`. Before this the type was silently ignored. **`session-vcs` (design-unknown-records, 2026-09-21: `{sessionId, host, kind, branch, cwd, at}` — the claude `vcs_state_changed` fact, pushed by session-brain.noteVcsState from either feed to EVERY connected client (not only the session's attached ones — the explorer that must re-list may be a client's only window); the client re-lists every open File Explorer whose directory is that cwd or under it on the same host; the card's git chip rides `active-sessions` (`vcs`), the timeline row rides session-status history; `attached` also carries `backgroundTasks` = the harness's last published level set or null).**

**Input queue (`queue-op`, 2026-09-06)**: `{type:'queue-op', sessionId, op:'steer'|'remove'|'steer-all', id}` — act on a message the user sent DURING a running turn. Validated against the harness's `backend-caps` `inputModes {queue, steer, queueOps}` row (NEVER a backend id), then formatted by the adapter's `formatQueueOp` and written to the wrapper's stdin. A SECOND gate follows it: the RUNNING wrapper's own `caps.inputQueue` advert (`wrapperCaps()` over the sidecar the wrapper writes) **or** the in-band proof that it has already published a `queue_changed` (a REMOTE wrapper's sidecar lives on ITS machine) — a session spawned before this release passes the caps row and would have its frame dropped silently (2.361.1/2.364.1), so it is refused with "its agent predates the input-queue update … Terminate + Resume". Every refusal answers `{type:'error', code:'queue-op-unsupported', scope:'action', sessionId, error, message}` naming the harness and what it CAN do. **A per-session `error` is a SCOPED refusal (renders in-chat, window untouched) only when it carries `scope:'action'` or one of the client's explicit codes (`input-rejected`, `not-codex-chat`, `queue-op-unsupported`)** — "any code is scoped" is wrong: `ended-during-attach` is coded AND fatal, and must keep taking the view-only rescue + Resume bar. Wrapper → server events (inside the normal record stream): `queued_input {msg_id, turn_id}`, `queue_changed {items:[{id, msgId, preview, ts, kind, from}], turn_id}` (the WHOLE queue on every change — the app-server's own `thread/queue/changed` carries no items, so the wrapper re-`list`s), `queue_op_result {op, id, msg_id, ok, reason, detail}`. The normalizer turns `queue_changed` into `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue', items}` (session state — never a transcript message) and stamps the originating user bubble with `queueState: 'queued'|'steered'|'removed'|null` through the ordinary `edit` op. `attached` and `created` both carry `queue` (the normalizer's `queueState()`) **and `queueSupported`** (attach: the wrapper's sidecar advert OR the normalizer having seen a `queue_changed`; create: always `false` — a just-spawned wrapper has reported nothing yet, and its baseline `queue_changed` turns the controls on moments later), so a reconnecting or newly created window starts from a fact. A `queued_input` from a wrapper that never published a queue falls back to the old system card instead of stamping a chip that could never clear.

**verify-r6 K4: the `permission-response` frame names only the ANSWER** — `approved`, `always`, `optionId`, a question's `answers`; `answerFrame` builds `updatedInput` and `updatedPermissions` from the SERVER's own record of the pending request (`recordOf` + PURE `answerFromRecord`): Allow runs the record's input, Always Allow sends the offer the card drew (`alwaysOfferFor`), a question keeps the record's input plus the client's answers only, an ACP option the request never offered is refused `permission-mismatch`; an older bundle's `permissionUpdates` is read as "always", its contents ignored. **`permission-response` → the CLI (lane L, 2026-09-25)**: `permissionUpdates` (the card's "Always Allow") reaches claude's stdin as `updatedPermissions` inside the allow response (`ClaudeCodeAdapter.buildPermissionResponse`) — the 2.1.281 stdio result schema's own key; the old `permission_updates` was stripped on arrival, so no rule was ever applied. For VibeSpace's own tools the client sends the WIDENED suggestion (`widenSuggestions`, src/agent-tool-rules.js); r2: for a HELD verb or tool the NARROWED one (the exact command line, never `<tool> <verb> *`), and nothing at all for an ask verb (`vibespace-page publish` — the card offers no Always Allow). The spawn flag itself did not change: claude spawns already carried one inline `--settings` JSON, which now also holds `permissions.allow` for those tools (row `claude.allowAgentTools`, default ON).

**A helper's permission ask (lane S1, B-6e95)**: a helper's `can_use_tool` rides the PARENT's stream (`agent_id`, no `parent_tool_use_id`), so its card is answered on the PARENT's stdin with the helper's request id — the frame is the ordinary `permission-response`. A helper View Log window sends its OWN virtual id (`sessionId: 'sub-<tool_use_id>'`); the ws case resolves the live session that holds the request (`src/server/helper-asks.js sessionForAnswer`), and an answer no live session holds answers `{type:'error', scope:'action', code:'permission-unrouted', requestId, error}` (never a silent drop — the card already flipped itself). Server → client: the normalizer's `msg` meta op `{op:'meta', subtype:'pending-asks', data:{asks}}` and the attach payload's `pendingAsks` = every answerable ask (`{kind:'main'|'question'|'helper', requestId, toolName, target, label, msgId, msgIndex, toolUseId, parentToolUseId, agentId?, at}`, oldest first); an Agent tool message gains `helperAsks: [{requestId, agentId, toolUseId, toolName, input, description, suggestions, resolved: null|'allowed'|'denied'|'cancelled', at}]` (edit op `{helperAsks}`). `attach` of a sub-agent view accepts `agentId`: a live view (`sub-<tool_use_id>`) whose buffers are gone falls back to the transcript on disk. For-you items filed for a helper ask carry `origin:'agent'`, `action:{type:'helper-ask', requestId, sessionId}`.

**OpenCode ask cards (S9 remainder, B-eac2)**: `permission-response` gained `via` (+ `host`). A card built from an OpenCode `question` tool declares `via:'opencode-serve'`, and the ws layer answers it on the SERVE's own route instead of a session's stdin — the conversation may have no live process here at all (a stopped conversation opened read-only can still show a pending ask). The card itself stays harness-neutral: it forwards what the record that created it declared, never a backend id. A refusal answers `{type:'error', scope:'action', code:'opencode-question', requestId, error}` — the card flips itself to "answered" optimistically, so silence would be a lie, and `scope:'action'` is load-bearing: without it a session-scoped `error` frame is read as "attach failed" and flips the LIVE window into the read-only Resume bar (the 2.363.1 law — a refusal rejects ONE action, it never condemns the conversation). Server → client `opencode-updated` `{kind:'revert'|'unrevert'|'question-replied'|'question-rejected'|'store', sessionId, requestId?, revert?, host?, reason?}` is the multi-client echo: every browser re-polls the session list and any open window on that conversation says in-line what happened. `kind:'store'` is the LIVE LANE's own invalidation (cache invalidation must NOTIFY): the SSE stream or the sqlite watch saw a change nobody asked about — including one made by a TUI on the same store — and the push is what makes it appear without waiting for that browser's 5s poll. It is COALESCED server-side (`OPENCODE_CHANGE_COALESCE_MS`, 2s) and streamed message parts are deliberately excluded, because each push costs every client one discovery sweep.

**OpenCode serve terminal (S9 remainder piece (c))**: `create` gained `opencodePty:true` — the session's pty is one the OpenCode SERVE owns (`POST /pty` on the serve, streamed over `GET /pty/{id}/connect`) instead of a local dtach spawn. src/server/opencode-pty-bridge.js connects that websocket SERVER-SIDE and hands the session layer the same `{pid,onData,onExit,write,resize,kill}` shim the daemon pipe-session uses, so xterm, input, resize and the buffer file work unchanged. The serve's port, its Basic auth header and its connect ticket NEVER reach the browser.

**`attached.messages` = the TEXT WINDOW (2.369.167, perf lane A)**: the live attach AND the view-only attach ship `normalizer.tailWindow()` — the tail counted in text cards (src/text-window.js; 50–400 records, ≤ 128 KiB past the 50-record floor since perf r1), not tail(50). `totalCount` unchanged. A pure deterministic function of the message list, so a same-epoch re-attach ships the same slab and the client's identical-skip (2.369.2) fires. No protocol field, no capability: an old client renders any N. Sub-agent attaches (`sub-*`) still send their full list.

**`attach {slab: 'floor'|'text'}` (2.369.167, perf r1)**: the web client says which slab it wants — `'floor'` = exactly tail(50) (the pre-lane slab) for a SUSPENDED view (a hidden window's queued re-attach) or a displayed one while any other attach is in flight on the socket (a cold open of N windows, a page reload, a reconnect's displayed windows: only the FIRST of the burst gets the text window); `'text'` (or absent — an older client) = the text window. Both the live and the view-only attach honour it (`tailWindow(attachWindowOpts(data.slab))`, src/text-window.js — the ONE mapping); a held resume (`slab:'held'` on the REPLY) ignores it (no slab). An old server ignores the field (it ships its own window). Additive: no capability gate needed in either direction.

**Resume by seq — `attach {sinceSeq, sinceEpoch, caps}`, `attached {opSeq, replay, slab}`, `lagged`, `msg.seq` (2.369.167, perf lane chunk D — the one wire change)**: every `msg` frame of a session's OWN normalizer (sessionId = the session; a subagent virtual session's frames are not stamped) carries `seq`, a per-session number from the session's op ring (src/op-seq.js; ≤ 2000 frames / ≤ 4 MiB, the exact text sent; reset — never renumbered — when rebuildHistory mints a new `normEpoch`). Client → server: every `attach` the web client sends carries `caps:['op-seq']` (src/lib/ws.js adds it at the wire), and a live ChatView's re-attach adds `sinceSeq` (the last seq it APPLIED) + `sinceEpoch` (the epoch that seq belongs to) — ONLY after it has seen `opSeq` from this server. Server → client: `attached` always carries `opSeq` (the last seq this socket is current to — the capability advert). When `sinceEpoch === normEpoch` and the ring still holds every frame after `sinceSeq`, the reply is `attached {…every live fact of the full payload, opSeq, slab:'held', replay:[the frames after sinceSeq, verbatim]}` with NO `messages` and NO `turnMap`; the client applies the live facts and runs the replay through `_onOp` (nothing rebuilt, compared or fetched). Otherwise (no since, another epoch, a seq outside the ring or never handed out) the full `attached` as before + `opSeq`. The `attach-ack` still precedes both; the reply is written synchronously after the verdict, so a replay is followed by exactly the later live frames. `lagged {sessionId, normEpoch, seq}`: sent ONCE to a client that advertised `op-seq`, has been answered by an `attached`, and whose socket's `bufferedAmount` exceeds 4 MiB when a stamped frame is due; that session's `msg` frames are then skipped for that socket until it re-attaches (seq = the last frame it was handed); telemetry event `ws-lagged` (the queue size). The client answers with `_reattach` (resume by seq). A client that did not advertise the capability is never cut (today's unbounded delivery — it cannot answer). Old client: sends no since, ignores `seq`/`opSeq`/`lagged`. Old server: never sends `opSeq`, so the client never asks. **perf r1: `sinceSeq` is the last seq the view APPLIED with every earlier op applied too — `_onOp` advances it only after the op ran, and an op that throws POISONS it: the view's next attach carries no `sinceSeq` (the server's full rung) and a same-epoch full reply is rebuilt from its slab (`_fullViewReset`), never caught up from `_windowEnd`. No wire change beyond the omission.**

**Virtual session attach**: `attach` with `sessionId` starting with `sub-` routes to subagent handler. `sub-{parentToolUseId}` returns live-buffered messages from parent session's `subagentBuffers`. `sub-agent-{agentId}` loads completed agent's JSONL from disk. Both respond with standard `attached` payload with normalized messages. **An ACP sub (lane agy-subagent-cards, .341): `sub-{subConvId}`** (an Antigravity subagent's own conversation id — the card's «Open timeline», a `conversation://` link and a restored layout name the same window) is served by src/server/acp-subagents.js from the parent's `subagentBuffers` (the journal's `sub` records — `{subId, toolCallId, rec: <an ACP record>}`), re-read from the parent's journal when memory has none (a restart), through an AcpMessageManager — an unknown id answers the empty `attached`. Live virtual sessions receive normalized `msg` ops via per-subagent normalizers. `attach` with `viewOnly:true` loads JSONL history without an active session (for stopped session history viewing).


## Channels — the communication panel (P0a, docs/design-communication-panel.zh.md)

- **Recalls and edits (lane channel-record-revisions, .262):** a message's read shape may carry `recalledAt` (ms the recall was LEARNED; the content stays) and `versions: [{at, text, original?, truncated?}]` (oldest first; `text` = the newest); the agent copy adds `revisionText`. Side kind `rv` `{msg, at, src, editedAt, text, blocks?, truncated?}`; a recall = the `fx` `recalled` flag. The `channels-updated` broadcast may carry `revised: {[convKey]: {[vendorId]: <row>}}`; a conversation row may carry `lastRecalled: true`. `vibespace-channels read <conv> --versions` prints every version. Adapter method `revisionOf(convId, {messageId, threadKey})` → `{state: recalled|edited|same|unknown, editedAt?, text?, blocks?}`, declared by `caps.revisions {recall, edit, how: byid|absent|none}`.

EVERY route takes `host` and passes it down: `hostId` is a PARAMETER, never a
branch (decision 15). v1 serves THIS machine only, and that is a NAMED refusal
(`501 host-not-served`) for anything else — a silent local answer to a
question about another machine is the failure the rule exists to stop.

- **2026-09-26 — THE AGGREGATED IM (design §5 inv 6, §6.2, §6.5, §7.3):**
  there is no `tracked` field and no `/track` route — every conversation of a
  linked account is listed and fetched. The digest's conversation rows are
  SLIM (`key, id, adapterId, adapterLabel, title, kind, participants, lastAt,
  lastText, unread, unlisted, refresh:{every, by}|null, cadence:{seconds,
  tier: hot|warm|cold, source: override|tier|push-safety, paused}, freshness,
  offers, assignment (the EFFECTIVE one, with `source:
  conversation|pattern|account` + `patternLabel`), held, outbox, lane,
  lastError`); identity warnings, grants and the full assignment ride the FULL
  view (`GET /api/channels/:a/:c` → `{conversation, adapter}`). Adapter rows
  add `scheduler {conversations, unread, hot, warm, cold, paused, overridden,
  unlisted, due, lastDiscoveryAt, discovering}`, `budget {unit, limit, spent,
  spentBy:{timer, agent, owner}, exhausted, waiting, resetInSeconds,
  settingKey}` (lane R2 verify: who spent the minute), `lastOkAt` (the last
  GOOD pass — "last sync"; `lastPass` is stamped by a failure too) and
  `backoffUntil` (the retry instant while a failed pass backs off, else null),
  `attachments {files, bytes,
  budgetBytes, fetch}`, `linkedAt`, `assignment` (the account grain),
  `patterns[]` (the rule grain), `auth.renews` + `renewWindowMs` (Lark), and
  `options` AS APPLIED (an adapter's `effectiveOptions`). **`channels-updated`
  is PARTIAL** after a pass: `{partial:true, changedKeys:[…], digest}` carries
  only the changed rows (clients merge in place); a structural change sends
  the whole digest. Reader routes (owner, cookie):
  - `PUT /api/channels/:a/:c/refresh` `{every: 30|60|300|900|'paused'|null}` —
    the owner's override (null = back to automatic), persisted `{every, by,
    at}`, broadcast; 400 `bad-request` outside the set, 404 unknown.
  - `POST /api/channels/:a/:c/refresh` — Refresh now (owner): a REQUEST into the
    account's set, judged and fetched by the account's own fetch loop (r5) →
    `{ok, appended, polledAt}` | `{ok, pending:true}` (unanswered after 30 s —
    the window updates when it lands); 429 `vendor-budget` + `Retry-After`; 429
    `refresh-queue-full` (200 waiting — an agent already at 180 of any origin:
    the owner's reserve, r6; the refusal carries `cap`); 409 `account-changed`;
    503 `stopped`. A refusal is answered the moment the loop judges it (r6);
    an `ok` at ITS fetch (r7) — never the pass's end.
    Inside a vendor back-off the owner's press is honoured ONCE per back-off
    window (one real attempt; its failure grows the wait one step and spends
    the press) and every further press in that window is 429 `backoff` with
    `retryAfterSec` + `backoffUntil` + `Retry-After` (r5); a press that fails
    INTO a back-off answers the same three (r4); concurrent refreshes of one
    conversation, whatever their origin, share ONE fetch (r4/r5) — every other
    refresh origin is refused 429 `backoff` during a back-off (r3).
  - `POST /api/channels/:a/:c/watch` — the open window's heartbeat (every 60
    s; hot for 90 s after the last); a stale conversation is fetched at once —
    never during a vendor back-off (`fetched:false`, r3).
  - `POST /api/channels/:a/:c/older` `{limit?}` → `{ok, records, source:
    local|vendor, fetched, appended?, exhausted, truncated?, refused?}` — the
    local page first, else the vendor page before the oldest stored record
    (`caps.olderHistory`), PREPENDED, never a wake (a spent budget answers the
    local page with `refused:'vendor-budget'`).
  - `GET /api/channels/:a/:c/attachment/:id?msg=<vendorId>[&inline=1]` — cache
    first, else the adapter's `fetchAttachment` (charged); `X-Content-Type-
    Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`,
    `Content-Disposition: attachment` unless png/jpeg/gif/webp with
    `inline=1`; 413-class refusal `too-large` past 100 MB; 429 on the budget.
    **R3 (2026-09-26, design §23.1):** the steps are PURE `fetchVerdict`
    (cache first · a remembered vendor refusal · ours only — `msg` looked up
    directly, however old · fetchable · enabled · a fetch in flight JOINED ·
    the back-off · the budget); `&retry=1` = the person's Retry (skips the
    remembered refusal, never the budget); a vendor `rate-limited` = 429 +
    `Retry-After` (and the account's other pictures wait it out: `backoff`,
    no call); `disabled` = 409; every refusal carries `Cache-Control:
    no-store`; a remembered one says `remembered:true`.
  - `GET /api/channels/search?adapter=<id>&q=` → `{ok, results:[{convId,
    title, record}], truncated, scannedBytes}` (≤ 64 MB read, ≤ 100 results).
  - **R4 (2026-09-27) — ACCESS and NOTIFICATION, two operations, access first
    (design §24):** at every grain `PUT …/access {access:[{principal:{kind:
    'agent'|'group', id, name?}, authority:'draft'|'send'}]}` (GRANT ACCESS —
    `[]` removes everyone, and with them their notifications) and `PUT
    …/watchers {watchers:[{principal, notify:'wake'|'digest', mode:'all'|
    'filtered', filter?|filterId?, digestMinutes?, dailyWakeCap?,
    receiptWake?, estimateAtSet?}]}` (NOTIFY — every principal must already
    hold access at that grain, else `400 watcher-needs-access {principal,
    index}`), where the grain is `/api/channels/adapters/:id/…` (the account),
    `/api/channels/adapters/:id/patterns/:pid/…` (a rule) or
    `/api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/…` (a conversation). A new rule is `POST
    /api/channels/adapters/:id/patterns {pattern, access[, watchers]}`; `PUT
    …/patterns/:pid {pattern?, access?, watchers?}`; `DELETE …/patterns/:pid`
    removes the rule with its rows. Answers `{ok, grain, access:[…], watchers:
    […], assignment}` (each watcher with its own `stats {hits7d, wakes24h,
    wakes7d, lastRefusal}`). Refusals `400 {error, code: bad-access|bad-watcher|
    duplicate-principal|watcher-needs-access|too-many-rows|bad-filter|
    bad-pattern|no-such-filter, why?, rule?, principal?, index?}` / `409
    authority-capped`. An inline filter's id is `f-<grain>|<kind:id>`, minted
    before validation. `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id {policy:'direct'|
    'review'|null}` sets the ACCOUNT's own sending policy (what a composed
    message reads). The digest's adapter row carries `accountGrain` (+ its
    alias `assignment`), `patterns[]` (each `{…, access, watchers}`) and
    `policy`; a conversation row carries `access` + `watchers` (each principal
    once, its finest grain) beside the compatibility `assignment`; the FULL view
    carries `own {access, watchers}` and `inherits {account, patterns}`.
    **EVERY WRITER OF A WAKE WATCH ASKS THE PERSON VERDICT (lane
    wake-approve-card, acceptance round 2):** a `…/watchers` PUT, a rule's
    `POST …/patterns` / `PUT …/patterns/:pid` carrying `watchers`, and a
    pre-split `…/assignment` (its watcher row is a wake) whose rows hold a
    WAKE (`deliveryModeOf` = wake: `delivery:'wake'`, a row that never said,
    a digest) ask routes/vault.js `personVerdict` first — any bearer ⇒ 403
    `{code:'human_only'}`, not this page ⇒ 403 `{code:'cross_site'}`, nothing
    written. A write with no wake row (a next-turn row, a removal, `[]`) is
    as before; an agent's own road to a wake is its ask (`POST
    /api/agent/channels/watch` `--mode wake` ⇒ a `wr-…` request).
    **A WAKE ASK HAS A LIFETIME (acceptance round 3):** the `wr-…` request
    and its item carry `expiresAt` (filing + 7 days, `WATCH_REQUEST_TTL_MS`);
    the answer says it (`expiresAt`, `request.expiresAt`; `watch --show`'s
    `request.expiresAt`); at the bound (2 waiting) `watch-request-limit`
    carries `oldest: {id, target, at, expiresAt}` and names it in `error`;
    past its end the engine expires it (request `expired`, item «expired ·
    the agent can ask again») and a decide answers `{code:'expired'}`.
    **EVERY WRITE TO A GRAIN THAT HOLDS A WAKE (acceptance round 4):** the
    gate keys on the GRAIN, not the body — each of the 13 grain-writing
    routes (the account's / a rule's / a conversation's `…/access`,
    `…/watchers`, `…/assignment`; `POST …/patterns`, `PUT` / `DELETE
    …/patterns/:pid`, `…/patterns/:pid/access`; a conversation's `…/filter`
    and `…/reach`) names the grain its PATH writes and asks the engine
    `wakeHeldAt(adapterId, grain)` (a wake row stored on it; a conversation's
    filter also when any wake row names it). Held — or the body would write
    a wake row — ⇒ THE person verdict: any bearer ⇒ 403 `{code:'human_only',
    wakeHeld:true}` («this grain holds a wake…»), not this page ⇒ 403
    `{code:'cross_site', wakeHeld:true}`; a rule's `pattern` or `watchExcept`
    alone, an access list, a removal included. A grain with no wake answers
    as before. The decide route answers an ask past its end — before the
    minute sweep or after it — 409 `{code:'expired', expiredAt, error: 'this
    request expired on <date> — the agent can ask again'}` (was 500 / 409
    bad-state).
    **«HELD» IS READ BY THE READER THAT FIRES (round 5):** `wakeHeldAt` reads
    the grain through the engine's ONE reader (`readGrainHolder` → PURE
    `F.grainAsFired` — the reader `F.effectiveGrants` fires with: a rule read
    with the account's access rows + grants, a conversation with every grain
    above it) and counts EVERY wake row stored on it, active or INERT (its
    principal's access gone — a reach or an access row that gives it back is
    a wake write) → `{held, rows, inert, why}`. The grain writer (`setGrain`)
    and the dialogs' lists read the same way: a write keeps every stored row
    it does not name (an assignment removed — `clear` — takes them too).
    `{kind:'path', convId: slug}` = a webhook path: held when its
    conversation, the account or any rule of the account holds a wake.
    r8: the cookie-free completion's refusal to the REMOTE peer is
    `403 {code: 'pair-wake-held', error: <one fixed sentence>}` — never the
    hub's agent names, titles or rules (they go to the owner's journal); the
    join judges its path's hold before anything is posted, and r9: AGAIN
    at the write (after the fetch, the path and the peer written with no
    await between) — a wake set while the frame was out refuses the pair.
    **A POINTER HIDES (round 3):** an access request's item (`open-channel`,
    a `closer: 'hide'` row — and the Outbox's pointer) closed by the person
    (`POST /api/user-todos/:id`, resolve-many) is stored `dismissed` /
    `resolvedBy:'hidden'` — never «done»; the request stays open; `POST
    /api/agent/channels/request` on a request still open answers `already`
    + `resurfaced: true` when its hidden item came back.
    **mirror-193 (2026-09-27): every `…/access` and `…/watchers` PUT (and a
    rule's `PUT …/patterns/:pid`) takes `base` = the STAMP of the two lists the
    writer drew them from (PURE `F.grainStamp` over `{access, watchers}` — each
    row's principal + STORED authority / `updatedAt`, sorted; a view row's
    `authorityStored` counts, a wake never moves it); a grain that moved since
    answers `409 {code:'grain-changed', error}` and NOTHING is written (the
    Grant access… / Notify… dialogs re-open on the lists as they stand). No
    `base` = unconditional, as before (agents, scripts). `GET
    /api/channels/adapters/:id/view` → `{adapter}` = the digest's adapter view
    of ONE account read fresh (`404 no-such-adapter`) — the account and rule
    dialogs draw from it, never from the panel's broadcast-fed copy.**
  - THE COMPATIBILITY WRITE (pre-R4): `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id/assignment` `{assignment|null}` (the
    account grain) · `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/patterns` `{pattern,
    assignment}` · `PUT|DELETE /api/channels/adapters/:id/patterns/:pid` (the
    rule grain) — one principal ⇒ one access row + one watcher row, replacing
    both lists · `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/estimate` `{scope, pattern?,
    filter?, notify?, digestMinutes?, dailyWakeCap?, principal?}` → `{estimate:{…,
    conversations, covered, sampled}, expectedWakesPerDay}` (a bounded read of
    the matching conversations' last 7 days). A FILTERED account / pattern
    assignment carries its filter INLINE (`assignment.filter`) — the engine
    mints `f-account-<adapterId>` / `f-pattern-<id>` before validating (hotfix
    2026-09-26). Refusals: `400 {error, code: bad-assignment|bad-filter|
    bad-pattern|no-such-filter, why, rule?}` — `why` is the validator's closed
    code (`F.ASSIGN_REFUSALS`, or a `validateFilter`/`validatePattern` code),
    `rule` the refused rule's kind; the client words `why`, never `error`.
- `GET /api/channels/adapters[?host=]` — the ACCOUNTS only, `{adapters:[{id, kind, builtin, enabled}]}` from the engine's records (B-df40 part 2, 2.369.202): the Settings window's "an account of this vendor is linked" fact, read once per open and only when a row asks; never the digest (which carries every conversation).
- `GET /api/channels[?host=]` — the index DIGEST: adapters (with their
  resolved lane), conversations (title/kind/participants/lastAt/unread; `tracked` until 2026-09-26),
  each conversation's resolved `convCaps`, its `offers` (read / send-as-user /
  send-as-bot, each `{offered, why}`), its `identityWarning`
  `{level, marking, verbatim}` and its `freshness` claim
  `{kind, state, seconds, why?}` — `state:'off'` with `why`
  (`untracked` / `adapter-disabled` / the scan resolver's own reason) for a row
  nothing will ever fetch (r3), rendered "not polling" / "not scanning" — plus
  `unreadTotal`. Each conversation's `lane` is `{via, why, source}` as the
  resolver answered it, and on a scan lane `source` is exactly what the ingest
  was gated on (r3): `source:null` means nothing was ingested. **Never message bodies, and
  never a composed human SENTENCE**: this payload is broadcast to every client
  at once while the language is per DEVICE, so the client renders the words
  from the structure (`channelCaps.freshnessText` / `identityWarningText`).
  Each adapter's `auth` is `{state: connected|expired|unknown, why, expiresAt}`
  — a RESOLUTION, never a stub. **r3 (agent groups):** each adapter row carries
  `sendStartsTurn` (a send there wakes an agent = a billed turn — the module's
  declaration), and the digest carries `quarantined:[{file, to, why, at,
  blocked}]` — every store file set aside (or BLOCKED: `to:null`) at boot,
  on the first screen.
- `GET /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId[?host=]` — one conversation's summary
  plus its adapter row (what the window's context bar shows). 404 with a reason.
  **§25 (2026-09-27):** `conversation.title` of an adapter declaring
  `caps.titleForm: 'subject'` (Gmail) is `cleanSubject`ed (the digest rows and
  search hits too; the index keeps the vendor's string); the adapter row adds
  `sendForm` (`draft` | `direct` — the composer's one line) and `sendGrant`
  (`{scopes, missing, console}` or null — the read-only line's console step).
  **inc-muk9jj0j-rel3:** `conversation.convCaps` / `offers` are re-judged ON
  READ when the cached verdict predates the account's last credential change
  (`auth.scopesAt` / `lastAuthAt`) — the send half from the held scopes
  (the adapter's PURE `sendCapsOf`), else `stale`; a consent / rebind /
  disconnect persists that for the whole account before its one whole
  `channels-updated` digest.
- `GET /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/messages?before=&beforeId=&limit=` —
  ONE page of normalized `ChannelRecord`s, oldest-first by `(at, vendorId)`,
