  strictly before the BOUNDARY RECORD `(before, beforeId)`. `limit` is clamped
  to 200. **The boundary is a PAIR, not an instant**: `at` is not unique (a
  Lark burst shares a millisecond, Gmail's `internalDate` is second-derived)
  and paging on it alone stranded every record of such a group at or after a
  page boundary. `beforeId` is that record's `vendorId`, which invariant 2
  makes unique per conversation; omitting it is still terminating but lossy.
  **The reader seeks as far back as the writer keeps (r3)**: it used to read
  only the newest 2 MiB, so a page boundary past that answered `[]` for ever
  while retention kept the records; it now walks earlier windows until the
  page is full or byte 0 is reached.
  **§25 (2026-09-27):** a record may carry `blocks` (the typed render tree —
  schema in src/channel-record.js); for an adapter declaring
  `caps.render: 'blocks'` a record stored BEFORE its tree existed is served
  WITH one (the module's `blocksOf`, read time — the log is never rewritten);
  `POST …/older` answers the same way. The AGENT read (`/api/agent/channels/…`
  via `readFor`) strips `blocks` — agents read `text`.
- **lane channel-threads (2026-09-28) — THREADS + REACTIONS (owner routes, cookie; every refusal `{error, code, retryAfterSec?, why?}`):**
  every record served by `messages` / `older` / the thread route carries its derived `place` (`{kind, quote:{of, author,
  text ≤ 120, loaded, external}, thread:{key, kind, isRoot, count, lastAt, separate, walked, walkedAt}}` — `kind` =
  THE classifier `placeKindOf`: plain / quote / topic-root / topic-reply / topic-quote; `thread` ONLY for a topic, a
  quote (a Lark reply without reply_in_thread) never carries one — quote-vs-topic, 2026-09-28) and its
  folded `reactions` (`[{key, glyph, label, count, mine, by ≤ 20 [{id, name}], byTruncated, customImage}]`, reaction
  ids never served). `GET …/:adapterId/:convId/thread/:msg?limit&before&beforeId` → `{ok, thread:{key, kind, root,
  count, lastAt, participants, walked, walkedAt, separate}, records:[root, …replies], exhausted}` — the LOCAL fold,
  never a vendor call (a message the log does not hold: `code:'thread-not-loaded'`, `records:[]`, `walked:false`; a
  QUOTE — a message in a reply chain, or the message quotes answer: `code:'not-a-thread'`, `records:[]`) ·
  `POST …/thread/:msg/refresh` → `{ok, appended, walked:true, key}` — the thread WALK (drain rule 20a: one flight,
  `thread-floor` 429 + Retry-After, `vendor-budget` / `backoff` 429, `not-supported` 501 where replies ride the
  listing, `disabled`, 409 `not-a-thread` for a quote — refused before any vendor call) · `POST …/thread/:msg/older {before, beforeId, limit}` — the rule-19 shape over the thread
  (`older-floor` = `refused` on an `ok` answer — never the thread's start); verify r3: past the local start the walk
  goes from the newest PAST what is held (depth = held + a page), `fetched` = what it brought; `vendorHasNoOlder`
  (+ `source:'memory'` when remembered — 0 calls) at the thread's start, `olderBeyondReach` when the adapter's own
  bound (Lark: the newest 200) stopped it while the vendor held more · `GET …/reactions?ids=<≤ 50>` → `{ok, reactions:{[vendorId]: list}, asOf:{[vendorId]: ms}}` (local)
  · `POST …/reactions/refresh {ids ≤ 20}` → `{ok, asked, joined, refused:[{id, code, rule, retryAfterMs}], floorMs,
  ttlMs, perMinute}` (rule 20b: `reactions-floor` per id, the ceiling / budget / back-off by name; 409
  `reactions-scope-not-granted` + `scopes` for the whole batch) · `POST …/messages/:msg/reactions {key}` / `DELETE
  …/messages/:msg/reactions/:key` → `{ok, reactions}` (as the user, one vendor call, never retried; 409
  `react-not-available` + `why`, 400 `bad-emoji`, 409 `already-reacted` / `reaction-cap` / `not-reactable` /
  `reaction-not-mine`) · `GET /api/channels/:adapterId/emoji-set` → `{ok, keys:[{key, glyph, label, custom}], quick,
  custom, at, replaces}` (6 h cache; 501 `not-supported`) · `GET /api/channels/:adapterId/emoji/:key?inline=1` — a
  custom emoji's picture through the account's cache (the key judged by its alphabet before any path; the
  attachment route's headers) · `POST …/send` and `…/propose` take `inThread:true` (with `replyTo`; 400 `bad-proposal`
  `why:'inThread'`, 409 `topic-forbidden`) and (2026-09-28) `placement` (the pane sends `thread`; 409
  `placement-not-offered` + `why` / `offered`, before anything exists) — every outbox view carries `placement` +
  `placementText` read through the alias (a proposal stored before the enum: `inThread` ⇒ thread, a `replyTo` ⇒ quote) · `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id {reactionPolicy:'propose'|'direct'|'off'}`
  · **lane lark-threads (2026-10-01):** `PATCH /api/channels/:adapterId/authors/:id {alias}` — THE OWNER'S NAME FOR AN AUTHOR
  (the VibeSpace 备注; owner-only: an agent bearer `vsst_` / `jbt_` is refused 403 `agent-forbidden` before the engine;
  `alias` a string through the name door, ≤ 80, `''` / `null` clears it) → `{ok, adapterId, authorId, alias|null}`;
  400 `bad-request` (no `alias`, an author id over 256 / with a control character), 404 `not-found` (no such account);
  stored in data/channels/aliases.json; ONE `channels-updated` carrying `authors: {[adapterId]: {[authorId]: {alias}}}`
  (each window re-spells that author's heads in place). Every record any read serves carries `author.display` (the
  owner's name › the vendor's way: the nickname, else the name, then `(department)` / `(job title)` per the setting
  `channels.larkNameField`), `author.vendorDisplay`, `author.alias` when set, `author.alt` {enName, nickname, jobTitle,
  department} and `author.external` when known — `author.name` stays the vendor's; a `channels-updated` `threads` entry
  also carries `walked` + `separate`; the feed view's `counters` add `threadHits`, `missingFetched`, `missingRefused`,
  `missingOther`, `rechecks`, `recheckWidened`; `grants` may hold `{what:'people', scopes:['contact:contact.base:readonly'], …}`;
  `POST …/refresh` (the owner's press) also re-lists the chat's newest page where thread replies are listed separately
  (drain rule 22b; never within 60 s of the previous press)
  · `GET /api/channels/:a/:c` adds `conversation.offers.{threadReply, react, unreact, readReactions}`,
  `conversation.threads {mode, read, listing, placements}` (lane reaction-hover: `placements` = PURE `placementsOf` of the adapter's caps row — the window's message bar offers Quote / Reply in thread only where declared), `conversation.reactionCaps`; the digest's adapter view adds
  `reactionsGrant {scopes, missing, console}`, `reactionPolicy`, `reactions {read, add, perMinute, listedThisMinute,
  calls60s}`. **The `channels-updated` broadcast** may carry `patches: {[convKey]: {[vendorId]: {reactions}}}` (≤ 50
  per conversation, the FOLDED result), `rereadReactions: [convKey]` past that bound, and `threads: {[convKey]:
  {[threadKey]: {count, lastAt, root}}}` — the window applies them in place and fetches nothing. Settings:
  `channels.reactionsPerMin` (20), `channels.reactionsTtlMin` (10), `channels.threadFloorSec` (60).
- `POST /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/read` `{at?}` — mark read; the engine
  re-derives `unread` from the log rather than trusting a counter, and with no
  `at` the instant is the NEWEST RECORD's — for future-dated records (with
  `now()` a future-dated vendor record keeps `unread` above zero for ever) AND
  for past-stamped ones (r3: the r2 spelling was `max(now(), newest.at)`, so
  for every real adapter a message stamped before the mark but fetched after
  it was silently marked read). With no record at all the mark is left where
  it was. **404 on an id this instance does not hold** — a route may not mint
  an index row — and a mark that changed nothing broadcasts nothing.
- ~~`POST /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/track` `{tracked}`~~ — REMOVED
  2026-09-26 (the aggregated IM; see the block above). Historically: the opt-in that
  decided whether anything was ingested at all. Tracking refreshes that
  conversation's `convCaps` (one of its three named refresh triggers),
  BROADCASTS the persisted change (r3 — it used to rely on the pass it kicks,
  which a spent request budget returns from before notifying), and then kicks
  a pass. 404 on an id nobody has discovered.
  **r6 (主开发: «every door that can re-activate an inert row is a wake door»):** the account grain and a rule HOLD the stored wake
  rows BELOW them whose state their access decides (channels-access `wakesBelow` — an inert row a grant there makes live, a live
  row that lives by that access alone); a NEW rule (`POST …/patterns`) is held when its `access` + `pattern` would make an inert
  row live (the route hands the gate the body's proposal — `ruleGrain` — the grain itself still the path's). The 403 NAMES what
  holds the grain: `{error: '… — held: Worker's wake on «Ops» is inert until a person re-grants', code, wakeHeld: true, heldBy:
  'watchers' | 'filter' | 'below' | 'rule' | 'account' | 'adapter', held}`. A write that changes a stored wake row's state says so:
  `setGrain` / `setReach` answers carry `wakes: {reactivated: [{grain, id, principal, who, where, words}], madeInert, said}`.
  `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id {enabled: true}` asks the gate for every wake stored on the account (`{kind: 'adapter'}`). The
  gate reads THE ONE WIRING's engine (src/server/channels-wiring.js) — never an argument — and an engine that does not own the
  account holds it (fail closed). `POST /api/channels/reach-requests/:id/:verdict` also decides a HELD DIGEST (`wr-…` of kind
  `held`, its For-you item `channel-held-digest`): approve = Deliver `{ok, delivered, n, carried?, why?}` (a refusal of the ladder
  keeps the hits pending, said; `why: 'nothing-left'` when its conversations are gone), deny = Discard `{ok, discarded}` — r8: `n` /
  `discarded` = exactly the card's count (the held hits live in the request's own store, never the live cap; `carried` = the
  block also took the watcher's newer hits); the answer's `request` never echoes the records. `GET /api/agent/channels/watches`
  rows carry `inert: '<words>'` for an inert row — r8: only on a grain the agent may read (a conversation it cannot read, an inert
  account / rule row, is not listed). **r8:** the 403's `held` / the `— held: …` words go to a caller that is never the person,
  through THE belt (`agentNames`: a conversation's title and a principal's name each a line piece); the account access route hands
  the gate its whole new `access` list, and a row whose reach that list cannot change (it only ADDS another principal) does not
  hold the write. **r9:** the gate judges the rows the write will STORE (`accessVerdict` — setGrain's normaliser: a group's `target`
  dropped), and the 200's `wakes.reactivated` / `madeInert` rows carry only belted `words` (no raw `who` / `where`). A held decide
  answers `skipped: k` for rows that were not records; a removed watcher RESOLVES its held card (`discarded`, fact `{why: 'watch-removed', n}`).
  **r10:** a held decision ends only when its RULE is gone — no stored row names the watcher (`watch-removed`) or the row stands inert,
  its grant revoked (`access-removed`) — never because it is not in effect now (a stopped target, a member not running). A Deliver its
  rule cannot take now answers **409 `not-in-effect`** `{why: 'not-running' | 'not-admitted', agent, n}` and keeps the rows (Discard
  still drops them); one whose rule is gone answers **409 `rule-gone`** `{why, n}` and the decision is ended as above.
  **r11:** a rule's reach is part of the rule — a decision held for a rule that no longer matches ANY of its held conversations
  (the rule edited, the conversation renamed) is ended as gone, `why: 'not-covered'` («the rule no longer covers this conversation —
  N messages discarded»), at the rule's edit or by a Deliver that finds it so (409 `rule-gone` `{why: 'not-covered', n}`).
  **r12:** the reach is judged PER CONVERSATION by the live prune's own reading (is the watcher still woken there through ANY
  grain — effectiveFor), never the held rule's pattern alone: a conversation the agent is still woken for (the account, another
  rule) stays held; when only SOME went, they drop by name and the decision stays open (its card «… · the rule no longer covers
  k of m conversations — d held messages dropped»), and every answer of the decide route carries **`dropped`** `{n, k, of, why,
  agent}` (the 200 beside `n` / `discarded`, the 409 `not-in-effect`); a 409 `rule-gone` for a digest whose every conversation
  went adds **`convs`** (m) and its `n` counts the part dropped before. Deliver sends each conversation on the road that wakes it now.
  **r13:** ONE Deliver press = ONE block (one billed turn): the held scope's, or the first account / rule grain that wakes one of its
  conversations now, carrying every one of them. The 200 adds **`pending`** (this watcher's rows still in the decision's conversations
  after the block — only when there are any) and, for a block the ladder refused, **`stashed: true`** («kept for its next turn») or
  **`held: true`** (still pending). A conversation dropped earlier that a later boot re-holds is live again (`dropped` is a row fact).
  The 409 `not-in-effect` `error` ends with the dropped part («· the rule no longer covers 1 of 2 conversations — 30 held messages dropped»).
  **r15:** the block HOLDS every chain it reads (each of its conversations' own chain, then every scope chain its conversations are
  woken through — the engine's `billedWake({roads})`), so no window or conversation flush reads the same rows while it waits on the
  ladder. **`n` = what went** (released by this press and no longer pending — carried or stashed). What the press did NOT get to the
  agent stays in the request: rows of a refused block that were not stashed come back, and with no scope road left ONE conversation
  goes per press, the rest are never released. Then the request is **open again** (`request.status: 'open'`), its card stays open
  with the count left, and the 200 says **`stillHeld`** (messages still held) + **`stillHeldConvs`** (in how many conversations);
  a refused block that was not stashed answers `delivered: false`, `n: 0`, `note: 'not delivered now — the messages stay held on the
  card'`. r13's `pending` and the refused `held: true` are gone. The record closes `delivered` only once nothing is left, with `n` =
  what went; a decision ENDED by the system counts every part it dropped over its life (a re-held conversation's earlier part included).
  **r16:** what comes back onto the card is written before the block lets go of its chains (no window reads it in between); a press a
  stop cut short is reopened at the next boot — its own card, `status: 'open'`, the count left — or closed `delivered` when it went;
  `skipped` counts each unreadable entry once per request.

- **P1a — the adapter's own consent flow and options** (design §10.1, §12.4,
  §13; every failure answers `{error, code, detail}` with a status BY CODE:
  `409 needs-credentials` = the row's integration resolves to none — the
  client opens the Integrations card FIRST; `501 not-supported` = an
  undeclared capability / a kind with no consent flow; `401 auth-expired`;
  `502` = a vendor refusal; `404 no-such-adapter`).
  - **r4 (2.369.165, docs/design-integrations-per-account.zh.md §2.4) — the
    account dialog's sign-in, the storage consent block's shapes, for an
    account that does NOT exist yet.** A CLIENT CHOICE in any body below is
    `credentialKey` (`cluster:<presetKey>` | `custom`), `clientPreset:'<k>'`
    (the storage dialog's spelling), `credential {appId, appSecret}` or
    `clientId` + `clientSecret` (a custom client — sealed under
    `.channels-key` at once, echoed nowhere); refusals `400 unknown-credential`
    / `invalid-client` (`detail.errors` = field → rule, never a value) /
    `own-retired`. **2.369.195 (design §2.7): `fromMount: <mountId>` is a
    fourth choice** — a storage mount's OWN client (a Drive / Gmail mount's
    custom Google client), copied SERVER-SIDE: decrypted with `.mounts-key`,
    re-sealed under `.channels-key`, the account then an ordinary `custom`
    one; never beside another choice (`400 ambiguous-client` — on start /
    connect (the `{flowId}` body too) / reauthorize, judged before anything
    is read; another vendor's mount is refused before its secret is opened);
    refusals
    `404 mount-gone` / `409 mount-no-client` / `400 mount-client-vendor` /
    `400 mount-client-unsupported` (Lark) / `409 mount-secret-undecryptable`
    / `503 no-mounts`; an agent's `vsst_` / `jbt_` bearer naming it is
    `403 agent-forbidden` (start / connect / reauthorize); a `fromMount`
    present but not a mount id (a number / object / array / `''`) is
    `400 bad-request` by name (`null` = absent; verify r2). At Connect
    `{flowId, fromMount:<another mount>}` is compared BY ID on the key-less
    read first (`flow-client-mismatch` with nothing decrypted; the same id
    ⇒ the secrets are compared); a bad `options` field is refused BEFORE
    the flow is taken (the sign-in survives). One audit line
    `{kind:'auth', op:'client-from-mount', mountId, by:'user'}` per copy.
    **Verify r3: every verb that borrowed a mount's client ANSWERS
    `fromMount: {mountId, name, clientIdPrefix}`** (start / connect — the
    `{flowId}` body too, from the flow's copy — / reauthorize; bounded, never
    a secret): the choice is BY MOUNT and the copy is the mount's client at
    the sign-in's START — a mount edited between the list (whose
    `clientIdPrefix` is a hint at list time) and the pick signs in under its
    CURRENT client, and this field says which; Connect naming the same mount
    lands the flow's copy, never a re-read. The consent machine bounds pending
    sign-ins: at most 32 run at once (begin #33 ends the oldest — verify r4:
    its status `cancelled: 'over-limit'`, its `error` the machine's own
    sentence, never `superseded`, which means "a newer sign-in of THIS
    account stands"); an ended flow's records retire 30 min after its end.
    **Verify r4:** the two ends that are the machine's own act — the 10-min
    timeout and the cap — are SAID: a pending flow's `status` reads
    `{running:false, done:true, ok:false, error}` with the sentence, an
    account's own flow (re-authorize / a rebind) writes `lastAuthError` +
    `lastAuthAt` and broadcasts (the Re-authorize dialog's watcher reads it);
    a pending record whose flow ended without finishing answers Connect
    `409 flow-failed` ("the sign-in ended before it finished: …"), never
    `flow-not-done`. A self-identifying agent bearer is `403 agent-forbidden`
    on EVERY verb that begins or completes a consent, whatever the body names
    (start / connect / reauthorize / oauth/callback / auth/finish — a sign-in
    is the owner's; with sign-in on the cookie gate answers 401 first).
    - verify r5: a self-identifying agent bearer (`vsst_`/`jbt_`) is `403 agent-forbidden` on EVERY consent and account verb
      (start / status / callback / connect / reauthorize / auth/finish / auth/cancel / PUT :id / duplicate / disconnect /
      DELETE :id / config / mount-clients) and on the storage side's ten lending routes (`GET /api/mounts/:id/config`,
      gdrive-auth/* + gmail-auth/* start/status/callback/cancel, `:id/drive-token`); with sign-in on the cookie gate
      answers 401 first — the agent surface is /api/agent/channels/*.
    - `GET /api/channels/oauth/mount-clients?kind=gmail` (2.369.195, owner
      only — an agent bearer is `403 agent-forbidden`) → `{kind, vendor,
      clients:[{mountId, name, type, email, clientIdPrefix}]}`: the top-level
      storage mounts holding their own client of the vendor this type signs in
      with (derived: a `drive-presets`-delegating row ⇒ `google`; Lark ⇒
      `vendor:null`, `[]`). No secret, no ciphertext, only the id's first 12
      characters. Declared before `GET /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId`.
    - `POST /api/channels/oauth/start` `{kind|backend, <choice>, options?, road?}` →
      `{ok, flowId, url, flow, kind, credentialKey}` (the consent under THAT
      client; nothing is created). lane pod-oauth-callback (.344): on an instance with an https address and a
      browser elsewhere (the POST's Origin) a Lark / Gmail consent is `flow.mode:'public'` — `redirect_uri` =
      `<address>/api/channels/oauth/cb/<kind>`, nothing listens (src/consent-road.js); `road:'loopback'` = the
      person's last resort (the adapter's loopback + the paste-back). A new account under a preset that names an
      option (`presets[].facts`, Lark's `brand`) takes it unless `options` names it. Re-authorize
      (`POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/reauthorize`) carries `road` the same way.
    - `GET /api/channels/oauth/status?flowId=` (omitted = the latest) →
      `{flowId, kind, credentialKey, running, done, ok, error, user, flow,
      token}` — `token` is the FLOW ID once signed in (the handle the shared
      block writes into its field; never a credential); `404 no-flow`.
    - `POST /api/channels/oauth/callback` `{url, flowId?}` — the paste-back →
      `{ok, flowId, user, token}`; a foreign `state` is `400`.
    - `POST /api/channels/oauth/narrow` `{flowId?}` — owner ruling 2026-09-28: THE ONE NARROWING RETRY of a running
      sign-in whose consent names optional scopes (Lark `im:message.reactions:read`; Lark refused it on its own page,
      error 20027) → `{ok, flowId, flow}` — the SAME flow, `flow.consentUrl` without them, `flow.narrowed` names them;
      `409 already-narrowed` (once per flow) / `409 nothing-optional`, `404 no-flow`. Every flow view carries
      `optional` + `narrowed`.
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:kind/connect` `{flowId, name?, options?,
    <choice>?}` — r4: CREATES the account from a finished sign-in (its client
    and token; the flow is taken once): `404 no-flow`, `409 flow-not-done` /
    `flow-failed`, `400 flow-client-mismatch` (a body naming another client) /
    `flow-kind-mismatch`. Answers `{adapter, flow:null}`.
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:kind/connect` `{credentialKey?, newAccount?}`
    — THE ACCOUNT MODEL (2026-09-22; r4: also any `<choice>`, `name`,
    `options`): mints a NEW account of the kind (the
    first record's id IS the kind, every further one `<kind>:<8 hex>`) when
    `newAccount:true` or no record of the kind exists, stamped with
    `credentialKey` = `cluster:<presetKey>` | `own` (validated against what
    the integration offers RIGHT NOW — `400 unknown-credential` naming it and
    the offered keys otherwise; omitted = the integration's current pick) and
    begins its consent flow under THAT credential. Without `newAccount` on a
    kind that already has an account it is the P1a path: the FIRST account is
    re-authorized, a choice there judged exactly as by `/reauthorize`.
    Answers `{adapter, flow}` where `flow` is `{flowId, mode,
    running, consentUrl, redirectUri, port, listening, refusal, pasteBack:true,
    startedAt, expiresAt}` — never the flow's `state`. A fixed-mode port
    already held answers `refusal:{code:'port-busy'}` on the flow and the
    flow keeps running on paste-back. `409 needs-credentials` when the
    account's key resolves to none (a withdrawn preset says `preset-gone`
    naming it — never another client).
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/reauthorize` `{<choice>?}` —
    re-begins ONE account's consent flow by its adapter id (never by kind).
    r4, the mount semantics: no client named / the same client ⇒ its consent
    (a same-id custom client with a new secret replaces the secret first); a
    DIFFERENT client IS a re-authorization under it — `{adapter, flow,
    rebind:true, credentialKey}`, the account keeping its old client AND
    token until that consent lands, then both replaced in one write (a failed
    consent changes nothing and sets `lastAuthError`). The pre-r4 `400
    credential-bound` is gone. A legacy record with no key is stamped first
    by its token's own evidence, else the integration's current pick.
    `404 no-such-adapter`.
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/duplicate` `{name?}` → `{adapter}` — r4
    D4: a NEW, UNAUTHORIZED account carrying exactly the engine's declared
    `DUPLICATE_FIELDS` (kind, the client choice — a custom secret RE-SEALED —,
    the declared options, the push claim, the sender line); never the token,
    tracked conversations, assignments, reach grants or the log; named
    `name` or `<label> (copy)`. It consents on its own (`/reauthorize`).
  - `DELETE /api/channels/adapters/:id` — r4 D5 REMOVE: `409
    account-referenced {error, detail:{refs:[{kind:'access' (R4; 'assignment' pre-R4), key, convId,
    title, principal} | {kind:'reach', grantId, principal, level, origin} |
    {kind:'outbox', id, key, state}]}}` while anything points at the account
    (assignments of its conversations, `scope.kind==='adapter'` reach grants,
    unsettled outbox proposals — agent groups never); otherwise `{ok,
    removed:true, id}` (record + index rows; logs stay). Builtin `400`.
  - `GET /api/channels/adapters/:id/config` — r4 D3, OWNER-ONLY (cookie auth;
    never broadcast, never logged, no agent route): `{config: {id, kind,
    label, credentialKey, client: {appId, appSecret, undecryptable} | null,
    presets, clientFields, options, push, senderHonestyLine}}` — the Edit
    dialog's prefill, the custom secret in the clear (the storage `GET
    /api/mounts/:id/config` rule). The ONLY channel answer carrying a secret.
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/auth/finish` `{url}` — PASTE-BACK: the
    redirect URL the user's browser landed on; `400 auth-failed` with the
    reason (a `state` mismatch says "restart the flow"); `501 no-flow` when
    nothing is running.
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/auth/cancel` — `{ok, cancelled}`.
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/auth/narrow` — the same narrowing retry for the account's RUNNING re-authorize →
    `{ok, flow}`; `404 no-flow` when none runs. The consent that lands records what it dropped on the account
    (`auth.refusedScopes`; a refresh keeps it, the next consent replaces it) and the adapter view's `reactionsGrant`
    = `{scopes, missing, refused, console, wanted}` (`wanted` = the declared `reactions` option is not `off`).
  - `POST /api/channels/adapters/:id/disconnect` — drops the token, cancels a
    running flow, retracts the failure item; r4: the record, its client, its
    conversations, assignments and grants stay for EVERY account (the pre-r4
    "a further account is removed" special case is gone — REMOVE is `DELETE`)
    and it ignores references. `{ok}`.
  - `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id` `{enabled?, options?}` — enable/disable
    and/or the adapter's DECLARED options (an undeclared key or a value
    outside its `choices` is a `400` naming it; `''` restores the default; a
    change re-runs discovery). r4: also `{label}` (rename) and `{credential:
    {appId, appSecret}}` — a custom client's SECRET replaced in place when
    the id is the account's own (answer `customClient` masked); another id or
    a preset is `409 client-change-needs-reauth` (use `/reauthorize`).
  - r4 digest additions (`GET /api/channels`, `channels-updated`): each
    adapter row carries `bindsPerAccount`, `presets [{key,label}]`,
    `clientHint`, `clientFields {id, secret}` (declarations), `customClient
    {appId, secretSet, secretMasked, undecryptable} | null`; the digest adds
    `kinds` = every connectable type `{kind, label, integration,
    bindsPerAccount, presets, clientHint, clientFields, setup, optionsSchema,
    receive, sendAs, pushOptIn}` (the type-first account dialog's input).
    `credentials` for an account-bound row lists presets only (`own`
    retired).
  - **P1b:** `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id` also takes `{push: {enabled?,
    claimedExclusive?}}` — the push switch (an opt-in lane, Gmail, is OFF
    until `enabled:true`) and the exclusivity DECLARATION (`exclusive` /
    `shared` / `unknown`, a closed set — anything else is a `400` naming it;
    `400 no-push-lane` on an adapter that declares none). Sending
    `claimedExclusive` is a RE-DECLARATION: it clears a demotion, zeroes the
    miss counters and retries the lane once. Answers `{ok, push}`.
- **P2 — assign / filter / estimate** (design §7, §10.2; every failure
  `{error, code}` with a status BY CODE: `404 not-found` on an id nobody
  discovered — never a minted row; `400 bad-assignment` / `bad-filter` /
  `no-such-filter` / `filter-in-use`; `409 authority-capped` when
  `authority:'send'` is asked for where either cap forbids it, the reason in
  `error`):
  - `PUT /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/assignment` `{assignment, estimateAtSet?}`
    — `assignment` = `{principal:{kind:'agent'|'group', id, name?}, mode:
    'all'|'filtered', filterId?, notify:'wake'|'digest', digestMinutes?,
    authority:'draft'|'send', dailyWakeCap?}` (defaults all / wake / 30 min /
    draft / 40); `assignment:null` unassigns (removes ONLY the
    `origin:'assignment'` reach grant). Answers `{ok, assignment}` AS IT READS
    (`authority` clamped, `authorityClamped`, `authorityWhy`, `authorityStored`).
  - `PUT /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/filter` `{filter, estimate?}` —
    `filter` = `{name?, match:'any'|'every', rules:[{kind, …}]}` over the
    CLOSED rule set; `filter:null` clears it (refused `filter-in-use` while a
    filtered assignment names it). Answers `{ok, filter}` with the saved
    `estimateAtSet`.
  - `POST /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/estimate` `{filter}` (`null` = all
    messages) — runs the PURE estimator SERVER-SIDE over the stored log
    (≤ 5000 records; the client never sees the corpus). Answers `{ok,
    estimate:{matched, total, matchedPerDay, totalPerDay, windowDays,
    sampled, truncated}}`.
  - The digest (`GET /api/channels` / `channels-updated`) carries per
    conversation `assignment` (as it reads), `filter`, `stats` `{hits7d,
    msgs7d, wakes24h, wakes7d, lastWake:{at, n, ok, lane, why, whys, cid},
    pending, lastRefusal}`, `authorityCaps` `{offersSend, sendWhy,
    policyRequiresReview}` and `wakeLatency` `{lane:'push'|'poll'|'scan'|
    'reconcile', seconds, coalesceSeconds, kick?, source?, why?}` — STRUCTURE;
    the editor says the words.
  The digest (`GET /api/channels`) now also carries `available` (connectable
  kinds not yet connected: `{kind, label, integration, credential:{source,
  why, missing, clusterLabel, credentialKey}, credentials, receive, sendAs}`) and per adapter
  `connectable`, `integration`, `credential`, `flow`, `lastAuthError`,
  `lastAuthAt`, `failureItem {id, code, at}`, `options`, `optionsSchema` and
  `auth.user`/`auth.scopes`/`auth.credentialSource` — never a token.
  **The account model (2026-09-22):** per adapter `credentialKey`
  (`cluster:<presetKey>` | `own` | null = legacy, follows the row's pick),
  `credentialLabel` (a preset's env label; `own` is worded by the client),
  `credential` = the facts resolved FOR THAT KEY, `credentials` = every
  credential a further account of this kind may bind to (`[{key, label,
  source, presetKey}]`, key + label only — the wizard shows a credential step
  only when more than one is offered), `credentialDefault` (the row's CURRENT
  pick = what a further account is bound to unless the wizard says otherwise —
  never this account's own key; c2), `auth.credentialKey` and
  `auth.tokenHeld` (does the record hold a token — false = never
  authenticated / disconnected, the ONE state the Connect verb may re-bind);
  `available[]` carries the same `credentials` list and `credentialDefault`.
  **P1b:** each adapter row carries `push` (`null` when it declares no push
  lane): `{enabled, optIn, transport, claimedExclusive, state, lastStateWhy,
  lastEventAt, demotedAt, demotedWhy, demoted{missed,total,rate,threshold,at},
  redeclaredAt, missRate{rate,total,missed,enough,threshold}, contentSince,
  droppedUntracked}` — the STRUCTURE; the sentence is the client's
  (`channelCaps.pushLaneText`). Never the raw sample batches.

WS: `channels-updated` `{changed:[convId], digest}` — ONE broadcast per
ingest pass (or per mutation), carrying the recomputed RESULT so a client
repaints without a fetch. Never one per message.

**P3 — outbox · policy · reach (design §8, §9):** every answer is typed;
`answer3` maps `not-found` → 404, `send-not-available` / `bad-state` / `reconcile-not-available` → 409,
malformed input → 400, `failed` / `unknown` → 502.
  - `GET /api/channels/outbox[?conv=<adapterId>/<convId>&limit=]` — the
    proposals (newest first, ≤ 500) + `awaitingTotal` / `unknownTotal`; each
    proposal is `proposalView`: `{id, adapterId, convId, key, title, text,
    originalText, replyTo, why, attachments, draftedBy:{kind, id, name},
    authority, at, updatedAt, state, policy:{mode, reasons, detail}, sendAs,
    identity:{sentAs, marking, where, text}, identityWarning, ttlAt,
    awaitingSince, edited, approvedBy, reason, result:{vendorMessageId, at,
    sentAs, lane}, receipt, receiptDelivery, history, canDecide}`.
    **a3 i18n (2026-09-18): STRUCTURE beside every sentence.** `proposalView`
    also carries `outcome` (`{kind: boot|threw|lost|send-not-available|not-landed|refused|expired|rejected, code, detail, userReason}`,
    PURE `channel-policy.outcomeOf`) and `reconcileWhyCode` — `reason` stays
    the English contract string agents read; the card words `outcome`. The
    digest's conversation row carries `adapterLabel` and
    `assignment.authorityWhyCap` (`{code: policy-review|send-not-offered, sendWhy}`);
    an adapter row carries `builtin` + `auth.self` (the built-in row's login
    is this instance, `auth.user` null) and `credential.whyCode/whyParams`;
    `lane.why`, `auth.why`, `lastPass.code`, a wake's `lane`/`refused` are
    CODES the client words (`channel-caps` authWhyText / laneWhyText /
    errorCodeText / deliveryLaneText / wakeRefusalText). Route failures are
    worded by `code` on the client (`src/lib/channel-words.js routeErrorText`).
  - `POST /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/propose` `{text, replyTo?, why?,
    attachments?}` — the USER's own draft (authority `send`), judged by the
    same policy + guards → `{ok, proposal, decision}`; 409
    `send-not-available` creates nothing. **r3 WAKE GATE (this route, /send
    and /approve):** where the adapter's send STARTS A TURN (`sendStartsTurn`)
    and the send goes out now, `expectWakes:1` must echo it — else 409
    `wake-count-mismatch` `{wakes:1}`, nothing created — and with auth OFF
    the owner's pace applies (the groups' persisted ledger, shared with the
    group routes) — 429 `rate-floor`, nothing created (an approve's proposal
    stays `awaiting-approval`). A blocked store answers 503 `store-blocked`.
  - **g3 (design §22.2 ①):** `POST /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/send`
    `{text, replyTo?, attachments?}` — the OWNER'S OWN MESSAGE, the
    composer's Send: sent at once AS THE USER, no policy and no guard
    (`decision {mode:'direct', reasons:[], detail:{ownMessage:true}}`), the
    same outbox record + audit (propose → attempt → outcome) + the never-
    re-sent `unknown`; same answer shape as /propose. 409
    `send-not-available` + `why` (the send-as-user reason) where only the bot
    identity or nothing is offered. The digest's conversation rows also carry
    `lastText` (the newest ingested record's text, one line, ≤ 160 chars).
  - verify-r6 (the outbox): **`approve` must carry `shown`** = the PURE `shownDigest` of the card it was pressed on (400 `shown-required` without it, 409 `changed-since-shown` when the proposal is no longer that record — nothing moves, nothing is sent); a reply's `replyTo` must be a message STORED for that same conversation (propose `bad-proposal {why: reply-anchor}`; at approval / send `send-not-available {why: reply-anchor-gone}`); an adapter declaring `caps.replyEnvelope` (Gmail) resolves To / Cc / Subject at propose — stored, shown, sent verbatim (`reply-envelope-missing` for a proposal from before); direction controls / zero-width characters are refused by name in to / cc / subject / replyTo.
  - `POST /api/channels/outbox/:id/approve` `{text?, expectWakes?}` (a different text =
    approve edited) — re-resolves `convCaps` first; `{ok:true, proposal}` when
    sent, else `{ok:false, code:'send-not-available'|'failed'|'unknown',
    error, proposal}`. r3: a proposal's view carries `wakes` (0|1); approving
    one that wakes needs `expectWakes: wakes` (409 `wake-count-mismatch`)
    and, auth off, is paced (429 `rate-floor`) — both leave it awaiting.
    **2026-09-27:** `{deliver?: 'next-turn'|'wake-now'}` = how the drafting
    agent hears of it (default `next-turn`: the receipt rides its next
    message; `wake-now`: one billed turn, spendReason `channel-receipt`, ONE per
    proposal, counted in `expectWakes`; auth off a floored wake-now is
    downgraded — `proposal.receiptChoicePaced` says why); any other value 400
    `bad-request`. A withdrawn proposal answers 409 `bad-state` + `state:'withdrawn'`.
  - `POST /api/channels/outbox/:id/reject` `{reason?, deliver?, expectWakes?}` →
    `{ok, proposal}` (the same `deliver` choice; 409 `bad-state` + `state`).
  - Proposal views (2026-09-27) carry `receiptChoice`, `receiptWake` (the ONE
    wake row), `receiptDelivery {…, choice, verdict, gone?}`, `receiptDrainedAt`
    (the stash drained into the agent's next message), `withdrawal {by, why, at}`,
    `replaces` / `replacedBy`; the card words the fate with PURE `receiptFateOf`.
    **Verify r2:** + `receiptDrainedHow` (`'drained'` | `'reconciled'` — a boot
    guess, no time), `receiptEvictedAt` + `receiptEvictedHeld` (the ladder's
    cap dropped the stored receipt unread — fate `evicted`, "Not delivered —
    <agent>'s queue was full; the receipt is kept here"). `reconcile` is
    serialized per proposal (a second concurrent press is 409 `bad-state`).
  - **P4:** `POST /api/channels/outbox/:id/reconcile` — a PERSON asks whether
    a LOST send landed (the only way out of `unknown`) → `{ok:true, resolved,
    state, answer:'landed'|'not-landed'|'unknown', reason?, proposal}`
    (`resolved:false` = still unknown, the ask counted); 409
    `reconcile-not-available` when the adapter's declared idempotency cannot
    answer (`none`), 409 `bad-state` on anything but an unknown proposal.
    `proposalView` gained `honestyLine` (the exact line that will/was
    appended, else null), `canReconcile` / `reconcileWhy`, `attemptAt`,
    `wire:{text, honestyLine, at}`, `sendHandle`, `reconcile:{n, lastAt,
    lastBy, lastAnswer, lastWhy, resolvedAt}`, `result.observed` /
    `result.honestyLine` / `result.reconciled`; the receipt gained
    `reconciled` + `honestyLine`.
  - **P4:** `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id` also takes `{senderHonestyLine:
    true|false|null}` (null = follow `channels.senderHonestyLine`) →
    `{ok, senderHonestyLine:{record, effective}}`; the digest row carries
    `senderHonestyLine` (null on a read-only adapter) and `identityObserved`.
  - `PUT /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/policy` `{mode:'direct'|'review'|null}`
    (null = the adapter's default) → `{ok, policy:{mode, source, declared}}`.
  - `PUT /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/reach` `{principal:{kind:'agent'|
    'group'|'everyone', id, name?}, level:'visible'|'requestable'|null}` (lane
    everyone-principal: `everyone` = All agents, stored `{kind:'everyone',
    id:'*', name:null}`; the same principal is accepted by the access /
    watchers writes — an All watcher fans out, a cap per running conversation;
    `POST /api/agent/channels/request` on a conversation All may see answers
    `{ok, already:true, level, via:'everyone'}`, no card) — writes (or with
    null removes) the USER-origin grant only → `{ok, reach:{entries:[{id,
    principal, scope, level, origin, at, by}], requests}}`.
  - `POST /api/channels/reach-requests/:id/approve|deny` → `{ok, request,
    grant}` (approve = exactly ONE visible grant, origin `request`; a `wr-…`
    wake request's approve writes exactly its frozen watch row). A PERSON's
    door (lane wake-approve-card, .360 — routes/vault.js `personVerdict`,
    asked before the engine): any bearer (vsst_ / jbt_ / vsak_) or a body
    `token` ⇒ 403 `{code:'human_only'}`; a write that is not this page (no
    browser — a bare curl on a sign-in-off instance —, another site, a
    foreign Origin) ⇒ 403 `{code:'cross_site'}`. No agent route decides one.
    Acceptance round 2: the decision carries the verdict's MARK — the request
    `decidedBy: 'person'` + `decidedAt`, its For-you item `resolvedFact:
    {decidedBy:'person', decidedAt}` (the only record whose stamp says «by
    you»; any other decider's record keeps «approved»).
    Round 4: a `wr-…` ask past its end (either side of the minute sweep) ⇒
    409 `{code:'expired', expiredAt}` — the page words it «This request
    expired on {date} — the agent can ask again».
  - The digest carries per conversation `policy` `{mode, source, declared}`,
    `reach` `{entries, requests}`, `outbox` `{awaiting, unknown, latestAt}`,
    the assignment's `receiptWake`, and at the top `awaitingTotal`.

WS: `channel-outbox-updated` `{changed:[proposalId], outbox}` — one
broadcast per proposal-store change carrying the recomputed outbox view; the
inline section and the Outbox window repaint from it, never from a poll.

### The channel witness (§26, B-099e — from the chat to the conversation)

Every `/api/agent/channels/*` handler that touches a conversation (read /
refresh / reply / compose / search / status `<id>` / request — `list` is
exempt: it reads none) records a TOUCH on the calling session AFTER an `ok`
answer (test-architecture §63). A touch = `{id, op, adapterId, convId|null,
proposalId|null, title, account, kind, count, at}` (strings frame-inert; a
composed message has `convId: null`). Both reads are cookie-only — an agent's
`vsst_` / `jbt_` bearer is **403 `agent_forbidden`**:

- `GET /api/channel-touches?sessionId=<webui id>` → `{ok, sessionId, live,
  touches:[touch…], turnAt}` — the session's ring (newest 200, oldest first)
  and its current turn's start; a stopped / unknown id answers `live:false`
  with an empty ring; no / a malformed sessionId is 400.
- `GET /api/channels/:adapterId/:convId/touches` → `{ok, key, touches:[{sessionId,
  name, op, at, n}]}` — every LIVE session that touched the conversation, its
  strongest op (reply > compose > read > search > refresh > request > status),
  newest first (the window's "Drafted by …").

WS: `channel-touch` `{sessionId, touches:[touch…], turnAt}` — a NEW touch is
broadcast at once, one broadcast per agent call (a search = one broadcast,
≤ 20 touches); a repeat FOLDED into the previous touch is re-sent under ITS
id in the session's next COALESCED broadcast (≤ one per 250 ms — verify r2:
200 reads in 2 s were 200 broadcasts; clients upsert by id). The ring is persisted in the session meta as `channelTouches`
and restored at boot.

### What waits for an agent (2026-09-27 — the stash made visible)

- **Session fact `stash`** on every `active-sessions` row: `{count, oldestAt,
  items:[{kind, label, n}]}` or null — both stashes of the conversation (the
  delivery ladder's `data/msg-stash.json` + the jobs engine's
  `job-notifications.json`); `kind` ∈ channel-receipt | channel | job | handback |
  window-request | notice | peer (`label` = the peer's name). Re-published
  (debounced 300 ms) on every stash write and drain. Carried-only in the client
  (LIVE_SESSION_FACTS `stash`).
- `POST /api/sessions/:id/stash/hand-over` (cookie only; an agent's `vsst_` /
  `jbt_` bearer → **403 `agent_forbidden`**) → `{ok, delivered, lane,
  remaining}` — the whole stash as ONE message through the delivery ladder
  (`kind:'notification'`, spend reason **`stash-handover`**), then exactly the
  delivered entries drained. Refusals: 404 `no_session`, 409 `no_conversation` /
  `nothing_waiting` / `unreachable` / `in_flight` (verify 2026-09-27: ONE
  hand-over in flight per conversation — a second click is refused, never a
  second turn) / `held_for_next_turn` (the process predates mid-turn
  notifications; the notices ride its next prompt) (the stash is untouched;
  while a hand-over runs its entries are CLAIMED — the next-turn injection's
  drain leaves them), 429 `spend_refused` `{why, retryAfter, identity, cap}` +
  `Retry-After` (untouched). The `active-sessions` row's `stash` fact
  (`{count, oldestAt, items:[{kind, label, n}], billed, inFlight, held,
  reachable}`) carries `billed` = would a hand-over now open a billed turn (the
  harness's lane: a claude inbox is charged mid-turn; a codex steer mid-turn is
  free) — the strip's word. **Verify r2 (2026-09-27):** `inFlight` = a
  hand-over for this conversation is on its way (every client's button waits);
  `held` = how many entries ONE hand-over would leave behind (its 12 KiB / 30
  entry budget); `reachable` = the harness has a live lane at all (`false` for a
  stash-only lane: no button). The success body is `{ok, id, delivered, held,
  lane, steered, remaining}` (`id` = `ho-…`, the same id the delivered frame
  names); every refusal that started a hand-over carries `id`; `in_flight`
  carries the running one's `{id, since}`. The claim is on disk (`ho` on the
  entries of `data/msg-stash.json` / `job-notifications.json`), the server's
  shutdown waits for a hand-over in flight, and a stamp left by a killed
  process is released at the next boot.
  **Verify r3:** `restarting` (503) = the server's shutdown shut the door before
  its wait for hand-overs in flight — the notices keep waiting; the restore of a
  handed-back frame requires the frame's EXACT text (a tag quoted in a peer's
  message restores nothing).
  **Verify r4:** the delivered-hand-over memory the restore reads is on disk
  (`data/stash-handover.json`, 24 h, ≤ 50 records) — a restart between the
  delivery and the wrapper's echo restores the originals too; the echo's own
  `kind` is judged (a `peer` frame never restores); `delivered` on the result
  counts every claimed entry (the cap no longer evicts a claimed one).

### Slack (design 012, lane S1)

- `POST /api/channels/oauth/start {kind:'slack'}` → `{flow:{mode:'paste', consentUrl: <Slack's manifest link>, listening:false}}`;
  `POST /api/channels/oauth/callback {flowId, url:<the pasted xoxp- token>}` (a bot / app token or junk = 400 `forbidden`,
  `detail.why:'not-a-user-token'`, the flow stays open); Connect as usual. Re-authorize = a new paste through `…/auth/finish`.
- design 017 (two pastes): the callback takes `box: 'config' | 'user'` (which step's box). `{box:'config', url:<xoxe.xoxp-…>}`
  → ONE `apps.manifest.create` → `{ok:true, step:'created', stepFacts:{appId, appName, teamId, teamName, installUrl},
  token:null}` — the flow keeps running; `GET …/oauth/status` then carries `flow.step` + `flow.stepFacts`; Connect before
  step 3 = 409 `flow-not-done`. A token in the wrong box or a refused create = 400 with `detail.code` = the closed why
  (`user-token-wrong-box`, `config-token-wrong-box`, `refresh-token-not-config`, `not-a-config-token`,
  `config-token-expired`, `config-token-scope`, `app-create-refused`, `rate-limited`, `transport`).
- design 018 (one Slack app per workspace): `POST …/oauth/start` and `…/reauthorize` read the browser's `Origin` (else
  Host) into the Slack state; `clientPreset: 'paste'` names the per-person rung (a Slack account with no client key);
  `clientPreset: '<preset>'` / `'custom'` + `clientId`/`clientSecret` start Slack's consent (`flow.mode: 'public'`,
  `url` = slack.com/oauth/v2/authorize). `GET /api/channels/oauth/cb/:kind?code&state[&error]` = the landing (NO cookie —
  2.369.214: src/auth.js exempts exactly this GET, another browser profile is the normal case; the signed state is the
  credential; ≤ 30 landings / address / minute, then 429 html): an html page in ONE language — the request's Accept-Language
  (zh* → zh, ja* → ja, else en; `Vary: Accept-Language`; lane oauth-landing-words, .348) — 200 connected / declined, 400 refused
  (`bad-shape`, `bad-hmac`, `wrong-kind`, `wrong-flow`, `expired`, `timed-out`, `superseded`, `cancelled`, `over-limit`, `used`,
  `failed`), each in its own words; an address is printed inside `<!--email_off-->` with `@` as `&#64;`; never echoes the code or the state. The POST
  callback of a `public` flow takes the landed address, its query, or the bare code a relay page showed (a foreign
  state = 400 `state-mismatch`). A preset without `relayUrl` on an http origin = refused `no-https`.
  lane pod-oauth-callback (.344): Lark (`/cb/lark`) and Gmail (`/cb/gmail`) land here too when their consent took the
  route — the page (src/consent-road.js `landingHtml`) closes its tab once connected: the route's CSP is
  `default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; script-src 'sha256-<CLOSE_SCRIPT>'` (that ONE script, for every kind).
  The kinds digest (`GET /api/channels`, `kinds[]`) names `consentRoute` (the route a custom app registers; null
  without an https address or for a paste type) and `presets[].facts` (`{brand}` — a preset's declared readable values).
  lane oauth-relay-road (.344): a Lark / Gmail consent rides a RELAY PAGE first — `flow.mode:'public'`, `flow.road:'relay'`
  (`'route'` for the instance's own route, null for a loopback), `redirect_uri` = the relay page, the state's clear `u` =
  the dialog POST's Origin (the browser's own origin, its port included). The relay of a COMPANY PRESET account = its
  own `relayUrl` (a Lark JSON entry / `VIBESPACE_INTEGRATION_LARK_RELAYURL`; a `VIBESPACE_GDRIVE_CLIENTS` entry's
  `relayUrl`) › the fleet's (the presets' `fleet.json`, the integration store's `fleetRelayUrl()` — lane
  oauth-relay-one-source, 2.369.348; the env `VIBESPACE_OAUTH_RELAY_URL` one more release) › none — never the setting
  (addendum 1); of a person's own client = the fleet's › the setting `channels.larkRelayUrl` / `channels.gmailRelayUrl` (default
  `https://problemfactory.github.io/vibespace/slack/`; `''` = none). It is ridden only when that page sends this origin
  back (slack-manifest `relayTargetVerdict`: a private origin — Tailscale's 100.64/10 and *.ts.net included — or, for the
  cluster's own page, this instance's own https host). The kinds digest adds `relayPages` — `[{url, who: 'fleet' |
  'page'}]`, the addresses a person's own app of the type registers once ([] for a paste type) — and, for those types,
  `presets[].relayPage` (a preset's own page › the fleet's; `null` = it has none: the route / the loopback). A Slack
  preset with no `relayUrl` rides the fleet's too.
  Lane oauth-setup-instructions (2.369.346): the kinds digest adds `oauthSetup` for a type whose registry row declares
  `redirect` (Lark, Gmail, Slack; null otherwise) — `{base, relayPages, presets: [{key, relayPage, clientType}]}`:
  this instance's https base (`''` = none), a person's own app's relay pages (the fleet's, the type's relay setting —
  Slack's too), each company preset's page (its own › the fleet's, `null` = none) and, for Google, its `clientType`
  (`'web'` | `'desktop'` | `null`) — the setup card's facts (PURE src/oauth-setup.js). Gmail's account option
  `clientType` (`''` | `'web'` | `'desktop'`; a `VIBESPACE_GDRIVE_CLIENTS` / gdrive-clients.json entry's `clientType` is
  a preset fact, `presets[].facts.clientType`, and a new account's default) decides the road: `consentRoad({…,
  clientType})` — `'desktop'` ⇒ the loopback on any port (`why: 'desktop-client'`, never the relay or the route),
  `'web'` ⇒ relay › route › the FIXED loopback (`fixed: true`; Gmail's consent row `mode: ['ephemeral','fixed','public']`,
  `callbackUrl` = the registry row's `redirect.loopback`). The registry rows' `redirect` block = `{console {path,
  links}, refusal, create, clientTypes, loopback, also, selfRegisters, vendor}` (checkRow).
  The fleet admin (vibespace-admin ≥ 2.11.0) serves the same static page at `GET /relay/` (+ `/relay/relay.js`; `/relay`
  → 308 `/relay/` with the query byte for byte), `data-allow` = its `VIBE_DOMAIN`; it reads, logs and calls nothing.
- The account view adds `policyModes` (`['review']` for Slack), `retention` (`purge-on-remove`) and `setup {probes, at}`.
- `PUT …/policy {mode:'direct'}` on a Slack conversation or account = `{ok:false, code:'bad-policy', why:'mode-not-offered'}`.
- A Slack proposal carries `prepared {mentions:[{name,id}], notifies, plain, at}` and `audience {kind: dm|private|public|external,
  orgs, members, title}`; `propose` refuses `why:'mention-ambiguous'` (`ambiguous:[names]`) and `why:'too-long'` (> 4 000).
- lane slack-search (2.369.259): the Slack row declares `caps.search = {via:'query', scope:'search:read', pageSize:20,
  pagesPerPress:2, perMin:10, cost:1, snippet:true, context:'around', match:'tokens', adds:'unsaved'}` — `GET
  /api/channels/search/full?adapter=<slack id>&q=` (a press: ≤ 2 `search.messages` pages; `page=<token>` = Slack's page
  number '2'…'100') and `GET /api/channels/:id/:convId/around?msg=<ts>&at=` (the channel at the hit, or a reply's thread;
  nothing stored) answer for Slack (were `not-supported`). No search:read held = `{ok:false, code:'needs-scope'}` with zero
  requests; Slack's 429 = `{ok:false, code:'backoff', retryAfterSec}` after ONE request (the method held for its Retry-After).

## Integrations & keys (P0b, docs/design-communication-panel.zh.md §14)

**a3 i18n (2026-09-18):** `publicView` carries `whyCode`/`whyParams` beside
`why` and `clusterWhyCode`/`clusterWhyParams` beside `clusterWhy` (the closed
set: no-values · no-preset · preset-gone{key} · ambiguous{n,prefer} ·
undecryptable{fields} · store-unreadable · rebound{from,to} · no-store ·
lookup-failed — PURE `integration-registry.credentialWhyText` words them),
plus `usedBy` (a declared phrase; `consumers` stays for tooling and leaves
the card). Every declared `label` / `help` / `callbackNote` / prerequisite /
`test.describe` / `test.caveat` / `wiredIn` is an i18n KEY (`i18nKey(…)` in
the registry; the client renders `t(key)`); `TRIM_NOTE` is said once per card.

All behind the cookie auth. Every body is `publicView(id)` — THE masked view
(`src/server/integration-store.js`) — and **no route ever returns a secret
field's plaintext**: not GET, and there is no "reveal". A set secret is
`masked: {field: '••••' + last 4}` (the tail only when the value is ≥ 12
chars). Every failure answers `{error, code, detail}` with a real status so
the client can toast it (`fetchJson` never throws).

- **r4 (2.369.165): an account-bound row (`bindsPerAccount`: fake / lark /
  gmail) is NOT a card** — `GET /api/integrations` leaves it out (the list is
  the six browser key rows) and GET one / PUT / POST test / DELETE on it
  answer `404 binds-per-account` BY NAME ("… is chosen per account where the
  account is added (Communication panel → Connect an account)"); its client
  is chosen in the account dialog (`/api/channels/oauth/*`, above). An
  unknown id stays `404 unknown-integration`.
- `GET /api/integrations` — `{integrations: [publicView…], storeError}`. Each
  view: `{id, label, fields (declarations only: key/label/secret/required/
  placeholder/help), setup {callbackUrl, callbackNote, prerequisites} | null,
  source: 'user'|'cluster'|'none', why, clusterKey (the preset IN EFFECT),
  savedClusterKey (the selector the user saved), clusterLabel,
  clusterAvailable, clusterWhy, clusterOptions [{key,label}] (key + label
  ONLY — never a preset's secret), delegate {multi, prefer} | null, fromEnv,
  set {field: bool}, masked {secretField: mask|null}, values {nonSecretField},
  missing [requiredFieldKeys], hasOwnValues, testedAt, lastOk, lastError,
  testKind, testDescribe, testCaveat, testButton, consumers, wiredIn, docs,
  storeError}`. `storeError` is `{code, message}` or null — `store-unreadable`
  (data/integrations.json exists but cannot be read; every write below is
  refused with 500 of the same code until it can) outranks the key-file codes
  (`key-unreadable` / `key-malformed`), and a PER-ROW `values-undecryptable`
  `{code, message: <the remedy>, fields}` (r3) says THIS row's stored values
  cannot be opened with the current key file — such a row answers
  `source:'none'` with `why` naming the fields, is NEVER handed to the
  cluster default, and reports `hasOwnValues: true` (a fact about the
  record: Clear my keys is the way out; the list's top-level `storeError`
  stays about the store / key file); a `cluster` row's `why` is non-null
  exactly when its saved default was re-keyed to the instance's only one
  (r2, the non-delegating radio's boolean intent).
- `GET /api/integrations/:id` — `{integration: publicView}`; 404 on an unknown
  row.
- `PUT /api/integrations/:id` — ONE verb, three payload shapes, each routed to
  ITS function: `{values: {field: value}}` → `setIntegration` (omitted =
  untouched, `''` = cleared, else trimmed + validated FIRST — 400
  `invalid-values` with `detail: {field: why}`); `{use: 'cluster'}` →
  `useClusterDefault` (409 `no-cluster-default` BY NAME when the env offers
  none); `{clusterKey: 'key'|null}` → `setClusterKey` (the delegating row's
  dropdown / a multi-default row's picker; stores the SELECTOR only and drops
  the user's own values; 400 `no-such-preset` listing what IS available).
  Answers `{ok:true, integration: publicView}`.
- `POST /api/integrations/:id/test` — the human's click, bounded (15 s), one in
  flight per id, dispatched to the runner the row's CONSUMER registered; the
  store constructs no vendor request. Answers `{ok, error, detail, testedAt,
  caveat, kind, integration}`; a row whose consumer has not landed answers
  501 `not-wired` naming the phase (`detail.wiredIn`) and records nothing.
- `DELETE /api/integrations/:id` — "drop my keys": `clearUserValues`, ALWAYS
  allowed, lands wherever precedence lands (cluster default or none).
  `{ok:true, integration}`. (r2: "always" is a statement about precedence,
  not about the file — PUT, POST test and DELETE alike answer 500
  `store-unreadable` while data/integrations.json cannot be read, because
  a write may not start from a state that is not the file's.)

WS: `integrations-updated` `{id, why: 'set'|'cluster-key'|'use-cluster'|
'clear'|'test', integration: publicView}` — ONE broadcast per write, carrying
the recomputed masked view so every client repaints that one card without a
fetch; it can carry nothing but the masked view by construction.

Cluster env (read ONLY by `src/server/integration-store.js`; the standing
census fails the names anywhere else): `VIBESPACE_INTEGRATIONS` = JSON
`[{id, key?, label?, values: {field: value}}]` (the helm Secret) and
`VIBESPACE_INTEGRATION_<ID>_<FIELD>` (single-field form; the JSON form wins
when both name a row). The `gmail` row reads neither: it delegates to
`VIBESPACE_GDRIVE_CLIENTS` through `MountManager.drivePresets()`.

## Background Work (2.342.0)
- Agent (vsst_ or jbt_ Bearer): POST /api/agent/jobs (create; vendor-vet + schedule floors; a body naming `envFrom` — itself or its `action.task` — ⇒ 400 `envfrom_retired: a job's secret comes from the vault: --vault NAME=vault://… — see vibespace-docs jobs`, lane job-secrets-retire), GET /api/agent/jobs (view-filtered list), GET /api/agent/jobs/:ref?wait&tail&answers (poll/long-poll, uniform not-found), POST /api/agent/jobs/:ref/{stop|start|rm|progress|ask|access} (control needs canControl; access needs owner + not user-locked; jbt_ tokens: progress/ask on SELF only).
- User (cookie): GET /api/jobs (full), GET /api/jobs/:id?tail, POST /api/jobs/:id/{stop|start|rm|answer|access} (access accepts lock:true/false), GET /api/jobs-escapes (read-only systemd/crontab listing). GET/POST /api/jobs-secrets are GONE (lane job-secrets-retire, 2.369.365: 404 — the plaintext data/job-secrets.json they kept is read by no one; a job's secret comes from the vault). verify-r6 J1: `answer` must carry the panel's `version` (a per-job, only-growing number — the ask route answers it) — missing ⇒ 400 `version-required`, another panel's ⇒ 409 `stale-panel {version}` (the one it waits on), nothing recorded.
- "Clear content…" (2026-09-28): a job snapshot carries `clearedAt` / `clearedBy`; with `?tail` on a cleared job whose last run STARTED at or before the clear, `logTail` is `''` and `logWithheld: true` (the run's words are withheld, said so — `vibespace-job logs` prints "(log withheld — …)", never "(no log)"); a run started after the clear serves its log as usual.
- WS broadcast: `jobs-updated {id, removed?}` to cookie clients only.
- MEMORY BOUND (2.369.360, lane jobs-memory-bound): create body (agent and user) accepts `memoryMb` — a size (`"8G"`, `"512M"`, `"1.5g"`, a number = MB) read exactly, ≥ 256 (below ⇒ 400 «memory: at least 256 MB …» for everyone), `0` / `"off"` = unbounded only from the cookie route (an agent's 0 ⇒ 400 «memory 0 (unbounded) is the owner's call …»); a scheduled task carries it in `action.task.memoryMb`. `POST /api/jobs/:id/memory {value}` (cookie; `inherit`/empty = back to the ladder) and `POST /api/agent/jobs/:ref/memory {value}` (owner conversation, canEdit) ⇒ `{memoryMb (effective), memoryAskedMb, memorySource, memoryCap}` from the next run (`unchanged: true`, no event, when the value is the stored one). Snapshots carry `memoryMb` (the ladder's EFFECTIVE answer now — capped by the instance's own memory.max less max(1 GiB, 25 %)), `memoryAskedMb` (before the cap), `memoryCap {limitMb, hubMb}` (null when nothing capped it), `memorySource` ∈ job|setting|cluster|default, `memoryBound {mb, by: cgroup|rlimit-data|none, why}` (the latest run's measured rung), `oomEnds`, `parkedFor` ('memory' when parked by the law); `GET /api/jobs` runs carry `memoryMb` + `memoryAskedMb` + `memoryCap` + `memoryBound`. Run causes gain `oom-bound` (its own bound) and `oom` (an OOM from outside / the instance's, judged at boot from `pressure.json`). The spec file gains `memory {mb, unit, rungs}`; the run's ctl dir gains `bound.json` (written once the rung is proven — `confirmed: true`) and (while the instance's anon is ≥ half its limit) `pressure.json {cgId, oomKillAtStart, oomGroupKillAtStart, jobAnonMb, anonMb, fileMb, usedMb, limitMb}`; a run the boot judged without an exit record carries `memoryJudge {why, jobAnonMb, anonMb, …}`; exit.json gains `memory {mb, by, why, unit?, confirmed, maxBytes, cause?, how?, peakMb?}`. Setting `jobs.memoryMb` (default 4096, read as stored); env `VIBESPACE_JOBS_MEMORY_MB` (the chart's `jobs.memoryMb`).
- VAULT VALUES (lane vault-job-road, 2.369.365 — design-vault-service-secret L1a): create body accepts `vault: ["NAME=vault://…", …]` (≤ 8; NAME `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,63}$`; a scheduled run's ride `action.task.vault`) — the grammar and the entry's family refused at create (400 `refused: <words> (the --vault value #n)` / `(--vault NAME)`: vault_bad_spec · vault_too_many · vault_bad_name · vault_dup_name · vault_name_refused · vault_bad_ref · entry_not_for_jobs · use_not_granted — never the text typed). The record keeps `vault: [{name, ref}]`; every snapshot carries `vault` (references only), `waitFor` (`vault` | `approval` while the new state `waiting`) and `waitWords`. At each spawn the value rides the wrapper's fd 3 (protocol 3) and the job's fd 4 + i; the env holds `NAME_FD` only. A job's own vault verbs stay `job_no_use`. Round 2: a create / start that ends `waiting` is answered as one — POST /api/agent/jobs `job.state: 'waiting'` + `job.waitWords`; POST /api/agent/jobs/:ref/start (and the owner's /api/jobs/:id/start) `{ok: true, state: 'waiting', waitFor, after: <the words>}`; a cron whose fires would wait adds `vaultWait: {waitFor, words}` to its create / start answer — and the CLI's first line is `waiting: … NOT started — <words>` (or `scheduled: … — but each fire's run WAITS`), exit 0, never «started». The start's `vault-use` line is written on the wrapper's ACK (`ok`), else `refused:<code>` (uncounted).
- UNKNOWN FIELD / FLAG (lane vault-job-road, L0's acceptance 2026-10-30 — «no silent failures»): POST /api/agent/jobs refuses a body field it does not copy — 400 `{code: 'unknown_field', fields}` naming it (the known set is DERIVED from the route's own field copy + the body `token` + `notifyOk`), so an older hub meeting a newer CLI says so instead of starting a job without its --vault values; `vibespace-job run / notify-cron` refuses an unknown flag by name with the flags it knows (exit 2, nothing sent) and a --vault that is no `NAME=vault://…` before anything is sent.
- TRIAGE (2026-09-14, design §13): every job snapshot carries `ack {acked, by: notified|agent-read|user-opened|null, at}` (server-computed over the FULL record) and `run.lastLine` (the last non-empty, secret-redacted log line of a terminal run, ≤200 cp). `POST /api/jobs/:id/seen` (cookie) stamps `user-opened` on a terminal one-shot and answers `{success, changed, ack}` (a no-op elsewhere); an owner-lineage `GET /api/agent/jobs/:ref` (poll/show/logs) stamps `agent-read` — a jbt_ self-read never does. A new run clears `ack`; a stash drained at injection stamps `notified` on every job it named (at or after that job's terminal instant).
- ARCHIVE (design §13 rule 5): terminal one-shots leave `data/jobs.json` for the append-only `data/jobs-archive.json` (newest 2000; a record keeps runs/notifyLog/ack and gains `archivedAt` + `archivedWhy` ∈ done | failed-acknowledged | missed-acknowledged | interrupted-acknowledged | unverified-acknowledged) on a sweep at boot and every 5 min on the engine's own tick (settings `jobs.archiveDoneAfterHours` default 24 / `jobs.archiveFailedAfterDays` default 7 since the ACK; 0 = never; never a service, a cron parent, a live pid, an open interaction, an unacknowledged failure, or a cron child whose schedule is still active). One `jobs-updated {archived:[ids]}` broadcast per sweep. `GET /api/jobs` carries `archivedCount`, `held` (the typed held-notification digest) and `ownerSession {conversationId, sessionId}` per row; `GET /api/jobs?archived=1` lists the archive (same snapshot shape + `archived:true`, `archivedAt`, `archivedWhy`); `GET /api/jobs/:id` reads through to the archive; `POST /api/jobs/:id/rm` on an archived id deletes it for good (record + log dir, broadcast `{id, removed:true, archived:true}`), any other act answers 400 by name. Agent: `GET /api/agent/jobs?archived=1` (view-filtered archive; `vibespace-job list --archived`), `GET /api/agent/jobs/:ref` reads through (`archived:true`, never a 404 for housekeeping), `POST …/:ref/rm` on an archived ref for control-holders. `rm` on an archived id answers 400 `archive write failed: <error>` and KEEPS the row when the archive cannot be rewritten — the removal is durable or it did not happen (2026-09-16).
- HELD NOTIFICATIONS ARE TYPED (2026-09-15, design §13 5b): every `data/job-notifications.json` stash entry carries `held {kind: spend-cap|rate-floor|not-reachable|off|wrapper-no-steer, why?, identity?, cap?, retryAfter?}` (an older entry with no `held` reads as not-reachable); `GET /api/jobs` and EVERY `jobs-updated` broadcast carry `held {total, byConversation: {cid: {count, kinds, reason}}}` (structure only — the client says the words through jobs-layout `heldText`); the delivery ladder's spend refusal `{ok:false, refused:'spend', why, retryAfter, identity:{key,name}, cap}` is what types it. `data/spend-budget.json` stamps are `{at, reason}` (a SPEND_REASONS member; a bare number from an older build reads as reason null / producer `unknown`), and a refusal / the 80 % notice names the window's top producers (`Spent by: job-notification ×9, auto-resume ×1.`).
- `POST /api/jobs/seen` `{ids: [..≤500]}` (2.369.121, the panel's "Mark all seen"): every id that is a terminal one-shot not yet acknowledged is marked `ack {by:'user-opened'}`; ONE save + ONE `jobs-updated {acked:[ids]}` broadcast; answers `{success, requested, acked, ids}`; unknown / running / archived ids are skipped silently. Session folds persist in user state `jobsPanelFolds` keyed by the session key (`s:<cid>` / `w:<sid>` / `manual`) beside the family keys.
- Owner auto-notify (2.344.0): create body accepts `notify: "on"|"off"` (absent = inherit group/global); create RESPONSE carries `notify: {enabled, source: job|group|global, mode: live-message|resume-inject|off, reason?, detail?}`; job snapshots carry `notify` (value or 'inherit') + `lastNotify {ts, lane: message|stash|off|suppressed, ok, to?, reason?}` (`channel` only in records older than 2.369.202 — the removed experimental rung). Task Group PATCH (`POST /api/tasks/:id`) accepts `jobNotify: true|false|null` (tri-state). 2.344.2: post-create override without recreation — agent `POST /api/agent/jobs/:ref/notify {value: on|off|inherit}` (owner-only canEdit, returns fresh preview; CLI `vibespace-job notify <id> on|off|inherit`); user side rides the access act (`POST /api/jobs/:id/access` accepts `notify`). 2.345.0: `POST /api/agent/jobs/:ref/{subscribe|unsubscribe}` (canView; jbt_ self-tokens excluded) — per-conversation subscription to a visible job's notifications (cap 10, cron parent covers children); snapshot +`subscribersCount`. 2.346.0: act `announce` {text} (jbt_ self-act OR canControl — puts text before the owner, view alone insufficient); create accepts `notifyOk` (scheduled successes notify); stash spill files under data/job-notifications-read/. 2.350.0: GET /api/agent/docs/:topic (index|jobs|task|status|ask|msg|pages|**browser**; vsst_/jbt_) = full per-tool manuals from docs/agent/*-manual.md; /api/agent/jobs-docs = jobs alias; user-todo items carry jobId (resolveByJob clears them on panel answer/expiry/rm). 2.347.0: subscribe accepts {filter: regex≤200} (per-subscriber, case-insensitive over notify text, fail-closed; re-subscribe updates); GET list accepts ?mine=1 / ?subscribed=1; list+detail responses carry `mine` + `mySubscription`; snapshots carry full creation params (cmd argv/cwd/envKeys, envFrom, restart, timeoutMs, untilOutput, notifyUser/notifyOk, catchUp, stopWithOwner, singleInstance). Settings keys: `agents.jobNotify` (bool, default true), `agents.vibespaceChannel` (bool, default false, experimental). Store: `data/job-notifications.json` (conversation-keyed offline stash, engine-owned atomic writes).

## Auto-continue after a usage limit (2.368.0; GENERIC over every harness 2026-09-08)

- WS client→server: `{type:'auto-resume', sessionId, enabled}` — the live per-session toggle (also persisted as session config so it rides the next resume). Server→client: `auto-resume` with `{enabled, explicit, globalDefault, armed, resetsAt, reason, resume, watch, lane, bucket}`; the same object rides `attached` as `autoResume`, and `attached.outputStyle` reports what the live session was spawned with.
- The four fields added 2026-09-08 are what the surfaces gate on, so none of them has to know a backend id: **`resume`** = this harness's resume-verb FORM (`'message'` claude | `'turn-start'` codex | `'prompt'` ACP | `null` = it cannot restart a turn, so it is never armed and the chip is not drawn); **`watch`** = armed but with NO timed continue promised (the reset is past the 26 h ceiling — only a reading saying the quota is back can continue it, and the chip must print no clock); **`lane`** / **`bucket`** = WHICH wall the wait is about (the harness's own `limitId` and bucket name), so a reading about a sibling limit lane or a different bucket can never be read as "the wall is gone".
- Client caps mirror: `capsOf(backend).autoResume = {signal, resume, supported}` — DERIVED from the descriptor (`deriveAutoResume` in src/backend-caps.js, written by the harness registry at load), mirrored key-for-key in `src/lib/agent-meta.js` and deep-compared by scripts/test-harness-contract.mjs.
- Settings: `claude.autoResumeOnLimit` (default for new sessions on EVERY limit-reporting harness, off — the key keeps its legacy prefix because renaming a persisted key is a migration) and `claude.outputStyle` (default style for new sessions).

## This instance's public address (2.367.0)

- `GET /api/instance-url` — `{frpConfigured, frpMissing, subDomainHost, authEnabled, published, desired, auto, url, sub, error, settingUrl, envUrl, effectiveUrl, source}` where `source` ∈ `frp` | `setting` | `env` | `frp-auto` | `null`.
- `POST /api/instance-url/publish {sub?}` — map the whole instance through the frp relay (refused when auth is off or the relay is unconfigured). `DELETE /api/instance-url/publish` — unmap; everything falls back to `agentd.publicUrl`, which was never written. WS broadcast: `instance-url` with the same status.

## The New Session form (lane new-session-form, .346)

**`create` gains `taskIds` + `tools`.** `taskIds: [T-…]` — every Task Group the form picked, IN SELECTION ORDER (src/new-session-spec.js `spawnGroupIds`: the id shape, de-duplicated, ≤ 16; an older page's single `taskId` is a list of one; the form sends both, `taskId = taskIds[0]`). The session holds `_initialGroupIds` beside `_initialGroupId` (= the first); every `groupsForSession` ask passes `initialGroupIds` (the pre-bind window, the browser ladder's Task-Group rung in selection order); the meta keeps `taskId` for old pages and adds `taskIds`; boot-restore reads it back; remote sessions the same. Its presence also tells the create that the form judged the browser profile by those groups: an explicit pick they do not admit (`mayAttachAtCreate`) starts without it (logged), never added to «Who can use it». `tools` — the 可用工具 pick, judged by each adapter with backend-caps `toolAllowlistArgs` against `capsOf(backend).toolAllowlist`: claude `{allow:[…], deny:[…]}` → `--allowedTools a,b --disallowedTools c` (a closed tool list; a ✗ Bash dropped), codex `{sandbox, approval}` → `--sandbox` / `--ask-for-approval` (terminal) or `CODEX_WEBUI_TOOLS_PICK` (chat); the judged pick is meta `toolPick` and rides the `created` reply as `toolPick` (the page latches it into the conversation's config key `tools`; resumeSession / inbox-reopen re-pass it).
- `GET /api/browser/admits?taskIds=T-a,T-b` → `{profiles:[{id, ok, via, taskId?, code?}]}` — the keeper's `admitsAtCreate`: `B.mayAttach` per named profile for a conversation that does not exist yet, through those groups (no key). Read-only; 503 without a keeper.
- **The create-time access row (no new route):** a ticked account in 「账号 · 以你的身份」 is written by the page, once the conversation's own id exists, as the account grain's whole list + ONE row `{principal:{kind:'agent', id:<conversation id>}, authority:'draft'}` through `PUT /api/channels/adapters/:id/access {access, base}` (the stamp it read; `grain-changed` ⇒ read again once; a conversation that already holds a row writes nothing).

## Per-session git worktree + claude's agent→user channel (owner rulings 9 and 8(c))

**`create` gains `worktree: true`** (claude only, gated on the `backend-caps` `worktree` row — never a backend id). The server PREFLIGHTS it before spawning, because `--worktree` makes the CLI exit 1 outside a git repo BEFORE the session exists and the window would die instantly with the reason buried in the buffer. Two refusals, both `scope:'action'` (they render in chat and leave live windows alone):
- `{type:'error', code:'worktree-unsupported', scope:'action', reqId}` — the harness has no such flag.
- `{type:'error', code:'worktree-not-a-git-repo', scope:'action', reqId, cwd, hostName?}` — the message names the cwd, the machine, and the CLI's own escape hatch (`git init`, or a `WorktreeCreate` hook in settings.json for another VCS).

The probe runs against a machine HANDLE (`hostId` is a parameter): locally a bounded `git -C <cwd> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`, remotely ONE `hosts._hostShell` round trip that answers both halves of the CLI's own `hook OR repo` gate. A probe that cannot ANSWER (timeout, unreachable) is not a refusal — the spawn proceeds and the CLI speaks for itself. The flag itself is emitted by the ADAPTER (`worktreeSpawnArgs`): new ⇒ yes, fork ⇒ yes, **plain resume ⇒ never** (the CLI re-enters its own recorded worktree; a second flag = a second tree). Remote spawns carry it with no transport special-casing at all — it is just another `spec.args` entry that `buildRemoteExec` quotes (`scripts/test-remote-shell.mjs` drift guard). **`--tmux` is never emitted on any path.**

**`active-sessions`, `attached` and `created` all carry `worktree` (boolean) and `worktreePath` (string|null)** — `worktree` is what THIS RUN is, `worktreePath` is the directory the CLI itself announced in its `system/init` frame. `created` carries them for the 2.368.4 reason: the creator window never receives an `attached`, and it is the window that has to draw the badge. **On the client they reach every surface through `LIVE_SESSION_FACTS` in src/lib/sidebar.js** — `_merge()` rebuilds the session row rather than spreading it, so a live fact that is not on that list never reaches the card, Session Properties or the fork; a new payload key must be added there (or to the renamed/derived set) or the drift guard in scripts/test-worktree-userchan-ui.mjs fails.

**`worktree-path` (server → client)**: `{type:'worktree-path', sessionId, worktree: boolean, worktreePath: string|null}` — the init-frame ARBITER, pushed on a CHANGE only (an unchanged re-attach frame is a no-op, so a reconnect storm cannot flap the badge). `worktree:false` is the CLI saying this run is NOT isolated (its worktree was gone — "The worktree binding has been cleared." — or a resume could not create one); the client drops the live badge and **does not** write that back over the user's saved pick.

**"Same cwd" is the QUESTION, not the verdict (2026-09-07 round 4).** The arbiter used to retire the fact whenever the announced cwd equalled the launch cwd — a NEGATIVE inference, and a plain RESUME of a worktree conversation satisfies it: a resume launches in the DISCOVERY cwd, and for such a conversation that IS the worktree (the CLI wrote its transcript from in there, so the JSONL's own `cwd` and the project-dir encoding both name it — measured: `claude --worktree` in `/tmp/vs-wtrepo-probe` produced `~/.claude/projects/-tmp-vs-wtrepo-probe--claude-worktrees-probe9`). So every resumed worktree session was reported as not isolated, permanently, because boot-restore reads `_worktree: !!meta.worktree`. The positive question — *is the announced directory a LINKED worktree?* — is answered by git itself (`rev-parse --git-dir --git-common-dir` differ inside one, agree in a plain checkout), asked at most ONCE per attach per directory, bounded and async, against the machine the session runs on (`hostId` is a PARAMETER: a local child process, or ONE `hosts._hostShell` round trip answering in `__VS_WT_YES__`/`__VS_WT_NO__` markers — never an exit code, because "not a repo" and "git is missing" are different answers). **A probe that cannot ANSWER touches NOTHING** (the ws-create preflight's tri-state rule) and says so in the log + a `worktree-probe-unknown` event. Known limit: a `WorktreeCreate` hook can isolate a run under another VCS, and such a directory also answers "not a git repo" — a resume that re-enters it in place retires the badge.

**`user-file-published` (server → client)**: `{type:'user-file-published', sessionId, toolCallId, files:[{path, name, link?, pageId?, size?, error?}]}` — the result of ONE `SendUserFile` tool call. `link` is a **RELATIVE** `/p/<id>` (the 2.366.1 URL law: the server never guesses an absolute URL; the browser joins it with its own origin). Pages are session-owned and **private by default**. Every failure (missing file, too large, unreadable, publishing unavailable) rides the same message as a per-file `error` and lands ON the card — a file the agent believes it delivered and the user never received is not allowed to be silent. LOCAL sessions only: a remote session's files are on another machine, so the card renders with no link rather than a guessed one.
